- 2026-08-30: **a comment in atablefor's red-team suite asserted the two halves of the wire were «allowed to differ» about a whole-valued float, and nothing anywhere backed the «allowed» (K-1029).** Now that Section 8.1 item 8 publishes the rule -- a query parameter declared `integer` takes an integer literal and nothing else, a body field declared `integer` takes any JSON number whose value is whole, and the difference is intentional -- both halves are pinned so neither can drift into the other: two decoder examples for the query half, including the reserved `limit` that reaches it by a different route, and a new `WholeValuedFloatBody` beat that asserts the pair on a booted origin and is the only beat in that suite whose claim is an ACCEPTANCE. Every `integer` declared across the fleet was audited against the ruling's own test -- can this field legitimately carry a fraction -- and none can, so nothing widened. Intent: a one-sided pin is how the accepted side of a published asymmetry disappears.

- 2026-08-30: **the per-assistant spending-cap tally was keyed on the currency STRING, so `"eur"` and `"EUR"` were two tallies for one currency and an assistant that alternated the spelling between chains collected its cap twice (K-1251).** This is the residue of the fix that made the tally currency-scoped in the first place: scoping a sum by a string the assistant itself signs is not scoping it by a currency, and both spellings are live in this repository. The engine now canonicalises `currency` — trimmed and lower-cased — where a signed mandate becomes a verified value, so the binding comparisons, the persisted rows, the payment call and the tally are keyed alike by construction, with the tally query folding its column as well for rows written before that; lower case because it is the spelling the shipped payment adapter's API documents. The currency DOMAIN is still deliberately open. Intent: a control an assistant can defeat by capitalisation is not a control.

- 2026-08-30: **a mandate `currency` was checked for presence and nothing else, so an empty string and a number were both accepted currencies (K-1250, K-1248).** Presence is not the constraint here: §11.2 compares the three mandates' currencies to EACH OTHER and never to a domain, so a chain denominated in `""` satisfies every binding rule, reaches the PSP as the currency of a real charge and becomes the key the spending-cap tally is scoped by — and mandates arrive as signed JWS, so the published schema was never in the path. The verifier now refuses a non-string and an empty or all-blank one at the same `403` the rest of the mandate-field class answers, while deliberately NOT closing the domain, which is a question no document has settled. In the same wave the operator-served `auth.md` stopped reciting a six-name OAuth error vocabulary where this engine emits eight. Intent: a guard should enforce what the specification actually says and be loud about the part it is choosing not to decide.

- 2026-08-28: **the count guard was one noun short of the class it had just gained, and its own header carried five hand-typed counts about itself (K-1241, K-1242).** `kiosk-reputation`'s RubyGems page says it ships two configurable example policies — true, but `policies` was not in the countable-noun vocabulary, and measurement showed the claim reached NEITHER the gated set NOR the printed broad residue, which needs the plural to sit immediately after the cardinal; the assumption that it was at least being counted somewhere was wrong. `policies` and `backends` are in the vocabulary now and the claim is held by a command that re-derives it from the shipped files, so the page keeps a true count instead of losing it. Separately, the header's description of its own corpus is deleted and printed by every run instead: a numeral typed into a Ruby comment is the exact class this guard reports rather than gates, so nothing could ever have caught it drifting, and it had drifted twice. Intent: a guard may not be the one thing in the repository that describes itself from memory.

- 2026-08-28: **the count guard now reads what a gem's RubyGems page says, which is the surface a false count was last found on (T-144, K-1217).** A gemspec's `summary` and `description` are the first and often only page a stranger reads about a gem, and they were in the ungated half; both spellings this repo uses are now censused, wrapped sentences joined first, with the extractor held to finding published text in every gemspec so a class that quietly stops reading cannot pass as coverage. The one count it found on that surface is elided rather than excused, a deployment-checklist entry that was excused by hand is now derived by command, and the header states plainly what the class cannot offer: a declaration marker would ship to the published page, so eliding or allow-listing are the only honest repairs there. Intent: extend a guard to the surface strangers read before extending it to the surface only maintainers read.

- 2026-08-28: **getgrocery's redteam header advertised eighteen attacks where the battery runs nineteen, and no run could disagree with it (K-1239, K-1183).** The missing one is `DeviceGrantRoleSelfSelection`, the shared claim-ceremony beat every demo runs and the only beat in that suite not local to it — omitted from a hand-typed enumeration whose totals are computed from the array beside it, so nothing went red and only a reader was misled; the identical omission of the identical beat was repaired in philslist's README days earlier. The enumeration is deleted rather than corrected, the way this same file already deleted its hand-copied profile map, and the run was re-driven bare to confirm what it actually attacks. The other five demo suites were checked for the same shape: four enumerate completely and two enumerate only the beats they call headline, with their group counts measured correct. Intent: a list that has to be re-typed to stay true will eventually not be.

- 2026-08-28: **hoteling carried two date guards that disagreed about what a date is, behind one refusal sentence, and its own code recorded the disagreement as a deferred decision (K-1234, K-1230).** `hotel_detail` kept a `Date.parse` because «what this verb accepts is already published behaviour», and once K-1230 narrowed the sibling guard to the spelling its refusal names, one demo answered the same «use YYYY-MM-DD» to two different sets. Documenting the looseness — the other option — is not available: measured, `Date.parse` is not a wider ISO family but a SCANNER that reads `09/01/2026` as 9 January, accepts `x2026-09-01x` and `2026-09-01'; --`, and completes `Tue`, `sep` and `1st` from TODAY's clock, so the accepted set depends on the day the call is made. What the verb actually publishes is `format: "date"` on both arguments, and measured against this demo's own validator that is the only spelling that reaches the handler at all — so the verb now calls the one guard, and two prose sites that taught the old split are corrected. Intent: one demo may not mean two things by one sentence, and «already published behaviour» is a claim to measure rather than inherit.

- 2026-08-28: **the shared-unit rule now holds thirteen of the seventeen `:per_demo` paths instead of one, and the four it does NOT hold are a judgement with a stated criterion rather than a backlog (T-136, T-145).** Re-measured at head, those paths share 110 unit names of which 71 have identical bodies once comments are normalised away, and the mass is the flow drivers' copied test harness rather than shared shipped code. The first question was whether two entries were simply mis-declared and should be compared WHOLE, and measurement says no: `pay_window.rb` normalises to 194 lines against 181 with 100 in common, `binding_flow.rb` to 69/125/119 across its three copies — the units agree and the beats do not, which is what `:per_demo` already says. 74 units over 180 copies are now declared, up from 4 over 10, including the DB-free spec harness three demos copied (where the discovery rule demanded three more units than the row had counted) and the shipped KYC trust anchors and intake cap two demos share. `redteam_suite.rb`, `isolation_flow.rb` and `schema_flow.rb` are deliberately left out — their shared names differ by harness GENERATION, one `get_json` having six distinct bodies over seven demos, so declaring them would freeze into the manifest the split T-119 exists to remove — and `cart_mandate.rb` cannot be reached at all, because what its copies share is a `scope` call rather than a `def`. Intent: «not compared» should mean somebody decided that, and the decision should say which units it was about.

- 2026-08-28: **`bin/check-demo-copies`'s shared-unit rule kept the FIRST definition of a name in a file and dropped every later one without saying so (T-136).** It was written against a module where each name occurs once, so the day a driver with two PSP stubs joined it three units would have counted as held while three definitions were compared to nothing — and a measurement taken with the old extractor read `initialize` and `call` as identical across three red-team suites where one copy defines each of them nine times. A unit is now every definition of that name in the file, concatenated in file order, which leaves the handle a bare name and every declaration already written valid. Intent: a lockstep rule that quietly compares less than it claims is worse than none, because it is read as coverage.

- 2026-08-28: **hoteling's stay-date guard accepted five spellings where its refusal named one, and two of them answered a question nobody asked (K-1230).** `Date.iso8601` is a family, not a format: measured, a basic `20260901`, a full datetime, an ISO week date and an ordinal date all parsed to the same day on the descriptor-less reserve path, where that sentence is the only thing telling an assistant what the operator takes — and the datetime form silently discarded a time an assistant may have meant as the check-in hour. The guard now takes the spelling it publishes, with the ISO parse kept behind it so a well-shaped non-day is still refused. Intent: on the path with no schema in front of it, the refusal sentence IS the contract, so the code has to mean it.

- 2026-08-28: **atablefor promised a set of acceptable values and then named none of it, on the first refusal a fresh operator install can produce (K-1231).** Both DB-derived refusals ended in «— currently » when the neighbourhood or upcoming-seating set was empty, which is an empty promise rather than an absence: an assistant parsing the sentence for values to retry with cannot tell «there is a set and you are not in it» from «there is no set». They now say «currently none». Intent: a refusal an assistant reads has to be as true when the answer is nothing as when it is a list.

- 2026-08-28: **a shipped page carried a claim about live litigation whose posture had reversed, under a date stamp that could not say so (K-1233, K-1170).** getgrocery's before/after cited the Amazon v. Perplexity injunction «as of 2026-06-22»; the Ninth Circuit vacated it on 2026-08-04 (No. 26-1444), holding that the user who tasks an agent is the one who accessed the site while leaving contract claims open — a ruling that argues FOR a sanctioned door rather than against it. The line now states the ruling and says why a stamp was not enough. Intent: a date stamp records when a claim was true, not that it stopped being true, and a pending case is exactly where the difference bites.

- 2026-08-28: **two demos whose every gate needed a booted origin can now prove their wire guards for the price of one `ruby` invocation, and the copy manifest can finally see the directory those proofs live in (T-137, T-116).** atablefor and hoteling shipped no `spec/` at all, so the only executable coverage of `party_size`, `whole_number`, the seating-time/seating-date/neighbourhood domain refusals, `booking_id`, the integer guard's magnitude arm, `stay_dates`, `past_stay`, the published check-in examples and `priceable_total` — every one of them a pure function — was the red-team battery, which needs a seeded database and a live Equihash toll. Each demo now runs the harness getgrocery's row wrote, at the head of its CI job: it asserts the TYPE and the exact SENTENCE of every refusal rather than that something was raised, distinguishes the shape answer from the range answer, freezes the property's clock instead of reading the runner's, names the one method it does NOT reach and MEASURES why, and ends by asserting ActiveRecord was never loaded. Both were watched fail by re-planting the coercion each guard replaced. Beside them, `spec/` sat outside the copy scanner's roots, so a file that now exists in three demos was declared by nothing and a fourth copy would have appeared in silence; the manifest names it, and the plant proves the entry is live. Intent: a proof about a pure function should cost a second, not a database — and a hand-copied file nobody declared is the drift this repo already pays a gate to prevent.

- 2026-08-28: **skooti stops retyping an Equihash size anywhere outside the file that maps a difficulty level to its numbers (T-123, K-1035, K-1036).** The schema driver's header quoted, in the past tense and inside guillemets, the very pair the line above it declared was not named in that comment; the red-team battery named the default pair in its own header and quoted both pairs again in the note explaining why it derives them. Every one of those moves with `KIOSK_POW_DIFFICULTY` and none was read from anything, so the demo's own discipline — say where a number comes from, or do not print it — was three lines away from being decoration. `app/services/pow_difficulty.rb` and the initializer's table are now the only places in this demo where a pair appears at all. Intent: a rule the tree states in prose is worth exactly what a grep can confirm.

- 2026-08-28: **a hand-typed count in shipped prose is now a red build rather than a discovery (T-143, K-1202, K-1205, K-1221).** `bin/check-prose-counts` runs the command a count declares beside itself — `<!-- count: N ¦ from: … -->`, the marker now carried by three sentences in the deploy runbook — and fails printing BOTH numbers when they disagree; beside that it censuses shipped prose newline-normalised, so a sentence that wraps over four lines is judged whole, and a count that is neither declared nor listed with a reason fails. Every judgement is made inside ONE SENTENCE and never inside a character window, which is K-1222: the sweep this one inherits used a ±320-character window and passed a defective sentence because an unrelated line fell inside it while that sentence's byte-identical twin failed, so the self-test plants a marker 146 characters away and requires the guard to stay red. 83 counts are gated behind 72 recorded entries, 38 of which carry a command and are re-measured rather than excused; the 465 in source comments are counted and printed on every run rather than gated, because an allow-file that large stops being read. Intent: a number in a sentence should be the output of something, or it should not be there.

- 2026-08-28: **a CI gate now asks whether every changelog answers each kind once, and the class it was built for had already come back (T-115, K-1015).** `bin/check-changelog` reads every tracked `CHANGELOG.md` and fails on a repeated subsection heading inside a release section, a repeated release section, a subsection above every release, a changelog with no declared role, or a gem directory missing the file its own gemspec publishes -- and it found `kiosk-redteam` carrying a second `Added` heading under one `[Unreleased]`, three days after the wave that merged exactly that shape, repaired here with every entry kept. It also settles where a changelog line goes (K-1159): the repository record at the root is the target for a change anywhere, including inside a demo, since a demo is a directory rather than a package; a gem's own file is required because it ships inside the gem; the two per-demo files are optional and are declared with the reason each exists. Intent: a convention that has to be remembered is one edit away from being gone, and this one already was.

- 2026-08-28: **atablefor's and getgrocery's `demo:pow` told the operator the flow solves four proofs and the run solves three, so the one number a viewer sizes a recording from was a hand-typed literal that had drifted (K-1221).** Both drivers now COUNT every solve where the solver is actually invoked — `equihash_register` pays a registration toll transparently for each identity the flow mints, which the old `proofs_solved` never saw — and both rake tasks assert the total against its two parts and print it beside the verdict instead of promising a number; getgrocery's README and both task descriptions lose the literal. Intent: a demo may not tell a viewer what a run will cost from a sentence nothing re-derives.

- 2026-08-28: **the «KB to verify vs ~1.3 GiB to solve» sentence K-1171 retired from the HN post was still shipping in nine `reference` surfaces, one of them a gem's RubyGems headline (K-1218, K-1217).** `kiosk-reputation`'s README, the engine's own `pow_gate` comment, `skooti.env.example`, `kiosk-pow-equihash`'s gemspec `summary`, atablefor's and getgrocery's READMEs and `demo:pow` banners, `deploy/CHECKLIST.md` and atablefor's `before-after.md` all quoted the GiB as what solving costs, full stop; each now says it is the reference solver's table and that a memory-optimised solver trades that table for time. Intent: the figure that carries the whole proof-of-work argument may not be stated as a floor on every implementation, on any surface.

- 2026-08-28: **five hand-typed counts that a fix wave had put back, and two it never reached, are elided again (K-1202, reopened).** An elision wave and a prose wave in the same session undid each other — «the full six-file `kiosk:install` set», three fresh «six»es in the philslist and stylish repo tours, tudu's «the ten verbs», kiosk-rls's «five migration verbs» and the deploy runbook's «six demos» — all measured exact and all now replaced by the handle a reader can re-run instead. Intent: nothing in the tree gates a number typed into prose, so the number should not be there.

- 2026-08-28: **the entry below claiming «the engine README stops teaching a registration path 0.4 deleted» outran its commit, and is corrected here rather than edited (K-1172, reopened).** That fix removed one sentence; `kiosk-server/README.md` went on naming «the initializer API» as a live second way into the registry 68 lines further down, and `kiosk-server/CHANGELOG.md` still promised that registrations made through it survive a reload — both now describe the one path the engine actually has. Intent: a changelog line is a claim like any other, so a false one is retired by a new line and never by rewriting the old.

- 2026-08-28: **the «Demo providers» table listed nine rows and the ninth was the test harness (K-1214).** `e2e` sat under a `Demo | Vertical | Deployment` header with its fixture inventory in the Vertical column, so anyone counting the fleet from that table got nine — and the sentence naming the two scales had to carve it out by hand to stay true. `e2e/` is now a paragraph below the table saying what it is and that it deploys nowhere, and the scale sentence no longer needs the exception. Intent: a table a reader counts from must contain only the thing it is counting.

- 2026-08-28: **K-1171 corrected the ~1.3 GiB claim at five sites and four more copies of it survived, one of them twelve lines above a site that same commit repaired — and three of the four were the deploy documents (K-1217).** `deploy/README.md` and the `atablefor`/`getgrocery` env examples are what a deployer reads before switching the heavy toll on, and they said the gibibyte is a property of the parameters rather than of the solver; atablefor's initializer said it twelve lines above where it says the opposite; and a fifth, found by this sweep, is the `pow_difficulty.rb` copied into all seven demos, which called the memory figure hardware-independent and stopped there. All five now carry the two clauses the repaired sites carry — the reference solver's sorted-nonce table, and a memory-optimised solver trading that table for time. Intent: the reader who has to decide whether to turn the toll on is precisely the one the overstated version misleads.

- 2026-08-28: **every demo README that describes `db/migrate/` called it the unmodified `kiosk:install` output, while all seven operator demos have carried a SEVENTH kiosk migration since 2026-08-27 (K-1205).** The generator emits six; `drop_kiosk_settlement_raw_jws` arrived afterwards as a NEW file — the only way a kiosk schema change may arrive, since a shipped migration is never edited — and the fleet took it at one timestamp, so an adopter diffing the directory against a fresh generator run met an undocumented extra file with nothing saying whether it was drift or intent. philslist's install-policy paragraph now states the rule, and the three repo-tour rows that describe the directory name the set. Intent: it is intent, and it costs one sentence to say.

- 2026-08-28: **the top-level README used ONE `Status` header for two incomparable scales, and the wave that removed the last shared token left nothing saying so (K-1204, K-1181).** The gem tables score API stability (`alpha` = pre-v1.0, what each gem's own README says about itself) and the demo table scores deployment (`active` = CI-gated and live at its own subdomain); with no value in common any more, a reader takes the demo column as the same axis and concludes the demos are more mature than the engine they run on. The columns are now named apart and one sentence above them says what each measures. Intent: a status column is the only thing many readers will read about a component, so it must not invert the maturity it reports.

- 2026-08-28: **`bin/check-publication-paths` compared the vendored section index BY KEYS ONLY, so the index every `§N.M` citation in this repository is resolved against went stale in its recorded source digest with the guard green throughout (K-1209).** The index already STORED a sha256 of the specification revision it was derived from and the comparison declined to read it, so a spec wave that adds and removes no heading changes the source document and nothing sees it — measured at head, the recorded digest named a revision that no longer exists while all 124 keys still matched exactly. The re-derivation now compares the digest as well as the keys and names `--record-sections` as the repair, `--self-test` carries a fixture that stales the digest and nothing else, and the index is re-recorded. Intent: a gate that answers a narrower question than its reader believes it asked is how a stale artefact certifies itself.

- 2026-08-28: **the seven operator demos and the kiosk-server default `skill_url` re-pin to `skill-v0.4.10.md` (URL and sha256 moved together, ADR-0012)** — the immutable cut that carries into the published skill the same correction this repo made at five sites a commit earlier: the heavy toll's ~1.3&nbsp;GiB is the reference numpy solver's sorted-nonce table, not a floor the parameters impose on every implementation (K-1211, K-1171); the wire is unchanged from 0.4.9. Intent: what each provider's discovery document advertises must be the skill cut that matches the wire it serves, and an assistant must not read this repo and the published skill saying different things about the number it budgets from.

- 2026-08-28: **the equihash gem said its ~1.3 GiB was a property of `(n, k)` in five places, and the same gem's own README explains two paragraphs of ASIC history that this is false (K-1171).** The peak is the reference numpy solver's sorted-nonce table, not a floor the parameters impose on every implementation — a memory-optimised solver trades the table for time, which is exactly how Equihash 200/9's real footprint fell to ~144 MB and made it ASIC-able. K-1070 de-portabilised the seconds and left the memory unqualified; the README, the gemspec, both `equihash.rb` comments and atablefor's initializer now name the solver the way they already name the machine. Intent: the memory asymmetry is the load-bearing claim of the whole PoW argument, so it must not be the one stated more strongly than it holds.

- 2026-08-28: **the hard-coded counts in shipped prose are gone rather than corrected, and two of them had already drifted (K-1202, K-1198).** `kiosk-server`'s README advertised «500+ passing specs» where the suite is 1506 examples and `kiosk-pow-cuckoo`'s «35+» where it is 44 — both understatements, both invisible to every gate, because no gate reads a numeral in prose; the ten that were still exact were re-measured and elided too, along with the `0.3.0` gem path in an illustrated `solver_path` result, since a number a future edit can falsify is only as true as the last person who remembered it. Intent: the only repair to an unguarded count that a later change cannot undo is not writing one.

- 2026-08-28: **the four-of-eight `before-after.md` asymmetry is now stated where a reader can see it, and the largest of the four is reachable from its own gem (K-1197).** Which demos carry the long-form contrast was declared only in `bin/check-demo-copies` and the CI workflow, so a reader met four demos linking a sibling document and four that did not with nothing anywhere saying that is intended — and getgrocery's own 687-line file had no inbound link at all. The top-level README now says which four, and why the long form is not free; getgrocery's README links its own. Intent: an intended asymmetry that only a build script knows about reads to everyone else as neglect.

- 2026-08-28: **`kiosk-redteam` gets the README its RubyGems page has been missing since it started shipping one (K-1196).** The gem's landing page was three lines — no install line, no example, no licence statement and no mention of the harness it actually ships — while its gemspec promises a driver for hostile HTTP scenarios against any Kiosk provider; it now has the shape its siblings use, names every scenario class with what it asserts, and states the three-state blocked/breach/skip contract including why a 5xx and a bare 402 are never passes. Both fenced examples were EXECUTED against a stubbed origin before publishing. Intent: the front door of a security gem is the one page a stranger reads before trusting it.

- 2026-08-28: **the demo table stops reading as if stylish takes money, and stops calling it alpha alone (K-1181).** FOUR demos configure no `payment_provider` and only three were labelled, while stylish's own initializer says it takes payment in the chair and its live discovery document advertises `[schema, queries, actions]` — no `pay` — exactly like the three that were labelled; it was also the lone `alpha` in a table of `active` siblings while serving 200 with 7 of its 8 demo tasks CI-gated, the same posture as philslist. Intent: a one-line status column is the only thing many readers will read about a demo, so it has to survive being read alone.

- 2026-08-28: **skooti's published contrast stops listing a file that does not ship and stops miscounting its own gem list (K-1189, K-1190).** The «what ships here» sentence named `SkootiDemo.xcodeproj` — a file `git ls-files` has never carried and `appclip/.gitignore` excludes, because it is GENERATED from `project.yml` by `make project` — and the adoption recipe said «those five» of a clause naming four gems. The sentence now names the sources git actually carries and says where the project comes from. Intent: a paragraph whose whole subject is what ships must be checkable against the file list.

- 2026-08-28: **philslist's README stops undercounting its own adversarial battery, and the beat it omitted was the security-relevant one (K-1183).** It promised «all fifteen the suite runs» and enumerated fifteen while the suite runs SIXTEEN and prints `all 16 scenarios BLOCKED` on every run; the missing beat is the shared K-072 claim-ceremony role-self-selection scenario, the one attack in that file not hand-rolled. It is now enumerated and the count is gone rather than corrected, so the next scenario added cannot make the sentence false again. Intent: a document that contradicts the run it describes teaches a reader to stop reading the run.

- 2026-08-28: **two shipped READMEs stop teaching two different names for the same query/action distinction (K-1182).** `kiosk-server` documents the declaration macro as `kind :action` while `kiosk-reputation`'s `Policy` base — the class an operator subclasses — documents the argument it receives as `:run`, and both were right about their own surface while neither said the two are the same verb; the mapping is now stated in both, on the code (`VerbController#create` serves `:run`, the pay endpoint arrives as `:pay`), so an operator branching a challenge policy on `:action` is told there why it would never fire. Intent: a name an adopter branches on must mean one thing across the documents that teach it.

- 2026-08-28: **the engine README stops teaching a registration path 0.4 deleted, through a link to a heading that does not exist (K-1172).** One paragraph of `kiosk-server`'s front door told an adopter that handlers «put in the registry the other way in (the initializer API)» need no `c.handlers` entry — `Kiosk::Server::Queries.register` was removed in 0.4 (T-081), the anchor resolved to nothing, and the section 116 lines below already says there is no second way; the sentence is gone and a scan of every tracked Markdown file now finds no unresolved same-document anchor anywhere in the repo. Intent: the first thing an adopter reads must not send them to an API that raises `NoMethodError`.

- 2026-08-28: **THE RED TEAM'S LEAK ORACLE WAS A FUNCTION OF ATTACKER-SUPPLIED INPUT, ACROSS ALL SEVEN BATTERIES (T-121).** Every hostile-input beat asserts that a refusal carries none of the runtime's own vocabulary by substring-searching the serialized body for `PG::`, `NoMethodError`, `22P02` and their siblings — and every demo answers a bad argument by NAMING the value it got, which is what makes those refusals useful to an assistant and is deliberately kept. So the bytes being scanned were partly the probe's own, and a probe whose value spelled a needle would have been reported as a BREACH on its own echo, under runners that tell the reader «A BREACH = a real hole in this demo — fix the app, not the scenario». Nothing was live — no probe set carried a needle, re-measured before the fix — and the failure mode was always a FALSE BREACH rather than a hidden one. The eleven scan sites now go through one shared `Kiosk::Redteam::LeakScan` that is told what each probe sent and discounts a needle only where those exact bytes are the probe's own, and two of the batteries carry a negative control on the ORACLE — a value spelling three leak strings, sent at the one argument per demo whose valid set no JSON Schema can express, with a vacuity guard that fails if the refusal stops echoing. Watched fail on atablefor, run and restored: drop the declaration from that one call and the probe reports `LEAK PG::` and the battery exits 1. Intent: a security check must decide what it can actually decide, and the answer must not be the attacker's to choose.

- 2026-08-28: **`demo:pow` can now be run at the parameters the tree actually ships, and says which of the two it ran at (T-110).** It is the only end-to-end exercise of the proof-of-work plane, and both copies spawned their server with `KIOSK_POW_DEMO=1` and nothing else — so the ambient `KIOSK_POW_DIFFICULTY` was DROPPED and every run was the toy `low` (n=96 k=5), while the shipped kiosk-pow-equihash default and the hosted atablefor charge n=168 k=7. Nothing anywhere demonstrated end to end that an assistant can pay the toll a real operator charges. The level is now forwarded to the spawned server and the pair printed at boot and beside the verdict, and — because a banner is a claim rather than evidence — the flow drivers report the `params` the server actually served and the task ASSERTS them against the level asked for, so an opt-in that silently fell back to `low` goes red instead of being narrated as `high`. The default is unchanged, so CI and a bare run cost exactly what they did; `KIOSK_POW_DIFFICULTY=high` was MEASURED end to end on both copies (atablefor 29.3 s / 1.40 GiB peak RSS, getgrocery 30.3 s / 1.39 GiB, both exit 0). The demos' READMEs, `before-after.md`, both rake descriptions and the CI comments that asserted the old forwarding behaviour now say which parameters the default is and how to reach the other. Intent: a demo that stands in for a claim must be runnable at the configuration the claim is about, and must never leave a viewer guessing which one they watched.

- 2026-08-28: **`bin/check-publication-paths`' identifier exemption was wider than the reason it was granted for, so it was narrowed to that reason (K-1158).** The rule is that a section marker directly behind a decision-record or ledger id names a part of THAT document and must not be resolved against the published protocol; but an id can also be used as an ADJECTIVE — «the pre-K-851 §11.6 read that as…» — where the section cited is genuinely ours and the id is only a date, and the exemption was covering that too. It now declines when the id is glued into a longer word, and, matching the clause its neighbour already carried, when the citation is attributed to our own specification in the same breath. Nothing was hidden: the one live site cites a section that resolves, so the verdict was right for the wrong reason — which is how an allow-list becomes a switch-off. The entry now suppresses nothing in either repository and is held live by two new self-test fixtures that were green under the old rule and are red under this one. Intent: an exemption must do only the work its stated reason describes, or the per-entry suppression counts stop meaning anything.

- 2026-08-28: **`bin/check-demo-copies` could not see a method shared by two demos, because the drift it exists to catch was happening one level BELOW its granularity (T-120).** The lockstep rule's handle is a relative PATH, so `whole_number/1` — byte-identical in atablefor and getgrocery — and `missing/1` — byte-identical in three demos — sat inside a file the manifest calls `:per_demo`, which its own header defines as «not compared», and a one-sided edit to either was exactly as undetectable as the stale comment and the half-propagated constant rename that made the script exist. The file stays `:per_demo`, because the guards genuinely ARE each demo's argument vocabulary; what is declared and compared now is the UNIT, in the shape the flow-runner rule already uses for a fragment inside another uncompared file, with `:code` semantics so each copy keeps its own prose. Promotion into a shared gem was considered and rejected for a four-line predicate: it buys a versioned public API and a release for every future word of a refusal message, and this script's own header already says such a promotion is a decision it must not pre-empt. The half that makes it a check rather than a list is DISCOVERY — a unit name appearing in two or more copies of a declared path fails the build until someone declares it and says why. Intent: «not compared» was being read as «nothing in here needs comparing», and shared code had been accumulating under it.

- 2026-08-28: **hoteling was the last demo whose `WireArguments` could hand an unbounded integer to a 4-byte column, and it was safe only by a coincidence of its call sites (T-125).** The helper took no ceiling at all, unlike getgrocery's and atablefor's siblings: `min_stars` reaches the RAISING shape (`stars.gteq(...)` casts, and `ActiveModel::RangeError` was a 500) and was covered only by its descriptor's `maximum: 5`, while `max_price_cents` had no declared maximum at all and survived only because the cheapest-rate expression it compares against is a correlated subquery carrying no int4 type — one ordinary denormalisation away from the same 500. `integer` now takes an optional `max:`, both filters pass the column's own width, `max_price_cents` declares it in `input_schema` too so the two layers agree, and the red-team battery probes one past int4 on both. No value that used to work is refused except one that cannot be represented; `limit` deliberately takes no ceiling, because it reaches no column and its published description says every integer is clamped rather than refused. Intent: a guard whose safety rests on a bound written somewhere else is not a second layer, and this one has now been the demo one over's bug twice.

- 2026-08-28: **a CI guard now asks at the introducing commit whether a shipped line points at material that does not ship (T-133).** `bin/check-publication-paths` reads every tracked file and fails on a private-workspace path, a private file named where a reader cannot resolve it, a citation of a superseded internal draft, a developer-machine absolute path, or a `§N` citation that resolves to nothing in the published protocol specification — with the exemptions (append-only changelog history, external-standard citations including the wrapped ones, identifier-internal markers, frozen skill cuts) each carrying an id and its reason, and a self-test that proves it red on a plant of every pattern before its green run is believed. The same file, byte for byte, is the kiosk.tech repo's guard. Intent: this leak class has only ever been caught by a periodic sweep, weeks after the commit that shipped it.

- 2026-08-27: **the seven operator demos and the kiosk-server default `skill_url` re-pin to `skill-v0.4.9.md` (URL and sha256 moved together, ADR-0012)** -- the immutable cut that qualifies the heavy toll's ~10&nbsp;s cost figure to the machine it was measured on (one M-series laptop core, reference numpy solver) and states the ~1.3&nbsp;GiB as a property of the parameters not the host, matching the landing and spec (K-1052, T-130); the wire is unchanged from 0.4.8. Intent: what each provider's discovery document advertises must be the skill cut that matches the wire it serves.

- 2026-08-27: **the citation scrub above missed twenty-six of its own class, because it counted the spelling it was looking for (K-1154).** Every repaired site typed the word «design» before the section number; twenty-six others in kiosk-server's engine and specs, kiosk-redteam's client and four demo flow drivers cited the SAME private document as a bare `§3.x`, which the scrub deliberately does not match because a bare `§` is how this repo cites the PUBLIC spec — and all three of those numbers resolve publicly to something else entirely (bearer auth, RS256, endpoint-path derivation), so the pointer was not merely dangling but wrong. They now take the mapping the first wave established (§8.1/§8.3 for verb names, §3.7.x for caching, the request-fingerprint construction stated inline with §10/§15.2 for the binding requirement). The line above keeps its wording; this line is its correction. Intent: a class is not drained by repairing the instances that match the pattern you searched for.

- 2026-08-27: **forty-seven comments in this repo cited an internal specification that ships nowhere, so this repo now says what it means (K-1154).** Sections of two umbrella-private specs stood in the code as the authority for rules a reader was expected to look up — one holding the 0.4 wire's caching, verb-name and request-fingerprint rules, the other the KYC broker's trust model — and the same bare section number meant a different document depending on which directory the citing file sat in. The wire rules ARE published, so those cites now name the normative spec's own sections (`spec/protocol.md` §3.7 caching, §8.1/§8.3 verb names); the broker is a demo the spec does not describe, so those sites state the requirement inline rather than invent a public section for it. No parenthetical was merely deleted: several carried the only recorded reason a security control exists, and inline `K-`/`ADR-`/`T-` ids stay beside them. Intent: a pointer beside a rule is a promise that the authority can be reached, and a public repo may not make one it cannot keep.

- 2026-08-27: **nothing could answer «is the fleet still serving the skill cut we ship?» except by hand, so `bin/check-live-skill-pin` answers it (K-1075).** Each operator's `/.well-known/kiosk.json` pins one immutable `skill-vX.Y.Z.md` and its sha256, that pin moves only when a deploy carries a new commit to the box, and between a cut and the next deploy the drift is SILENT by construction — the served pin is internally consistent, so the dual-check an assistant runs passes while it follows instructions three cuts old. The script derives the seven origins from `deploy/README.md` rather than a list of its own (a demo is a Kiosk operator iff it configures the engine, which is what excludes the KYC broker, and a demo the table maps no host to is an error rather than a silently dropped row), takes the expectation from each demo's own pin cross-checked against the newest cut on disk, and reads ONLY the discovery document — which answers 200 on origins whose human pages are 500. It is deliberately NOT a CI job: it compares the LIVE fleet against the WORKING TREE, so every skill cut makes it correctly red until someone deploys, and a gate that is red on a clean tree gets switched off. Intent: the gap between what is shipped and what is deployed should be a question anyone can ask in two seconds, not something a sweep stumbles on.

- 2026-08-27: **«six days» was a duration nobody measured and a past tense that has not arrived, in three shipped prose sites about K-1074 (K-1151).** The append landed `3a834cc0` (2026-08-23) and was corrected `86c89923` (2026-08-27) — four days IN THE TREE — while how long the boxes have served those 500s is not knowable from this side, and the outage is not over: a correction reaches a running database only on a redeploy, and the redeploy has not happened. `ci.yml`'s K-1082 header, `bin/check-migration-replay`'s own header and `CLAUDE.md`'s never-edit-a-shipped-migration rule now state the measurable half and decline the rest; the dated entry below keeps its wording, this line is its correction. Intent: a cost cited as the reason for a rule has to be a cost someone measured, or the next reader is right to discount the rule.

- 2026-08-27: **`bin/check-demo-derivations` said the promise rule fired «beside ONE block of twenty-four», a figure wrong in its total and backwards in its set relation (K-1110).** The corpus declares 25 blocks and the script checks 24 of them, and the block the rule fired beside is the `derived: none` one — precisely the block that is NOT among the twenty-four — so «one of twenty-four» inverted the argument the sentence was making. Both comment sites now say it that way and point at `--list` and the success line for the split instead of carrying a number; the K-1106 entry below keeps its wording, this line is its correction. Intent: a count in shipped prose is a claim, and one carried over from a ledger summary is a claim nobody measured.

- 2026-08-27: **the watched-fail recipe recorded beside `bin/check-migration-replay` in `ci.yml` had gone dead, so anyone following it proved the gate VACUOUS rather than live (K-1150).** Re-appending `add_column :users, :display_name` to the shipped tudu migration now exits 0, because K-1074's repair migration supplies that column on every vintage; the block now gives the mutation that still fires (delete the repair, then append the column to the already-recorded devise migration → exit 1 on four vintages), keeps the second direction re-measured, and stops quoting a replay count that every new schema vintage invalidates. Intent: a recipe that no longer fails is indistinguishable, from the reader's chair, from a gate that has stopped working.

- 2026-08-27: **`spec/conformance-drift-baseline.tsv` is generated and nothing checked it still was — so `bin/check-conformance-drift --check` now does, and CI runs it.** Its sibling `spec/conformance-anchors.tsv` has had a staleness check since K-1007; the baseline had none, and the very first one shipped a header its own committed generator had stopped emitting, so a hand-edited fingerprint or row would have read clean forever. The manifest's rule could not be copied — "equal to what the generator would write at head" would drag the baseline forward on every commit and set the sweep interval to zero — so `--check` asks about form and provenance instead: row shape and order, a header byte-equal to what the generator re-renders from the file's own values, and every fingerprint re-measuring AT THE COMMIT THE FILE NAMES, never at head. `--reheader` repairs a drifted header without touching a fingerprint, so tightening the prose can never cost the interval. The drift REPORT is still not a gate and no job runs it. Intent: a generated file that nothing can tell has stopped being generated is a document, not a measurement.

- 2026-08-27: **the conformance matrix could say its citations still RESOLVE and nothing could say the code under them had MOVED, so a 537-anchor work-list was walked by sampling (K-1123).** `bin/check-conformance-anchors` fails on a renamed, deleted or duplicated label — a check on the citation — while a cited example can be rewritten line by line, keep its label and pass forever; the last sweep hand-verified about ten rows out of ~185, chosen by section recency, because nothing derived which rows deserved it. `bin/check-conformance-drift` fingerprints the ANCHORED REGION of each anchor — the line the label sits on plus the block it opens, not the file's blob sha, which would flag all 33 anchors in `well_known_spec.rb` for one line moved elsewhere in it — records them in `spec/conformance-drift-baseline.tsv`, and afterwards lists what moved, optionally naming the matrix rows. It is NOT a gate and no workflow runs it: drift is the normal state between sweeps, and a CI job on it could only be silenced by re-recording the baseline in the same commit, which sets the interval to zero and destroys the signal. Intent: the JUDGEMENT of whether a test still asserts its statement is a sweep's and no script's, but the SELECTION of which ones to re-judge is mechanical, and leaving it unmechanised is how a work-list gets sampled.

- 2026-08-27: **the seven operator demos and the kiosk-server default `skill_url` re-pin to `skill-v0.4.8.md` (URL and sha256 moved together, ADR-0012)** -- the immutable cut in which the skill stops telling an assistant that re-paying a settled order is rejected, which this engine does not do: `settled_replay` answers only a chain that is byte-identical to one already settled, and a fresh chain's fresh mandate `id`s collide with nothing and simply settle again. Intent: what each provider's discovery document advertises must be the skill that matches the wire it serves.

- 2026-08-27: **two more sites of K-1112's false sentence survived in shipped guard prose (K-1131).** `bin/check-demo-copies` prints «`POST /kiosk/query` and `POST /kiosk/run` have no route» while telling a demo author that their own string about the wire is wrong — the one moment a reader has most reason to believe it. No DEDICATED route is drawn for either name; both fall through to the per-verb pair and answer the ordinary `404 not_found` problem document, hint and all. The guard's ASSERTION was correct and is untouched; only the sentence beside it moved. Intent: a guard that mis-states the wire while correcting someone about the wire teaches the wrong fact with maximum authority.

- 2026-08-27: **`Kiosk::Server::DeviceVerification.approve` defaulted `role:` to nil while the module invites hosts to build their own consent page on it (K-1127).** The engine's own controller has always passed the approving human's role, but a provider that took up that invitation and did not know about the keyword got role-less bindings with nothing to tell it — and `specification.html` now says normatively that the role a binding carries is the approving human's. The keyword is required; `nil` is still a legal answer and still binds at `registration_role`/absent, so what is gone is not the role-less binding but the SILENT one. Intent: on the one call where a human's consent is turned into a privilege, the privilege must be stated rather than defaulted.

- 2026-08-27: **the coverage that was supposed to catch the claim-ceremony escalation existed in ONE demo, and the shared beat that ran everywhere could not fail (K-1128, K-1129).** `kiosk-redteam`'s `PrivilegeSelfSelection` probes `/auth/register` — where the role has always been server-pinned — with an invented role the vulnerable code refused anyway, so it printed BLOCKED over K-072 for nineteen days; the four beats that DO test the binding ceremony were written into `kiosk-demo-stylish`'s own suite, leaving six demos with no binding-role assertion and a safety that rested on each declaring a single role, which is the very mitigation that had already expired. The assertion is now a framework scenario every demo runs — the four demos that hand-roll their battery pull in the gem for it — and it names a role the origin genuinely DECLARES, taken from the profile AND from the wire so a stale list cannot empty it. The e2e harness, which walked the grant and asserted nothing about the role, now pins the refusal on both spellings and both transports and pins the minted token's role to the approver's. Intent: a coverage claim that rests on a condition nobody re-measures is how the last one hid.

- 2026-08-27: **the wave that fixed the claim ceremony's role escalation cited K-1109 throughout, and K-1109 is an unrelated ledger row about elision markers in two before/after documents (K-1130).** The blocker it actually closed is **K-072**. All 27 in-tree citations now read K-072 — 24 in code, specs, views and the stylish README corrected outright, and the three that sit inside published changelog entries corrected in place with a note saying what they used to say, since the entries themselves are append-only. **Four commit subjects cannot be corrected at all** — `7efb2127`, `79933216` and `c3ca9d4b` here, `c40add1` in `kiosk.tech` — because published history is never rewritten; this entry is the pointer for anyone who follows a K-1109 citation out of a `git log`. Intent: the constitution requires an inline citation to name a row so a reader can find the reasoning, and one that resolves to the WRONG row is worse than none — it tells that reader a security escalation was a documentation nit.

- 2026-08-27: **the K-1070 entry below counts the skill cuts that pin the byte-locked `solve.py` as «twelve», and twelve is none of the readings of the tree (K-1116).** Measured over the tracked `skill-v*.md` set: **ten cuts pin the content-addressed `kiosk.tech/pow/solve-5810e16a17b1.py` URL and its SHA-256** (`skill-v0.3.10` through `skill-v0.4.7`), **eleven files counting the `skill.md` alias**; fourteen cuts carry the SHA-256 alone, because `0.3.6`–`0.3.9` pin the same bytes at a `raw.githubusercontent.com` URL instead. Twelve was true while `0.4.5` was the newest cut and stopped being true when `0.4.6` and `0.4.7` shipped. The number that matters for «do not edit these three files» is the URL-and-hash one — ten cuts plus the alias — and it is stated with its reading here rather than by amending the entry that has it wrong, because a published changelog is append-only. Intent: a count an editor is meant to be deterred by has to name what it counted.

- 2026-08-27: **four places in `kiosk-server` told a reader that `POST <endpoint>/query` and `POST <endpoint>/run` have no route, and one of them is the gem's shipped README (K-1112).** The 0.4 cutover deleted the two DEDICATED routes, but both names still match the constrained per-verb pair the engine draws last, so what a 0.3 caller actually meets is the ordinary `404 not_found` problem document — hint and all — that any unregistered verb name gets, not «a routing 404 with no Kiosk body» and not «no hint payload». The engine, `VerbController`, `WireController` and the README now say that, which is what the gem's own routing specs have asserted all along. Intent: the deletion is still a hard cut and the sentence describing it has to survive being executed — this one had already been quoted as an oracle by the ledger (K-805) for a routing fact it never established.

- 2026-08-27: **the account-binding claim ceremony took the assistant's role from the assistant, and the human approving it was never shown one (K-072 — this entry cited K-1109 when written, which is an unrelated row; corrected in place 2026-08-27, K-1130).** `POST /oauth/device_authorization` is UNAUTHENTICATED, and it read `role`/`scope` off that request, filtered it by membership of `config.roles` alone, and baked it into the minted JWT — so on an origin declaring more than one role a stranger asked for the privileged one and a plain customer's approval granted it, at first bind and again on every rebind, while the engine's own consent page showed a key fingerprint and a timestamp and no role at all. The parameter is now REFUSED rather than ignored, and the role is sourced where ADR-0011 puts it — the approving human's own `Identity#role`, captured at the verify page exactly as the link direction captures it at mint — which also gives the claim direction the roles-from-IdP inheritance it never had (an owner's claim used to bind at `registration_role`). The page now names the access it hands over. Intent: a ceremony whose whole product is a human's consent must not mint a privilege that human neither holds nor was shown.

- 2026-08-27: **the four published transcripts promised completeness that nothing could back, and the rule against exactly that fired beside none of them (K-1106).** `bin/check-demo-derivations` has refused «unedited» / «verbatim» / «first line to last» beside an unchecked block since K-1051, but the check sat inside the `derived: none` branch, which K-1099 had reduced to a single illustrative swap — so it guarded ONE block of twenty-four and none of the four quoting a recorded run, which is precisely where K-1062 found «verbatim … nothing else touched» false and hoteling's block missing an entire RUN. It now covers `derived: transcript` too, on the argument that a subset test can never reach a completeness claim; the window it scans became the whole intro, because getgrocery's promise sat two paragraphs above its declaration and scored zero; and the four intros were rewritten to state what the mechanism holds and hand completeness back to `abridged:`, which is a human's signature and now says so out loud. Intent: a sentence a reader trusts because it sounds mechanical should be mechanical, or should not be published.

- 2026-08-27: **the paragraph that enumerates a cut and the field that declares it are now held together by name (K-1107).** Nothing tied an `abridged:` declaration to the prose three lines above it, and in two of K-1103's five instances the PARAGRAPH WAS RIGHT WHILE THE FIELD WAS WRONG — the worse direction, because the field is the half a guard reads. Where the paragraph enumerates the cut in numbered sentences, every backticked bare identifier in those sentences must now appear in the field; run against the pre-K-1103 tree it catches that instance mechanically, and at head it caught one live vagueness, hoteling naming «one verb's guards» where its own prose names `availability`. Scoping to the numbered sentences rather than the whole paragraph is what keeps it honest, since three of the five paragraphs end in a KEPT clause a naive reading would take for a cut list. Intent: the readable half and the checkable half describe one cut, so let one hold the other.

- 2026-08-27: **the guard's success line said more than the guard proved, and its header said less than its narrowing cost (K-1108).** «every line held to a literal its declared producer prints» is the exact sentence that printed while hoteling's published `  OK  http_pay == 200` was mutated to `== 500 (charged nothing, refunded twice)` and the script exited 0 — every caveat lived in a header no CI log carries. A green run now states its own scope in two labelled clauses, HELD and NOT HELD, naming the token swap, the unread JSON values, line order, single-run provenance and completeness, and pointing at the full list with a count derived from the file rather than remembered. The mirror omission is fixed in the same pass: K-1105's tighter frame rule can redden an honest COMPUTED number, and the header now says so. Intent: a gate whose success message over-claims is read by more people than its source, and is the reason a green run stops meaning anything.

- 2026-08-27: **five `abridged:` declarations did not let a reader work out what was cut, which is the field's one job (K-1103).** skooti's transcript named what it KEPT and left the `/etc/hosts` hint above it and the assertions footer below it named nowhere; hoteling's two controller snippets said «each verb's» and «both verbs'» where the files ship five queries and three actions and the blocks quote three and two — the prose three lines up had it right both times, which is the worse direction, because the guard reads the field; getgrocery's initializer snippet declared two of the six line groups it quotes; and atablefor's called a 241-line `Kiosk.configure` block «~60-line», so a reader computing the complement was told ~55 lines were cut where the answer is ~235. Each now states the cut, or the kept set together with the universe it came out of, per K-1104. Intent: this field is a human's signature on a completeness claim almost nothing can check, so the part that IS readable has to be exact.

- 2026-08-27: **`abridged:` now says which sense it means, and the corpus and the guidance finally agree (K-1104).** `bin/check-demo-derivations` told an author the field is «`none`, or a phrase naming what was LEFT OUT» while eleven of the seventeen snippet declarations it guards name what was KEPT — so an author following the error message wrote one shape and an author reading the documents wrote the other. The ruling picks by the field's purpose rather than by the count: the cut is what a reader cannot see, so the cut sense is canonical, and the kept sense is admissible only when the declaration also names the universe those lines came out of, which is what makes the complement derivable. The eleven were already legal and stay; the script now states the rule where it used to state half of it, and enforces the shape of it — a declaration that says «only» must say what it was only-of. Intent: a guard whose guidance disagrees with the artefacts it guards teaches the wrong thing twice a year, and the mechanical half is declared a shape check in the same breath.

- 2026-08-27: **the verdict lines that say whether the payment, the booking and the unlock worked were held to their punctuation and nothing else (K-1105).** `bin/check-demo-derivations` waves a pattern through without reading its wildcards once it carries "enough literal text", and it counted `==` as literal text — so `puts "  OK  #{label} == #{expected}"`, which prints twenty of the twenty-six published `OK … == …` lines, cleared the bar with `OK` plus `==` and both of its values went unread; hoteling's own `  OK  http_pay == 200` mutated to `== 500 (charged nothing, refunded twice)` passed, and the script said every line was held to a literal while it did. The bar now counts only alphanumerics, a bare number in a checked frame must be one the declared producers write as a numeral, any other whitespace-free capture must be a word they can print, and no capture may swallow the literal that terminates it; all 26 published lines were mutated in place to prove it, 47 of 50 red before and 50 of 50 after. What is left is a swap between two tokens the producer already names, which the header now states as its own bullet. Intent: a guard that reports on a line's punctuation while claiming to report on its content is worse than none, because it is the reason nobody looks.

- 2026-08-27: **two published transcripts enumerated a cut nobody had run, and `abridged:` is now bound to the rake DAG instead of merely being non-empty (K-1101).** hoteling and getgrocery each declared — and repeated in the prose — that everything above their first quoted line was «`demo:setup`'s `db:drop`/`db:create`/`db:schema:load`/`db:seed` chatter and nothing else»; measured by running the task it is eleven lines for hoteling with FIVE of them not db chatter, and thirty-three for getgrocery. The boundary claim was exact both times and only the enumeration was wrong, so the declarations now name the TASK whose output was cut and `bin/check-demo-derivations` resolves that name — the task must be one the demo defines, it must be a declared prerequisite of the quoted task, and no line of the block may be output that task itself prints. What the cut CONTAINS still cannot be checked without regenerating, which this script refuses for a reason, and the header says so. Intent: a field that is required but never read is a signature nobody countersigns, and a human eye reading the document is what caught both.

- 2026-08-27: **one of this repo's own guards was invisible to `grep`, and the byte that made it so is gone (T-128).** `bin/check-demo-derivations` marked a `#{…}` interpolation with a raw NUL, so `grep -n <constant> bin/check-demo-derivations` printed «Binary file … matches» and EXITED 0 — a real match with its evidence suppressed, under `/usr/bin/grep` as much as under the shell's wrapper, and with no zero for the «never report a zero» rule to catch. The marker is now U+FFFF written as an ASCII escape, a permanent Unicode noncharacter that — unlike NUL, which the script's own `\0` unescaping could produce from an ordinary literal — cannot arrive from quoted content, and the script aborts if one ever does. Intent: a guard a session cannot grep is a guard whose constants get quoted into ledger rows from memory.

- 2026-08-27: **the one command three adoption recipes tell an adopter to type is now held to the generator that answers it, instead of being exempted once per document (K-1099).** Three of `bin/check-demo-derivations`' four `derived: none` escape hatches were the same fenced `rails g kiosk:install`, byte-identical in atablefor, getgrocery and hoteling — a cross-demo fact spelled as a per-document exemption, so nothing would have noticed one drifting to `kiosk:setup` or the generator being renamed under all three at once. A third block class, `derived: generator`, derives the namespace TWICE from the generator itself — from its path and from its class nesting — and requires the two to agree with each other and with every line of the block; which demos carry the recipe is declared, so one that drops it fails too. Intent: cross-demo agreement is the weak oracle here, because three identical copies of a wrong command agree perfectly.

- 2026-08-27: **the four `before-after.md` narratives now DERIVE from named producers instead of promising it in prose (K-1051).** Every fenced block in them declares, in a comment a script reads, the rake task or source file it came from and what was left out; `bin/check-demo-derivations` then holds each transcript line to a literal that task actually prints and each snippet line to a line of the file it quotes under a declared, EXECUTABLE transformation — line MEMBERSHIP, never regeneration, because ids and dates change every run and one transcript legitimately has two shapes depending on the hour. The header states what it cannot do, starting with the fact that a subset test can never prove a transcript is complete. Intent: ten ledger rows were found in these four published documents by a human eye and none by a gate, and the «verbatim … nothing else touched» sentence they carried was the guarantee.

- 2026-08-27: **K-1090's own completeness sentence was false, and the sixteen engine and demo sites it missed now speak the vocabulary the engine already defined (K-1090, reopened).** The 21-page fix held; what did not was «the class is now closed fleet-wide», written after a sweep of static HTML that never read Ruby — `errors.rb` contradicted itself 200 lines apart, calling the branch point «the envelope's `error.code`» in its header and «flat, not `error.code`» at `to_problem`, and `server.rb`'s own module index still billed `Result` as a «success envelope» years after `to_envelope` was deleted. Sites where «envelope» is ACCURATE — the internal handler↔dispatch seam, the wrong-door bodies that really are nested, the agents.txt discovery envelope, AP2's spending envelope — are deliberately untouched. Intent: a do-not-re-audit claim is consulted by later sweeps before they decide not to look, so an over-broad one costs more than the defect it covered for.

- 2026-08-27: **cross-demo prose about the FRAMEWORK gets an oracle, and it says out loud what it cannot see (K-1069).** `bin/check-demo-copies` compares files; the sentences BETWEEN them — the routes drawer, what `rails g kiosk:install` emits, whether registration is tolled, whether KYC gates it — had no producer to derive from and so no checker at all, which is how one framework fact shipped at two texts in two demos and how one document contradicted itself across three sections. Nine claims are now declared over the two per-demo documents every other rule leaves uncompared, each bound to the framework file that makes it true, and the header states the four things the mechanism structurally cannot do — foremost that it enforces ONE text without knowing whether that text is right. Intent: the alternative to an honest partial guard is not a complete one, it is the human eye that found all five of these by accident.

- 2026-08-27: **the sixteen wrong-door bodies stop calling themselves «the Kiosk error envelope», and the one that answers a machine becomes a problem document (K-1092).** Seven CSRF rescues and seven JSON sign-out 401s named their courtesy body with a phrase that means the WIRE CONTRACT — which is a flat RFC 9457 problem document, not the `{ok:false, error:{…}}` shape 0.4 deleted — while the engine's own two signposts had recorded that exact distinction all along; the demo copies now say what the engine says, and the shape stays, because a body handed to a caller that dialled a HUMAN page must not pretend to be a wire answer. `Kiosk::Handler`'s wire-dispatch guard is the exception and changes shape: it is the one such body with no human on the other end. Intent: a demo is read as the reference implementation, so a courtesy that describes itself as a contract is the thing that misleads, not the courtesy.

- 2026-08-27: **«the retired 0.3 endpoints answer an ordinary 404» was true only for an authenticated caller, in 29 places, and is now tested rather than reworded (K-1094).** Authentication precedes verb dispatch, so `POST /kiosk/query` and `/kiosk/run` answer `401 unauthenticated` to a caller with no bearer — and every `RetiredWire` beat in the fleet dialled WITH one, which is why seven suites asserted the sentence and none probed the case it was wrong about. Two shipped READMEs, seven signpost comments, six walkthrough narrations, thirteen redteam sites and the e2e harness now carry the qualifier, and every beat probes both callers. Intent: a claim about the wire that no probe can falsify rots back; the anonymous probe is the half that cannot.

- 2026-08-27: **six deploy templates pointed an operator at a PoW-difficulty file no demo has ever had, and the class now has a gate (K-1093).** They cited `lib/pow_difficulty.rb`; it is `app/services/pow_difficulty.rb` in all seven demos and the runbook shipped beside them said so correctly the whole time. They now name the constant rather than the path — skooti's spelling, which cannot rot when a file moves — and `bin/check-demo-copies` resolves every source path a `deploy/env` template cites against the demo that template configures. Intent: second time a `deploy/` document has been caught citing a path nobody verified, and a template is the file an operator actually opens.

- 2026-08-27: **the same false endpoint list K-1088 deleted from the CSRF body was still live on the page every unrouted path returns, and is gone from all three static signposts (K-1090).** `public/404.html` — what `PublicExceptions` serves for EVERY unguessed path on seven origins, and the surface a lost assistant reads most often — told it to fetch the catalog «for the endpoints it actually serves — register/login, schema, query, run, pay», two of which have no route; all three pages also described an RFC 9457 problem document in the vocabulary of the 0.3 wrapper RFC 9457 replaced. They now name what the catalog carries, say `application/problem+json`, and point at the flat `code`. Intent: this is the third survivor of one class the changelog already called closed, so `bin/check-demo-copies` now reads these pages WHOLE — comments included, because every byte of a static page is served — and refuses both retired vocabularies.

- 2026-08-27: **the e2e harness stops discarding the only diagnosis its own failure messages cannot reconstruct (K-1091).** `rails new` ran with both streams to `/dev/null`, so a generator failure surfaced as a `cd` into a directory that was never created; two psql fixture inserts threw away the Postgres error that NAMES the violated constraint and answered every cause with one sentence. The generator now logs to a per-invocation file whose tail the failure prints, the inserts let stderr through, and the negative control that MUST fail keeps its discard with a comment saying why. Intent: same class as K-1084 at far lower stakes — a step whose plumbing destroys the evidence still costs the next reader the diagnosis.

- 2026-08-27: **the heavy PoW toll's seconds now name their machine everywhere in this repo that is still editable (K-1070).** K-1052 fixed the spec page and the landing; the gem's own README, its bench README, the library comments, the **published gemspec description rubygems.org renders without a clone**, the deploy runbook and checklist, three env templates and all seven demo initializers still printed `~10 s` (or `~9–10 s`) as a bare fact. They now say «on one M-series laptop core», the only hardware behind the figure, and state that the ~1.3 GiB is a property of (n=168, k=7) rather than of the host. Intent: a number an assistant or an operator budgets from must name what produced it; the three byte-locked `solve.py` copies twelve published skill cuts pin are deliberately untouched and can only move in a wave that republishes the solver under a new content-addressed name.

- 2026-08-27: **a column REMOVED from the engine's schema now ships a `DROP`, exactly as a column ADDED ships an `ADD` (K-1086).** `kiosk.settlements.raw_jws` was taken out of the canonical `CREATE` on 2026-08-23 with no migration to take it out of a database that already had it, so on any box provisioned in that window the column survives `text NOT NULL` with no default, head's eight-column settlement INSERT does not name it, and Postgres refuses every one — permanently, since nothing in the tree could reach it. The seven demos that hold the table gain a guarded drop migration; `bin/check-migration-replay` reported the wall on 20 schema vintages before it and none after, and a replayed 2026-08-20 database goes from a not-null violation to a settlement row. Intent: the delivery gap K-1074 named is symmetrical, and only its additive half had ever been written down.

- 2026-08-27: **the wrong-door signpost seven demos hand a JSON-dialing assistant stops naming two endpoints that 404 (K-1088).** The CSRF rescue told a caller that had just dialled the human sign-in page to fetch `/.well-known/kiosk.json` «for the register/login and schema/query/run/pay endpoints» — false twice over, since protocol 0.4 deleted `POST /kiosk/query` and `POST /kiosk/run` outright and `capabilities` has published module names since T-095. It now names what the document actually carries, and `bin/check-demo-copies` refuses the retired 0.3 wire vocabulary in these files so the next copy cannot inherit it. Intent: this is a live response body read by someone who is lost by construction and cannot check the hint, and the same string had already been corrected on the human pages and left here.

- 2026-08-27: **the production-boot smoke gains a third surface, and the roster rule it rested on is re-stated rather than defended (K-1085).** «One demo per unique HTML surface» assumed the production-only classes are per-surface; that holds for the eager-load, assume_ssl and bodyless-error classes and NOT for the K-436 class this script exists for — «the code SELECTs something this app's database has not got» is per-app, because the SQL is. tudu boots now too: its `/shared` housemate board is the fleet's only page rendered from a hand-written four-table `exec_query`, and its sign-up the fleet's only open registration. Intent: the widening does not close K-1074 and the script says so — it loads `structure.sql` from zero, so the box-versus-tree gap stays `bin/check-migration-replay`'s and the live probe's.

- 2026-08-27: **the box's only recovery tool stops throwing away the evidence of its own failure (K-1084).** `deploy/demo-reset.sh` ran all three of its commands with both streams discarded and read only the exit code, so an operator over `ssh` got «SEED FAILED» and nothing else — for failures that by their nature do not reproduce anywhere else, on the tool K-1074's box repair runs. Both streams now go to a per-invocation log, each failure branch prints its tail and the exact command to re-run, and the script exits non-zero when anything failed. Intent: a tool whose plumbing destroys the result it exists to produce is the same defect as reading a gate's answer out of a stale file.

- 2026-08-27: **the to-be-public repo stops pointing readers at private files, and eight shipped sentences stop describing a system that changed under them (K-1071, K-1072, K-1076..K-1081, K-1087).** Ten code comments, READMEs and changelog entries cited an umbrella-private decision log or ledger by bare NAME — a form every path-shaped scan reported clean — while summary lines, a gemspec's repo URLs and a security-posture paragraph still described a stub IdP, a 0.3 wire and a repository that will not exist; each now states what it means from what a clone actually contains.

- 2026-08-27: **`db:migrate` is exercised against a database that already exists, and the two defects that were only visible there are fixed (K-1074, K-1082, K-1083).** Every gate in this repo builds its database from zero out of `db/structure.sql`, which made production the only place `db:migrate` ever met a pre-existing one — so a column APPENDED on 2026-08-23 to a tudu migration first shipped a month earlier reached structure.sql and every laptop and could never reach the running box, and four live pages answered HTTP 500 for six days with CI green on all of them. Measured while fixing it: the 2026-08-20 renumbering of the kiosk-owned migrations makes `db:migrate` ABORT one step in with `PG::DuplicateTable` on any database that already carries the kiosk schema, so every deploy since has been half-completing in silence and no corrective migration could have been reached at all. The column moves to a new migration file, the engine's emitted DDL is guarded so it can replay (and repairs the three columns K-646's fold moved, which a guard alone would have stepped over), and `bin/check-migration-replay` now loads each historical structure.sql, runs `db:migrate`, and diffs the catalogue — one step per demo in the demos job, ~4-8 s. Intent: a migration set that only works from zero is a migration set the deploy cannot deliver, and the boxes need `deploy/demo-reset.sh` once because a schema they cannot migrate into is not repairable by shipping more migrations.

- 2026-08-26: **six demos stop promising, in the file an integrator copies from, a routes drawer that already shipped (K-1068).** Every `config/routes.rb` except getgrocery's carried «In a follow-up release these will be mounted via the engine's own routes drawer; for now we wire them manually here» while `kiosk-server`'s engine ships both halves today — the root-relative discovery block it appends to the host's route set and the mount-prefixed drawer — and atablefor's own file contradicted its promise seven lines below it. Measured before deleting rather than assumed: the 28 routes the engine draws cover every kiosk route all seven demos hand-wire, with nothing left over in any demo, and each of the six files already explains further down that it hand-draws BECAUSE that is the escape hatch the engine documents — so the two lines were removed, not rewritten, and getgrocery's shape is now the fleet's. Intent: this comment is where the same false claim in three published `before-after.md` files came from, and the copy check treats `routes.rb` as per-demo, so nothing was holding the six against each other.

- 2026-08-26: **an internal phase ledger stops shipping with the repo that goes public (K-1060).** A 32-line progress file under the agent-workflow scratch directory was TRACKED, so `git clone` reproduced it: internal phase labels, a dead branch name, pre-rewrite commit ids that no longer resolve, and a list of one demo's known open defects, published without the ledger that would let anyone act on them. It is untracked at the tip and stays on disk for the workflow that reads it; per the append-only rule this is a new commit removing the path, never a history rewrite. Intent: `.gitignore` never applies to an already-tracked path, which is exactly how this file sat inside a directory whose own ignore file reads `*`.

- 2026-08-26: **the four published `before-after.md` files stop asserting things no run produces and no file contains (K-1062..K-1067).** Three of them offered a fenced block under a «verbatim … nothing else touched» guarantee that had drifted into hand-editing — stale assertion strings, an omitted `Server stopped.`, and in hoteling's case an entire missing RUN 3 — so each block was RE-DERIVED from an actual run and, where an excerpt is genuinely wanted, the document now says where it begins and what was cut. The code snippets carried the same shape of promise and broke it more expensively: two of them had dropped the `maximum` ceiling K-1047 had landed hours earlier, un-shipping a fix inside the most copyable artefact the project publishes, so every snippet is now derived from the file it names with each elision marked at the point it happens. The prose was corrected against the code in the same pass — the registration chain all four demos actually run (challenge → 402 with server-minted challenges → solve → retry, which an assistant cannot front-run), the gate skooti's honesty note claimed and `register` does not have, hoteling's payment gate as the disjunction it is, the discovery controller kiosk-server has shipped for a while, and the model methods «we touch nothing of yours» was quietly untrue about. Intent: these files are the ones an integrator copies from, they have no gate at all, and a document that un-ships a fix as fast as it lands is the same class of defect as the fix.
