- 2026-08-26: **CI's atablefor matrix comment stops describing a capability assertion that has not existed since 0.4 (K-1067).** It named `[schema,query,run]`, the retired 0.3 vocabulary, where the engine computes `schema`/`queries`/`actions` and the demo's own task asserts them; the site survived K-1055's sweep because that sweep's blast-radius grep was filtered to `*.md` and required a space after each comma. Comment only, no job behaviour touched. Intent: a maintainer reading the matrix should learn what the gate asserts, not what it asserted two protocol minors ago.

- 2026-08-26: **skooti's published contrast stops attributing a KYC result to a flow that performs no KYC, and its reservation hold stops advertising an expiry it does not have (K-1053, K-1054, K-1056).** The block the document introduced as «a recorded no-human run» of `rake demo:rideflow` carried `"http_kyc":200` and a narrated «prove identity once → HTTP 200» step, for a compliance check the driver explicitly skips — a licence-free scooter rents on ownership and payment alone, and the attestation gates the motorcycle instead. It is now the verbatim RUN 1 transcript of an actual run, assertions included, rather than a hand-written approximation, which is how the invented key got in; the same pass replaces the two «TTL hold» sentences with what `reserve` really writes (the demo's own README already said the hold has no expiry) and conditions the README's «lighter than the default» PoW claim on the `KIOSK_POW_DIFFICULTY` knob, which at `high` makes skooti's params the bundled solver's default exactly. Intent: a block a document calls a recording must be one, and the fleet's four `before-after.md` files are a published surface no gate reads.

- 2026-08-26: **two more published claims in the unguarded `before-after.md` class are brought back to the tree (K-1055, K-1057).** atablefor advertised its capabilities as the retired 0.3 names `[schema, query, run]` while the engine computes `queries`/`actions` and the demo's own `demo:schema` asserts them; getgrocery told the reader its recorded run left the operator «a real order and a real payment» when that recording is the secret-free path whose settled amount is `stripe-mock`'s constant zero — the section says so twice in bold two lines away, but the sentence is quotable on its own and this repo goes public. Intent: a contrast document is read by people who will never run the task, so its sentences have to survive being read alone.

- 2026-08-26: **the heavy PoW toll's headline seconds now say which machine produced them, everywhere the wording is still mutable (K-1052).** `~10 s / ~1.3 GiB` is published as a bare fact on the landing page, in the `pow_notice` an operator running at `high` SERVES in its discovery document, and in an immutable skill cut an assistant is told to budget its solve from — while the only measurement behind it is one M-series laptop core with the reference numpy solver, and this project's own pre-submit checklist says not to quote the exact seconds until that is reproduced elsewhere. The memory figure is essentially hardware-independent and stays as it was; the seconds now carry the hardware, in all seven copies of `app/services/pow_difficulty.rb`. Intent: a number an assistant budgets from should name what it was measured on; the published cut that cannot be edited is the reason to say so everywhere that can.

- 2026-08-26: **a quantity the published descriptor calls valid is a typed 400 instead of a crash, in the two demos where a caller-supplied integer reached a bounded column unchecked (K-1047).** getgrocery declared `items[].qty` as `{type: "integer", minimum: 1}` with no ceiling, so a body its own descriptor accepted — 6_153_249 units of a €3.49 line — priced past the 4-byte `orders.total_cents`, `Order.insert!` raised in Ruby before any SQL reached the database, and the wire answered `500 action_failed`; sweeping for the class found atablefor's `party_size` doing the same on BOTH its surfaces, where it reached an ActiveRecord COMPARISON against `restaurant_tables.capacity` and put the runtime's own class name in the error body. Each bound is now DECLARED wherever JSON Schema can express one — a `maximum` that is the column's own width, inventing no basket size and no house limit on party size — and enforced by the handler where it cannot be: getgrocery's cart TOTAL is a sum of the operator's catalogue prices, so that half is a refusal naming the maximum, taken as soon as the prices resolve and before the replace path locks a row. Both batteries gained the probe the whole fleet was missing: every hostile-arg list in it varied an argument's TYPE and not one had ever sent an integer too LARGE for the column behind it, which is how this walked through three hostile-shape waves. Intent: a request the published contract calls valid must never be answered with a crash, and the axis that hid this one should be probed rather than remembered.

- 2026-08-26: **the flow-runner helper the whole demo fleet's ability to FAIL now rests on is held in lockstep, in the one directory the copy check does not scan (K-1046).** K-1043 closed a fail-open by hand-copying the same fifty lines of comment and `def` into seven `lib/tasks/demo.rake` files — byte-identical in six of them once the helper's own name is substituted — and `bin/check-demo-copies` excludes that directory on a rationale that said «per-demo by construction, and already covered by bin/check-ci-tasks», both halves of which the copy made false (the sibling check compares task inventory, never one demo's rake file against another's). The checker now declares the FRAGMENT rather than the file: the comment-plus-`def` block must agree byte-for-byte across the six, prose included, and the exit-status tail — status read first, abort quoting the child's own last line, JSON parsed only after — must agree across all seven, so tudu's deliberately different argv-form helper is exempt from how it builds the child and not from what it does with the child's status. Intent: the newest hand-copied construct in the fleet should not be its only unguarded one, and an exclusion's stated reason has to stay true.

- 2026-08-26: **atablefor's PoW section header names the selector instead of one of its legacy aliases (K-1045, the third false posture claim in the block K-1042 just repaired).** The header gave a single env flag as the `:query` toll's activation condition — true when three mutually-overriding `if ENV[…]` blocks were the whole mechanism, false since K-497 replaced them with `ATABLEFOR_POW_MODE`: the mode resolves to `:reputation` in production with no variable set at all, four other switches reach the same toll, and `deploy/env/atablefor.env.example` ships `KIOSK_POW_MODE=reputation` while setting that flag nowhere, so the header was false in the very environment this repo publishes for its flagship demo. It now scopes itself to the toll and names the selector; the variable-to-mode mapping stays written out once, at the selector, because re-listing it in a header is the second-copy class this file has been repaired for twice. Measured: getgrocery's equivalent header is accurate — that demo never got a mode selector — so this is deliberately not swept wider. Intent: a header that says when a gate is on must name the thing that decides it.

- 2026-08-26: **hoteling's headline demo task becomes runnable twice (K-1044).** `demo:book` was the only one of the seven tasks in that file declared without a `: :setup` prerequisite, and one pass consumed the entire inventory the driver can reach: the controller renders `Property.order(:name)` so the driver picks the same property every run, the flow pins the same three nights, and the task drives the flow twice — happy path plus the SKIP_PAY negative — so it takes both of that property's room types, the second as a `reserved` hold that nothing ever releases (the non-expiring hold is K-936's decided posture, so the flow may not "fix" this by releasing it, and releasing it would not be enough while the happy path's room stays `confirmed`). Reseeding is the remedy its six siblings already use and CI already did at job level; the README menu now states the reseed rule where the commands are listed, because a flat list of seven commands should say that running any of them drops the database. Intent: the task a reader is told to run first must survive being run again.

- 2026-08-26: **the demo fleet's flow-runner harness starts reading the exit status of the driver it just ran (K-1043).** All 33 flow-driver invocations across seven operator demos captured with a bare backtick (or `IO.popen`) and never consulted `$?`, so a task's verdict rested entirely on "did a line starting with `{` appear" — which fails OPEN: a driver that printed its well-formed JSON line and then died was reported as a PASS, demonstrated at head on stylish (child exit 3 → "All schema assertions passed", exit 0). The `*_flow.rb` scan that called the exposure latent missed the one driver in the set not named that way: getgrocery's `script/rls_proof.rb` prints its summary line before `exit 1` on a breach, so the shape was reachable, and masked only because that rake task happens to re-check the same three numbers itself. Each demo now funnels every driver call through one helper that reads the status FIRST and, on a non-zero child, aborts naming the child's own last output line — so a real failure stops reading as a JSON defect that points at the wrong file. The sibling `psql` probes are deliberately left alone: they capture into a value that is then compared, so they fail closed. Intent: the reference harness an adopter copies must not certify a run whose exit code it never looked at.

- 2026-08-26: **atablefor's initializer stops telling a reader that its no-human booking flow does no proof-of-work at all (K-1042, the sibling K-1041's class sweep was scoped not to touch).** Two lines in the opening PoW block said the `demo:book` flow passes with no PoW handling and that booting without `KIOSK_POW_DEMO=1` means no PoW — unconditional claims about the origin's posture under a header that scopes only the QUERY toll, while `registration_pow_count`/`registration_pow_params` are assigned after the `case ATABLEFOR_POW_MODE` block ends and the flow's own driver registers through the 402-solve-retry helper. Both sentences now name the toll they are about, and the register gate is pointed at rather than restated, copying the clause the same file's `off` mode entry already carried. Measured across the seven demo initializers and the e2e fixture, atablefor was the only remaining file with the claim — it survived K-1041's sweep because that grep was for the literal words the hoteling copy used. Intent: a posture sentence has to name its gate, whatever wording it reaches for.

- 2026-08-26: **hoteling's initializer stops claiming its whole origin's PoW is off by default, one line above the claim K-1038 just repaired (K-1041).** The opening paragraph made an unconditional off-by-default statement about the origin's PoW posture and only then narrowed to the browse flag — while `registration_pow_count`/`registration_pow_params` are assigned outside the `KIOSK_POW_BROWSE_DEMO` branch, so the register gate is on with the flag unset, which the same file already said in every place it describes that gate. The sentence now names the two gates and their opposite default postures and leaves each gate's section to own its detail; measured across the seven demo initializers and the e2e fixture, hoteling was the only file carrying the claim. Intent: the posture sentence a reader forms a demo's threat model from has to say WHICH gate it is true of.

- 2026-08-26: **getgrocery's red-team header stops mapping out a `Profile` that sits in the same file (K-1040, the tail of the class).** Three lines typed which verb was bound to `per_user_query`, `forge_action` and `gated_action`, directly above the pointer that had just replaced the `requires_kyc`/`pow_difficulty` pair for the same reason — so the block told a reader the constructor was the single copy while restating it. Nothing was false, and the map is deleted rather than guarded: measured, six of the seven demo red-team suites carry no such map at all, hoteling and skooti included — the only other two that build a `Profile` — so the orientation it bought was already being paid for elsewhere, at the constructor, where each binding now carries the note the map was compressing. What it could not survive is a rename, which is the K-710 shape twice recurred; T-078's commission stays scoped to beat NAMES and is deliberately not widened to argument values. Intent: one place per fact, and a header that claims a single copy must not be a second one.

- 2026-08-26: **the e2e harness stops retyping the one register-PoW constant it already sets (K-1039).** `n=96 k=5` was hand-written in the fixture that defines `E2E_REGISTRATION_POW_PARAMS`, in the shared register helper, and in `assistant.sh` — the file an adopter reads to see what a real client does — so one hand edit to the constant would have left three documents describing a toll the server no longer charged. Weaker than the demo rows it follows, and measured to be: this harness resolves no difficulty knob, so nothing but that hand edit could move it and all three copies were true. All three now point at the constant instead of quoting it, and the count stays asserted where it already was — off the 402 the server answered with. Intent: a harness that exists to show real behaviour should not describe it from memory.

- 2026-08-26: **hoteling's initializer stops opening with an Equihash size its own environment can change (K-1038).** The file's first paragraph stated the browse toll's `(n, k)` outright while `EQUIHASH_BROWSE_PARAMS = PowDifficulty.params` reads that pair out of `KIOSK_POW_DIFFICULTY` further down — so `high` made the first sentence of the file false at 168/7 with no edit to the tree, and the hosted deploy already ships that setting for a sibling demo. It was the one line of its kind across the four initializers that carry these numbers; every other states the knob's mapping or conditions itself on which level the demo ships. A comment cannot read a value, so the sentence names the knob and the mapping stays written out once, beside the constant it governs; the escalation claim itself is untouched, because the constants behind it are real. Intent: the first thing a reader of a config file sees must not be the one claim in it that an operator can falsify by setting a variable.

- 2026-08-26: **getgrocery's and hoteling's red-team banners stop typing the gate posture they stand on (the two siblings K-1035's own report surfaced).** Each printed `requires_kyc: false` as a hand-kept literal directly beneath a line that already read `profile.pow_difficulty` off the object, and then glossed that derived number with a typed `(register PoW ON)` — so `0 (register PoW ON)` and a KYC-demanding profile announcing `false` were both printable, 660 and 940 lines away from the `Profile.new` they restate; getgrocery carried a third copy of both in its own file header. Both banners now read the profile every generic scenario reads to decide whether it is applicable, and the file-header copy is replaced by a pointer to the single constructor. Measured across all seven demos: skooti, getgrocery and hoteling are the only suites that build a `Profile` at all — the other four print nothing but derived counts — so the class is closed rather than moved. Intent: a battery's banner is the first claim an evaluator reads, and it must not be able to describe a different run than the one underneath it.

- 2026-08-26: **four more skooti places stop naming an Equihash size an environment variable can move (same class, outside the file K-1035 fixed).** `n=96 k=5` was typed into the schema driver's header comment, into both the `demo:redteam` and `demo:schema` `desc` bodies, and into the published `before-after.md` — while `KIOSK_POW_DIFFICULTY=high` moves the register gate to 168/7 with no edit to this tree, which is the setting atablefor's hosted deploy already ships. The driver now READS the pair off the 402 the server answered with (every challenge carries its own `params`), which beats any config read: it is what THIS server demanded of THIS request, and it needs no assumption about which environment the harness handed to which process. The two `desc` bodies NAME THE KNOB rather than derive it, deliberately — a `desc` is evaluated on every rake/rails task load, so a derived one converts a documentation string into a load-time failure mode — and the narrative names the knob for the plainer reason that prose has no run to read from. Intent: a number a reader cannot check is only as true as the last person who remembered to change it.

- 2026-08-26: **skooti’s red-team header stops typing facts it can read, and seven registrations stop pretending to configure a gate (K-1035).** The two lines above the count K-1033 had just made honest were still hand-kept literals: `Equihash n=96 k=5 (pow_difficulty>0)` and `requires_kyc: true`, restating a `Profile` 450 lines below and a server knob one file over. Measured, the pair announced the opposite of the run it introduced — a profile set to `pow_difficulty: 0, requires_kyc: false` still printed `>0` and `true`, and `KIOSK_POW_DIFFICULTY=high` gated /register at n=168 k=7 while the header kept claiming 96/5. Both halves are read off the objects the run actually uses now (the profile, and the same `PowDifficulty` module the initializer reads), with the driver-env caveat stated where it is printed. Beside it, the `pow_difficulty: 20` typed into all seven skooti-local `register!` calls is gone rather than re-derived: the kwarg is INERT — `build_register` never reads it, PoW rides the server’s 402 challenges — so threading a live value would have removed the literal while keeping the lie that a call site was configuring something. Intent: a battery’s header is the first claim an evaluator reads, and a claim that cannot follow the thing it describes is only true by luck.

- 2026-08-26: **one skooti `demo:redteam` run stops reporting its own size two different ways (K-1033).** The header printed `scenarios: 17` — the registry — and the summary forty-eight lines later printed `26 BLOCKED`, which is that registry plus the nine one-off beats skooti runs outside it; both numbers were derived and correct, and the pair still read as «nine attacks ran that the header did not admit exist» to the one reader who matters, an evaluator deciding whether the battery is real. The header now says what it counts and the summary prints the decomposition (`26 BLOCKED (17 registered + 9 skooti-local)`), both still read off the live collections, so no literal was introduced and the nine lambdas are not pretended into registry entries; skooti is the only one of the seven demos with beats outside `runner.run`, and the file now says so where the number is printed. Intent: a battery's own output is a claim about what it ran, and two numbers for one run is a claim that contradicts itself.

- 2026-08-26: **`book_table` stops telling a caller an identifier was not given when it was given (K-1030).** A `restaurant_id` or `restaurant_table_id` of `0` or below parsed fine, was read by the guard, and was then reported as «missing param: …» — the K-581/K-582 class of error body that misinforms rather than informs, and a case the same demo already split correctly one line down, where a zero party answers «party_size must be >= 1». A genuinely absent identifier keeps the «missing» wording byte-for-byte and the wrong-shape sentence is untouched; the out-of-range case gets its own sentence naming the bound. Nothing observable moved, because the declared `minimum: 1` refuses that value a layer earlier — the descriptor-less path the guard exists for is where it was reachable. Intent: a refusal an assistant acts on has to name which of the three things went wrong.

- 2026-08-26: **which demos ship the per-demo shape guard is checked by the build instead of asserted in a comment (K-1031).** `bin/check-demo-copies` declares `app/operations/wire_arguments.rb` `:per_demo` — «not compared» — so its `why:` was unguarded prose, and two of its facts had gone false: it said the copies had «nothing else in common» while a helper had since become byte-identical in two of them, and it accounted for six demos of eight when four lack the file for three different reasons. The demo set moves to the `ships:` declaration seven other entries already use, so a fifth copy or a vanished fourth fails the build; what genuinely IS shared between the copies is stated as a measurement and left to T-120, whose question is the mechanism for holding a shared METHOD inside a per-demo file. Intent: the prose in a manifest is a claim about the tree, and a claim nothing reads goes stale in silence.

- 2026-08-26: **the two identifiers `book_table` opens with stop being read looser than the schema that declares them, one line under the guard that was just fixed (K-1028).** `restaurant_id` and `restaurant_table_id` were still a bare `.to_i` after K-1027 corrected `party_size` beside them: six of the standard probe shapes raised a `500` for a value the descriptor already forbids, and `1.5` came out as 1 — so a fractional id RESOLVED TO A ROW THE CALLER NEVER NAMED, which the seeded data then turned into either a confirmed booking at the wrong table or a refusal naming a table nobody asked for. Neither is reachable on the wire, and both are reachable on the descriptor-less path the guard's own comment names as its reason to exist. They go through the same JSON-Schema `integer` `party_size` uses now (`2.0` still resolves to 2, so nothing observable moved), and the red-team beat's comment — which correctly said these two pinned the DESCRIPTOR ALONE — is rewritten to enumerate the two layers there now are. Intent: the sentence a beat writes about itself has to stay true the day the code under it changes.

- 2026-08-26: **atablefor's shared party-size guard stops being looser than its own schema, and the demo finally gets the hostile-shape battery its siblings have carried since K-773 (K-1027).** The guard read a bare `raw.to_i`, so six of the standard probe shapes — `true`, `false`, `[]`, `{}`, `[1]`, `{"a" => 1}` — had no `to_i` at all and raised a `500` for a value the published descriptor already forbids, while `1.5` came out of it as a party of ONE. The file's own comment names the descriptor-less `BookTableOperation` path as the reason the guard exists, and that is exactly the path where nothing has coerced the argument, so the guard did not hold where it said it was needed. It mirrors JSON Schema's `integer` now (whole numbers, `2.0` included, because json_schemer accepts that — re-measured against this demo's own bundle), and a new red-team beat probes the shapes on `book_table`'s five arguments, `availability`'s party size and `cancel_booking`'s id, enumerating which layer answers which rather than claiming coverage. Intent: a defence-in-depth layer that only holds while the layer in front of it holds is not a second layer.

- 2026-08-25: **the two remaining wire integers that were read looser than their own schema now agree with it, and the second layer is measured rather than asserted (K-1025).** getgrocery's `delivery_slot_id` read `raw.to_s.to_i`, so `1.5` came out as slot 1 — inside the declared 1..6 — and hoteling's `limit`, `min_stars` and `max_price_cents` bypassed the strict guard that demo already owned for `property_id`. Neither was reachable on the wire: the schema and the query decoder both refuse those shapes first, which is precisely why a defence-in-depth layer that only holds while the layer in front of it holds was worth fixing rather than leaving. Both red-team beats now record WHICH layer answers which probe, and the claim was corrected where the mutation disagreed with it — dropping `min_stars`' declared type leaves the four SHAPE probes refused and the two RANGE probes answered 200, because `minimum`/`maximum` stop applying to a value nobody declared a number. Intent: a guard should be exactly as strict as the contract published in front of it, in both directions.

- 2026-08-25: **the one sentence in a demo's `application_controller.rb` that asserts a fact about the tree is now checked against the tree (K-1021).** That file is compared `:code` — identical modulo comments, which is right for a header that must name its own demo — and that is exactly how «the Kiosk wire controllers ship their own bases inside kiosk-server» lived in seven demos plus the e2e fixture until someone read it: nothing in `bin/check-demo-copies` looked at prose at all, in any copied file. Each copy must now carry «here `Kiosk::A` and `Kiosk::B` subclass X», and both halves are derived from the controllers on disk rather than from a list kept in the checker; the pre-fix sentence fails in the demo that carries it. Intent: `:code` means the prose is unguarded, so a per-demo comment claiming a fact rots in N copies at once.

- 2026-08-25: **getgrocery's cart guard now refuses what its own schema refuses, so «both layers» is two layers (K-1020).** The demo's second-line check read `(item[:qty] || 1).to_s.to_i`, and the comment above its red-team probe said `false` was the one shape only the schema caught. There were two — `false`, which `||` reads as absent, and `1.5`, which `"1.5".to_i` turns into 1 — and both came out of the guard as a legal quantity of one. Neither was ever reachable on the wire, because `input_schema` declares `qty` an integer and validates every call; what was wrong is the story, and a defence-in-depth layer that only works while the layer in front of it works is not one. The guard now mirrors JSON Schema's own `integer` (whole numbers, `2.0` included, because the validator accepts that), and dropping `qty`'s declared type leaves the battery green instead of booking two silent 200s. Intent: the second layer should be worth the sentence written about it.

- 2026-08-24: **the last two env vars an initializer still resolved for itself moved to the environment files, and an initializer can no longer resolve one at all (K-699, K-657).** Phil decided in August that env reading, dev/test fallbacks and crash-if-absent fetches belong in `config/environments/*` as Rails custom config and that initializers READ it -- and the sweep that carried it out missed the same pair in all seven copies at once, `app_role` and `system_role`, because nothing looked. They are published from the environment files now, byte-identically across the seven, with behaviour unchanged; `bin/check-demo-copies` fails on any `ENV.fetch` left in a demo initializer, carrying one declared allowance whose staleness is itself an error. Beside it, the four files of the KYC surface are pinned to the two demos that were reviewed, so a verb shape already decided against cannot spread to an eighth demo -- and cannot quietly disappear either, which is the tripwire the wire migration will have to trip. Intent: a convention nobody checks survives exactly until the next copy-paste, and these seven apps ARE copy-paste.

- 2026-08-24: **a rename that invalidates a published conformance citation now reddens the build that made it (K-1007).** The Kiosk conformance matrix maps every normative statement of the protocol to a test in this repository and names that test by the text of its `it`/`describe`/`context` label -- line numbers rot silently, anchors fail only on a real rename -- but the matrix lives outside this repo and the checker that walks it was run by nothing except a person: one wave renamed a device-authorization example, invalidated a cited anchor mid-wave, and it was caught only because a second process happened to be running the checker. The 533 anchors that point here are published as `spec/conformance-anchors.tsv` (path and label, no prose), `bin/check-conformance-anchors` asserts each occurs exactly once, and a CI job runs it on every push; the generator on the other side fails when the file is not current, so the two halves cannot drift apart. Intent: evidence this repository publishes about itself should be checkable by this repository.

- 2026-08-24: **every skill consumer moves to `skill-v0.4.7.md`, and the guard that holds a demo's README to its task list now holds it to its VERB list too (K-981 relink, K-987, K-986, K-988..K-994).** getgrocery's README opened by naming three queries and three actions against four and four, so `kyc_status` -- the verb an assistant polls to clear the alcohol gate -- was undocumented in the demo's own README while `demo:agecheck` asserted it; `bin/check-ci-tasks` now derives each demo's registered verbs from its controllers and fails when one is named nowhere in the README, which is the same presence-only rule it already applies to `demo:` tasks. The truthfulness pass beside it: the reset script no longer says a demo order is cited on the landing (it is not, and that falsehood was the stated reason getgrocery is reset differently), the e2e README stops miscounting the published schemas, stylish stops configuring a spending-cap seam a payment-free origin can never consult, `Base#user_active?` says it is an unused extension point rather than describing a callback nothing fires, an 83-line block duplicated inside kiosk-server's CHANGELOG is gone, skooti's orphaned `bin/ble-unlock` is anchored where the firmware instructions are, and two doc pointers that resolved to nothing are corrected. Intent: a README that is wrong about the surface it documents costs more than one that is silent.

- 2026-08-24: **the browser link page stops dropping the human's role, and every skill consumer moves to `skill-v0.4.6.md` (K-995, K-981, K-888).** The engine's own «Link an assistant» page minted a link code without the holder's role while the JSON endpoint beside it captured one, so roles-from-IdP (ADR-0011 Path A) simply did not happen on the door a human actually walks through: a fresh key was provisioned at `registration_role` — at stylish, an owner minting from the page got a `customer` assistant — and a rebind kept the key's PREVIOUS role while its principal changed, which is the one case the landing page promises outright. The ceremony's outcome no longer depends on which surface performed it. Beside it, the device-grant user_code alphabet's published brute-force space is 31^8 everywhere it appears rather than in one file, and the seven demos plus the engine default re-pin to the cut that finally tells an assistant what `reach` is. Intent: a security property that holds on one of two doors is not a property.

- 2026-08-23: **the demos stop explaining themselves to the reader and start reading like the reference they are (K-917, K-918, K-714).** Phil's own review of `board_controller.rb` named the problem: 47% of the fleet's copyable `app/` code was comment, and much of it narrated the ledger rather than the code — how a bug was found, what a line used to be, which finding removed it. The rule now is that a comment explains the CODE and never its history: 5641 comment lines become 3556 across the same 92 files, and what stayed is the part a reader cannot re-derive (a fail-closed reason, an ordering requirement, an Arel trap that looks right and is silently wrong). Beside it, twelve verb `description`s stopped restating protocol rules `skill.md` already states once — empty-array semantics, the 400 that names its domain, "not paginated" — which is 1.6 KB off every catalog fetch on six origins and, more to the point, prose that says what the VERB means instead of what the protocol means. And the live-activity table stops being created by `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` on the request path: it is a migration in all seven demos, the insert is a bind-parameter `exec_insert`, and a spec asserts no DDL reaches the wire path at all. Intent: the demos are what an operator copies, so they should be worth copying.

- 2026-08-23: **the eighth origin gets the rule the other seven got, and the guard stops looking away from it (K-974).** The e2e fixture host published an appointment slot two months in the past at three declaration sites and in the prose beside them — the exact class K-972 closed across the demos, still standing here because the guard that enforces it scanned `kiosk-demo-*` and nothing else. The slots are resolvable procs now, and the guard reads the fixture host's controllers too. The reason the fix had been deferred — that a dynamic slot would make the catalog's ETag move under a rolling memo window and flake an e2e assertion — turned out to be wrong, and is now pinned as a test: the memo epoch is a cache key and never reaches the digest, so a rebuild with unchanged data publishes the same version. Intent: a rule that holds for seven origins and not the eighth has a hole shaped like the thing nobody looks at.

- 2026-08-23: **a deploy can no longer be quietly missing its edge rate limit (K-976).** `deploy/Caddyfile` ships the per-IP backstop commented out, and has to — a stock Caddy binary refuses to load a config naming `rate_limit`, so shipping it enabled would break a default install at boot. That made enabling it a manual runbook step, and skipping a manual step was silent: the box comes up, all eight sites serve, and nothing anywhere says `/kiosk/auth/register` is an unbounded CPU sink — the exact exposure K-540 exists to close, reproduced by anyone following the shipped docs. `deploy/check-edge-ratelimit.sh` is the loud half: it reads the installed Caddyfile and names every vhost that does not reach a `rate_limit` directive, and a CDN or WAF in front has to be DECLARED rather than assumed. The runbook and the checklist now make running it a step rather than an option. Intent: the safe configuration should be the one you cannot skip by accident.

- 2026-08-23: **the anti-scalping toll and the harness that pays it are both gated now, and the exclusion that hid them was never measured (K-790, K-841, K-844).** `demo:pow` was opted out of CI on every demo as "the heavy memory-hard battery" — but the weight belongs to `KIOSK_POW_DIFFICULTY=high`, which only the hosted deploy sets, so CI would have run it at the `low` default in 2.7 s on atablefor and 3.7 s on getgrocery, less than tasks the same job already runs. Both now run, which is the first gate anywhere on atablefor's valid-solve/wrong-nonce pair and on either demo's bad-proof penalty counter. Beside them, getgrocery's and hoteling's redteam batteries run a second time with the operator's toll switched ON, so the client's 402-PoW retry is pinned on the wire rather than only against a stub. Intent: an exclusion has to be measured at the configuration CI actually uses, and this is the third whose stated reason described a different one.

- 2026-08-23: **a demo never writes down a day, and a guard makes that a rule rather than a habit (K-972).** Three demos published `example_params` an assistant copying verbatim was refused for — getgrocery's delivery date and atablefor's seating had been 400s for a fortnight, hoteling's was correct on the day it was measured and would have expired on 1 September — and stylish quoted a fixed instant back at the caller in the very refusal that asks it to retry. Nobody had edited any of them: the clock moved, which is the one defect a review pass cannot be relied on to catch, because the file it must look at has not changed. Every dated example is now a proc the engine resolves when the catalog is READ, each origin names that instant once in its own clock, and `kiosk-test-support` fails on any calendar date in demo runtime code. Intent: an example that says «copy this verbatim» has to still be true tomorrow.

- 2026-08-23: **atablefor's public board stops having a login address to fall back on (K-973).** The reservations board anyone can fetch fell back to the diner's email local part masked past two characters when an account had no display name — the construction philslist dropped in K-913 and tudu in K-950, and the one §7.2 now names outright as «not a third option». The address is gone from the board's SELECT as well as from the fallback, so no later reader of that row can publish it by accident, and a nameless diner is published as an opaque handle derived from the account UUID rather than from anything a reader can enumerate. Intent: the third demo with this shape closes it the same way as the first two, and the column stops being available to get wrong.

- 2026-08-23: **tudu's roster names people, and the name is never what they log in with (K-950).** `list_members` plucked `users.email` into a wire field called `handle`, so every housemate on a shared list read every other housemate's login address, and the demo's own web board published a masked local part of the same column — consented disclosure, which is materially weaker than an open board, and still a credential on the wire. Both surfaces now read `users.display_name` through one helper, an account that chose no name is published as an opaque `member-<hex>` derived from its UUID rather than from anything a reader can enumerate, and tudu's sign-up form grows the field that lets a real visitor choose one. Two redteam beats pin it: no account address anywhere in a roster or `whoami` body, and a visitor who signs up with a name is named by it. Intent: sharing a list with somebody is not handing them your credentials.

- 2026-08-23: **there is no availability in the past, and nothing sells into it (K-969).** hoteling answered `200` to `reserve_room` for `check_in: "1900-01-01"` — a real booking, a real quote, and an `availability` list of rooms for nights that have already happened — and stylish booked an appointment at any well-formed instant, including a century ago. Both now refuse from a single guard the read and write halves share, and each demo STATES what «past» means for it rather than leaving it to the reader: hoteling sells by the DAY in the property's own clock and counts tonight as bookable, stylish sells an INSTANT and refuses one that has passed, and getgrocery's two halves stop reading two different clocks for the same question. Every demo with a dated verb now pins both halves adversarially — an availability probe that must be refused, a booking probe that must be refused, and a future control beside each so the refusal cannot pass by refusing everything. Intent: an assistant must never be shown a slot it cannot book, and must never be quietly sold one it never saw.

- 2026-08-23: **a settlement stops carrying a signature nobody writes (K-948).** `kiosk.settlements.raw_jws` was `text NOT NULL` set to the empty string by its only writer, sitting beside three mandate tables whose identically-named column holds a real assistant-signed JWS — which is exactly the misreading that shipped as "non-repudiation both ways" (K-876). The column is dropped rather than renamed or given a producer: an operator counter-signature would be a normative protocol element and an ADR, not a column, and ADR-0002 has said `settlements` has no `raw_jws` since the table was named. Intent: the schema should not promise evidence the protocol does not collect.

- 2026-08-23: **two lockstep copies with no consumer are deleted, and the manifest learns to say who is supposed to have a file (K-860, K-883).** hoteling's `script/devise_session.rb` and skooti's `script/bound_assistant.rb` were held byte-identical to six and three copies that drivers actually run, while nothing in their own demo required them. Deleting them alone would have left the next wave free to copy them back unnoticed, so `bin/check-demo-copies` entries may now declare their exact `ships:` set — an unlisted demo that ships the file fails, and so does a listed demo that stops. Intent: a hand-copied file earns its place by being used, and the check should be able to say so.

- 2026-08-23: **the three cashier decorators read a cart the way the wire hands it over, and no demo lockfile can be short a platform its siblings carry (K-713, K-962).** A mandate arrives as a JWS and the verifier symbolises only its top-level claims, so `line_items` reach the cashier with String keys always — yet getgrocery's, hoteling's and skooti's decorators each branched on a symbol spelling as well, thirteen times, which no request could ever produce and only the demos' own in-process capture-window drivers kept alive; those drivers now build the wire's shape and the branches are gone. Alongside: philslist alone resolved for `arm64-darwin` and nothing else, so the deploy box rewrote its tracked lockfile on every push, and `bin/check-demo-copies` now holds one PLATFORMS set across all eight. Intent: a demo is copied verbatim, so a shape it implies must be a shape that exists.

- 2026-08-23: **every skill consumer moves to `skill-v0.4.5.md`, and a solved proof is validated against the published PoW schema on the live wire (K-872..K-875, K-849).** The e2e schema-conformance step now checks the `Kiosk-PoW` header this origin accepted against `pow.schema.json`'s client-written half, which the 402's cross-file reference could never reach, so the u64 index bound has over-the-wire coverage instead of unit coverage only.

- 2026-08-23: **link-code single-use is decided by the row instead of by a check against a stale snapshot, the demo fleet stops shipping HSTS-less and job-less scaffolding, and eight DB-wide `COUNT(*) >= 1` assertions start naming the row the run just created (K-887, K-888, K-890, K-916, K-931, K-933, K-861, K-862, K-884, K-885, K-946, K-509).** `POST /auth/claim` read `consumed?` off the snapshot it had just fetched and only wrote the consume after binding, so two concurrent redemptions of ONE link code bound two DIFFERENT assistant keys to the human's account and neither got the `409` that would have told the human their code leaked; both binding ceremonies now take an atomic conditional claim from the same store primitive, placed after the possession proof (a bad proof must leave the code live) and before the bind. Alongside: the edge Caddyfile emits `Strict-Transport-Security`, which nothing anywhere did, so a client's first request to a bare hostname was plaintext before the redirect; ActiveJob leaves seven applications that never defined a job; skooti's last three flow-only helpers leave the production eager-load set; and the three payment demos' happy-path assertions can no longer pass on a row an earlier run left behind. Intent: a demo is what a first adopter copies, and a guarantee that is argued in a comment rather than enforced by the row is a guarantee only until the second request arrives.

- 2026-08-23: **hoteling stops promising an expiry it never enforces, tudu's home page starts carrying both discovery signals, and the served-area vocabulary gets one owner (K-936, K-944, K-947, K-708, K-805, K-832, K-935, K-937, K-943, K-954, K-957, K-965).** `reserve_room`'s wire descriptor told an assistant the hold "expires on its own" and "the room-night goes back on sale" — `expires_at` is written and read by nothing, so a hold stamped an hour ago still confirms; the deadline is now described as recorded rather than enforced, everywhere it is described, and the constant is named for what it is. tudu was the one demo whose ROOT answered with the `<link rel="kiosk">` tag alone while the fleet claim says all seven carry the `Link:` header too, and both signals now come from one helper. hoteling's twelve-district enum moves beside `AMENITY_POOL` with a seed-time check, so a rename cannot leave a filter value no row can reach. The rest is claims-vs-behaviour repair on documents an adopter reads: skooti's gate map and its no-KYC scooter flow, prove's retired four-verb enumeration on its own landing page, getgrocery's hand-written attack totals (now derived, not restated), the e2e harness's file inventory and its one over-claimed "what it verifies" bullet, the salt width two READMEs disagreed on, and the last `query + run` sentence a demo still printed on screen. Intent: a launch surface is only as honest as the sentence a reader checks first.

- 2026-08-23: **the three cashier decorators stop hand-quoting SQL, and the two gates that had been argued rather than asserted now exist (K-654, K-724, K-773).** getgrocery's, hoteling's and skooti's payment adapters kept the atomic compare-and-set claim they need — `update_all` still has no RETURNING — but every value in it now travels as a `$N` bind instead of through `conn.quote`, and hoteling's and skooti's spliced `SET` fragment became two whole statements; nothing was exploitable, and that was never the charge, because a demo is the file a provider copies and the copy is where the forgotten `quote` lives. Three standing assertions land with it: a spec over the tracked demo sources that fails on any new value splice, a skooti spec that presents the licence gate with the non-boolean spellings the wire cannot produce and asserts each one still blocks, and a hostile-shape redteam beat in each of the three converted demos. The beats found a live 500 on their first run — a well-formed `check_in` of `0000-01-01` priced a 739,000-night stay past the `total_cents` column and crashed on INSERT — now a typed 400 (K-968). Intent: a fix nobody can re-break on purpose is a fix that has not been made.

- 2026-08-23: **the equihash README's headline lever is measured rather than asserted, and every skill consumer moves to `skill-v0.4.4.md` (K-956, K-871).** The comparison table claimed a `millions×` solve/verify lever for the shipped n=168 k=7 while the same file's benchmark table gave ~9.6 s of solve against ~18 ms of verify — ~530×, wrong by about three orders of magnitude, and the one number in that README a reader checks because the lever IS the metered-toll argument (ADR-0007). It now states the ratio with both operands beside it, a spec derives it from the README's own numbers so a retune cannot leave it behind, and the unbenched Cuckatoo29 column says so instead of borrowing the same word. The seven demo pins and the engine default follow the new skill cut, and the pin spec now also asserts that the cut the demos pin names every device-grant error `DeviceCodeGrant` can emit — read out of the engine, never listed — plus a give-up horizon. Intent: a number no gate derives is a number that rots, and the skill an operator pins is only as good as the loop it tells an assistant to run.

- 2026-08-23: **a verb declares whose rows it may touch, and nine across three demos now say so (K-949, spec §7.2 / ADR-0028).** `reach :published` / `:consented` / `:role` joins `kind` in the handler macro family, defaults to `:principal` when a declaration says nothing, and is published as a required field on the public schema catalog — so philslist's open board, tudu's shared lists and stylish's role-gated staff forecast state on the wire what they had been doing silently against the spec's strongest sentence. A reach outside the four raises at class-body load; a fleet-wide spec holds the reviewed inventory of cross-principal verbs against the sources in both directions; and philslist's and tudu's isolation flows now call every `principal`-reach verb as an adversary and fail if one returns the foreign row, so deleting a declaration is caught on the live wire rather than in a comment. Intent: an authorization model is the operator's to choose, but it has to be legible to the assistant on the other end.

- 2026-08-22: **two gems shipped a `LICENSE.txt` that is not the Apache-2.0 text their gemspec declares (K-959, K-960).** `kiosk-pow-equihash` carried the MIT license verbatim, and `kiosk-reputation` carried an Apache-2.0 text with three altered sentences — one of them inside the section 3 patent grant, the clause an adopter of a framework is most likely to read, where "patent claims licensable by such Contributor" had become "patent licenses licensable by each Contributor". Both now carry the same canonical Apache-2.0 bytes as the other twelve, so all fourteen gem `LICENSE.txt` files and the repository `LICENSE` are one identical document. Intent: the licence a gem declares and the licence it ships must be the same text, and a licence is quoted, never retyped.

- 2026-08-22: **the open classifieds board stops publishing every seller's email address (K-913).** `browse_listings` is cross-owner by design, so its seller column was disclosed to every principal that could complete `/auth/register` — and that column was `users.email`, which meant registering an assistant returned the login address of every account holder who had ever posted; the public web board published a masked local-part of the same column, which against a known domain is barely a mask. Sellers are now named by an opaque pseudonym derived from the account UUID rather than from any address, identical on the wire and on the page, and PER-SELLER rather than per-listing on purpose: unlinkability is a promise a small board cannot keep, while "these two listings are one seller" is what a classifieds board is for and is the household property philslist exists to demonstrate. The contact path the leak was standing in for is stated rather than invented — the board relays no messages, so a seller reaches a buyer through the contact detail they chose to put in their own listing text, which `post_listing` now tells an assistant to ask its human for. A redteam scenario fails if any account address reaches the wire again, if a handle stops being opaque, or if it stops being stable per seller. Intent: a demo whose pitch is that a human is not tracked must not hand out the user table.

- 2026-08-22: **philslist's and tudu's READMEs stop claiming they ship no mandate or settlement tables (K-940).** They do ship them, and have since the MIGRATIONS-FROM-SCRATCH rebuild made every demo the literal output of one unmodified `kiosk:install` — a uniformity `check-demo-copies` now enforces, because per-demo subsets are how the fleet drifted in the first place. The three sentences are corrected rather than the schema re-pruned: what makes these two demos non-commercial is the absent `pay` route, the absent PSP and `pay` absent from every advertised surface, none of which the presence of an empty table touches. Intent: "no payments here" should name the things that would take money, not the tables that sit empty.

- 2026-08-22: **a descriptor schema may be derived from the operator's own data, and the catalog refreshes without a deploy (K-922 / K-919, Phil).** A closed set whose domain IS a table — a classifieds board's sections — was written out by hand, so an operator who added one got a schema that refused it until someone redeployed; Phil's ruling is that «каталог должен обновляться динамически, без деплоя», because deploying to publish a listing or put fresh bread on a shelf is absurd. Any part of `input_schema`, `output_schema`, `example_params` or `example_row` may now be a proc, called when the catalog is SERVED rather than when the class body is read (which is `db:create` too), memoized for a minute against the per-request validation path, and re-resolved after it — so the served enum, the `400` that names it and the `?v=` version on `schema_url` all follow the data. The fill is mutex-guarded because Puma is multi-threaded and an unlocked memo runs the query once per racing thread and can publish two different answers in one window; philslist's two `category_slug` enums are the first adopters. Intent: a schema should state what the operator's surface actually accepts today, and nothing about adding a row should require a restart.

- 2026-08-22: **philslist's open board loses its `status` filter, and its keyword search stops answering a different question (Phil's correction; K-914, K-915).** `browse_listings` IS the open board, so a caller-facing status knob could only ask for something the board does not serve — it is removed rather than kept and explained, and "did my listing sell" stays `my_listings`' question to answer under its own contract. In the same file the keyword search now escapes LIKE metacharacters, so a human searching "50% off" is answered that question instead of every title beginning "50"; this was never SQL injection — the value is adapter-quoted — and the comment claiming no caller value is ever spliced into SQL is corrected to what is actually true rather than deleted. Intent: a filter that quietly changes the question is worse than a refusal, whether the reinterpretation happens in a clamp or in an escaping layer.

- 2026-08-22: **the `Kiosk::Query` / `Kiosk::Action` split is removed — one controller may declare both (K-921, Phil).** A controller carried a KIND, so a resource an operator thinks of as one thing — a board you browse and post to — had to be two classes for a reason the domain does not have. There is now ONE mixin, `Kiosk::Handler`, and a per-declaration `kind :query` / `kind :action` macro that says which verb reaches each method; it is required rather than defaulted, because either default silently picks an HTTP method for a handler. The class-level "this one provably cannot write" property is deferred, not lost: it returns as an opt-in `read_only!` if it is ever wanted, which states the guarantee instead of implying it from an include. Nothing on the wire moves — same verbs, same methods, same catalog — and the e2e origin now proves the new shape by serving `my_appointments` and `book_appointment` from one controller. Intent: the framework should split a controller when the operator's domain does, never because the framework does.

- 2026-08-22: **the `rel="kiosk"` discovery signal names the versioned cut an operator pins, not the mutable `skill.md` alias (K-927).** All seven demos advertised the alias in the home page `<link>` and in the matching `Link:` header, so the one artefact an operator and kiosk.tech share was the one carrying no version and no digest — while the `/.well-known/kiosk.json` pin beside it was versioned, hash-pinned and dual-checked. Both signals now read `Kiosk.configuration.skill_url`, the same accessor the pin is rendered from, so an origin advertises exactly one skill and re-pinning stays one edit in the initializer; `bin/check-version-parity` holds the source and each demo's `demo:schema` holds the served response. Intent: a discovery signal an assistant follows must be as pinnable as the pin next to it.

- 2026-08-22: **`POST /auth/unlink` answers `204 No Content` (K-870).** It rendered `{ok: true}` — a body no published surface documented, on an endpoint whose two siblings in the same ceremony have their responses spelled out in the spec. The body is withdrawn rather than written down after the fact, the same answer K-855 got, and the spec now states the 204 beside `/auth/link` and `/auth/claim`. Intent: an undocumented body on a published endpoint is a contract nobody agreed to, and the cheapest true fix is to stop sending it.

- 2026-08-21: **ROADMAP.md is measured against the tree instead of describing a wire that was deleted in 0.4 (K-925).** The public roadmap still validated a body `pow` field (a header since ADR-0022) and named `query`/`run` bodies, and it listed as v0.5 four things that shipped: unconditional per-verb `input_schema` validation, the `405` with `Allow`, the CI conformance run over live response bytes, and the vendored-schema sync-check. The whole file was re-verified bullet by bullet, which also caught three claims in the other direction — the KYC broker self-asserts rather than proving account-possession, the JWKS publishes exactly one key so there is no rotation overlap to publish into, and the classifieds board has no contact channel at all. Intent: a roadmap that lists shipped work as future undersells the release and overstates the rest, and it is linked from the public README.

- 2026-08-21: **ONE REGISTRATION PROOF NO LONGER BUYS UNLIMITED KYC BROKER INTAKES (K-586).** `request_kyc` was metered by nothing on either KYC demo — getgrocery's reputation policy challenges `:query` only, skooti configures none at all, both price registration at one proof, and the request table carried no per-principal bound — so a single agent could open verifications for as long as it cared to. Harmless against a broker stub that bills nothing, a direct budget hole the day a paid issuer sits behind prove.my. At most three verifications may now be PENDING per account, counted through the same isolation predicate `kyc_status` reads with and checked BEFORE the broker is called; the cap is self-clearing because an approved or declined request stops counting. Intent: the lever that bounds abuse of a third-party intake is a cap on what is outstanding, not a fee — and a refusal that has already cost an intake is not a cap.

- 2026-08-21: **A REPLAYED `pay` ON AN ALREADY-SETTLED CART IS IDEMPOTENT (K-850, ADR-0026).** §11.6 requires an assistant whose `pay` response was lost to re-send the identical chain, and the engine answered that duty with `409 conflict` carrying no settlement at all — so the safe retry was also the uninformative one, and every operator was implicitly required to publish a per-user paid-state query for the assistant to go and reconcile against. A replay of the same chain, byte for byte, whose cart already settled now returns that settlement; a chain re-presented with different content, or one whose cart never captured or is still in flight, keeps the `409` before any capture. Intent: an error status is not idempotence — it is a different answer to the same request.

- 2026-08-21: **EVERY SKILL CONSUMER RE-PINNED TO `skill-v0.4.3.md` (K-847).** The seven demo initializers and the engine default now advertise the cut that carries the idempotent-replay guidance, the widened operator-text-is-data surface list and K-835's unlink note; re-pinning is part of the skill change rather than follow-up, because until it happens no operator serves the text that was written. Intent: an operator advertises the skill it was actually built against, and the pin is the only thing that makes that checkable.

- 2026-08-21: **THE SHARED AUTH-CHALLENGE STORE §15.2 REQUIRES NOW EXISTS (K-751).** The spec puts the server-held auth nonces under the same sharing requirement as the PoW spent-id set, and the reference shipped only the in-process default — so an operator who scaled past one worker got a handshake that succeeded roughly 1/N of the time, surfacing to the assistant as an unexplained rejection of a correctly-signed request. `AuthChallengeStores::ActiveRecord` and `SchemaDefinitions.auth_challenge_sql` are the sibling of K-738's pair, down to the decisions: ActiveRecord because it is already a hard dependency, `take` as ONE atomic `DELETE … RETURNING` so the row and not Ruby picks the winner, and no eleventh canonical migration — the table is added deliberately when an operator scales out. Intent: a normative sentence with no referent implementation is a requirement the reference does not itself meet.

- 2026-08-21: **THE ENGINE'S OWN API DOCS STOP TEACHING A DESCRIPTOR SHAPE THE SPEC WITHDREW (K-598, K-671, K-680).** `Queries`, `Actions` and `HandlerMixin` told an operator — in the very files an adopter reads to learn the macros — that the descriptor fields were "all OPTIONAL" and that `output_schema`'s composition would be settled later, while 0.4 makes `input_schema` and `output_schema` REQUIRED on every verb, the published schema lists both in `required`, and the mixin itself has raised at class-body load for a missing one since T-068. The three rows were one cluster and the two public surfaces had already moved; this is the third. Intent: the gem's documentation is a claim, and a claim the shipped code contradicts costs an adopter a debugging session.

- 2026-08-21: **A PROVIDER THAT BUMPS `min_client` NOW SAYS SO EVERYWHERE IT PUBLISHES IT (K-747).** `config.min_client` drove `/.well-known/kiosk.json` and was ignored by the `Kiosk-Min-Client` response header, which emitted the protocol constant — so setting it produced an origin whose discovery document and whose every wire response disagreed about the same advisory number, with nothing to tell a client which was authoritative. The header reads the configured value; the default is unchanged, because the setter defaults to that constant. Intent: a configuration knob that works on one of the two surfaces it names is worse than no knob.

- 2026-08-21: **AN EMPTY CART IS NOT A CART (K-857).** K-741 made `line_items` REQUIRED on a cart mandate and deliberately said nothing about its contents, so `[]` satisfied the schema, the verifier and the presence check while carrying exactly as much reconciliation value as the omission K-741 was filed against — with `total_amount_cents` already bounded at `minimum: 1`, that is a positive charge with nothing itemised under it. The verifier refuses it, §11.2 says so normatively, and `mandates.schema.json` states `minItems: 1`; the three move together so no surface accepts what another refuses. Intent: presence was never the constraint — the constraint is that a settlement can be matched back to what was bought.

- 2026-08-21: **A REBIND'S PRE-LINK TOKENS NOW REALLY STOP VERIFYING, INCLUDING THE ONES MINTED IN THE REBIND'S OWN SECOND (K-836).** §6.3 says a claim rebind MUST watermark-revoke the key's pre-link tokens exactly as unlink does, and the second-resolution `iat` plus the store's strict `iat < watermark` left every token minted in that one wall-clock second verifying for its full remaining lifetime — measured 3/3 against a booted demo. Stamping the next second the way `unlink` does closes it and breaks something else: a rebind hands back a replacement token and §6.3 names `/auth/login` as a second way back in, and both would then be born revoked. So the rule moved OFF the caller and into the bundled IdP, which now dates every mint at the agent's watermark whenever one is ahead of the clock — a token issued after a revocation can no longer be issued already-revoked, whichever endpoint issued it. tudu's link driver drops the 1.1s sleep it used to SKIP the hard case with and forces it instead: align to a second boundary, mint one more pre-link token, claim in that same second, assert that token dies while the re-login lives. Intent: a MUST that holds except for one second is not the MUST the spec states.

- 2026-08-21: **`/auth/claim` stops shipping a field no published surface documents (K-855).** The controller rendered `AccountBinding.bind!`'s result hash whole, so an internal fresh-key-vs-rebind flag rode the wire on every claim while `protocol.md` §6.2 spells the response out as three fields and §6.3 requires that a rebind be indistinguishable from any other rebind. The response is now an explicit allow-list of those three. Intent: the wire carries what the spec says it carries, and the next field added to a service result has to be put there deliberately rather than arrive there.

- 2026-08-21: **ONE PROOF STOPS HAVING INFINITELY MANY SPELLINGS ON THE WIRE (K-842).** `header_nonce` is a u32 in both specs, but neither the Equihash verifier nor `pow.schema.json` bounded it — and the verifier folds it in with `pack("V")`, which truncates mod 2**32 rather than raising, so `0`, `2**32` and `-(2**32)` seeded identically and all three verified true against one set of indices. The verifier now refuses an out-of-range value in its cheap pre-check block and the schema states the same inclusive bound, in both copies. Intent: a proof-of-work identifier that admits aliases is not an identifier, and the schema must refuse exactly what the verifier refuses — no more and no less.

- 2026-08-21: **SIX DRIVERS STOP WRITING DOWN THE ANSWER THEY WERE SUPPOSED TO MEASURE (K-707).** `http_register: 201` was a literal in four flow drivers — three of them next to a comment explaining that `equihash_register` aborts unless the server returns 201, which explains the constant without making it a measurement — and three rake tasks "asserted" those literals against the same number, so the assertion line could only ever pass. `equihash_register` now returns the register response CODE as a third element (every caller that binds two is unaffected), the drivers report what the server answered, and skooti's `kyc_flow` reports the reserve and pay codes it used to discard entirely behind a constant `200`. Measured: making the helper report 200 turns hoteling's `demo:book` red, where the same change was previously invisible. Intent: evidence a driver typed is not evidence, and a gate that cannot fail is not a gate. (K-707)

- 2026-08-21: **TEN STATEMENTS BESIDE DEMO CODE THAT THE CODE DID NOT HONOUR (K-712).** One class, one sweep: `demo:setup` headers that named a command their body did not run — tudu's ran `db:migrate` under `schema_format = :sql`, which RE-DUMPS the tracked `db/structure.sql` and dirtied the worktree on every run; two `db:migrate` fallbacks branching on a `db/structure.sql` that is tracked in all eight demos, so the arm was unreachable in every checkout; a Stripe-key ternary whose "empty" branch had already been filled in twelve lines above; skooti's `demo:rideflow` RUN 4, the same call and the same assertion as RUN 2 with the labels changed, buying a second server boot and a second Equihash registration for a property already proved; tudu's and philslist's boot helper aborting on a readiness failure with the spawned `rails s` still running and its pid known to nobody; three `STATUSES` constants that named a set no `validates` and no CHECK constraint enforced; stylish's staff-role migration contradicting itself six lines apart and its `User#kiosk_role` promising a result "always one of `Kiosk.configuration.roles`" while returning a bare varchar column verbatim — now fail-closed to the least-privileged role, which is the only safe direction for an authorization input. Intent: in the code an adopter copies, a sentence next to a command is read as true. (K-712)

- 2026-08-21: **THE TOY BAD-PROOF COUNTER STOPS SHIPPING A `/tmp` PATH IN CONFIGURATION AND STOPS TRUNCATING ITSELF AT BOOT — AND ITS TWO READERS CAN NO LONGER DRIFT ONTO DIFFERENT FILES (K-785, K-711, K-707).** atablefor's and getgrocery's initializers — the file an adopter copies — hardcoded `/tmp/kiosk-<demo>-bad-proof.sqlite3` and wiped it on every process start, while `script/pow_flow.rb` re-typed the same literal independently; on a drift the driver opened an empty sqlite, read 0 for every count, and getgrocery's driver PRINTED the zeros as a pass because it asserted only the innocent identity's half. `rake demo:pow` now owns the location, wipes it for the run and exports it to both the server and the driver, the initializers keep only a `Rails.root.join("tmp", …)` default for a bare `rails s`, and getgrocery's driver asserts BOTH halves of the per-identity property and derives `served` from the live response instead of reporting a constant the rake task then 'checks'. Measured: pointing the driver at a different store now fails loudly and names it, where it used to exit 0. Intent: a demo must not teach an operator to write a wipe-on-boot fixture into production config, and evidence a driver hardcodes is not evidence. (K-785, K-711, K-707 partial)

- 2026-08-21: **THE LAST THREE FLOW-ONLY HELPERS LEAVE THE DEMOS' RAILS LOAD PATH, AND EVERY `ignore:` LIST IS NOW EMPTY (K-856, finishing K-659).** `devise_session.rb` (7 copies), `bound_assistant.rb` (4) and skooti's `rental_token_issuer_kat.rb` sat in `lib/` — eager-loaded by `rails server`, where nothing uses them — and were then hand-excluded from that load path one filename at a time, so every demo carried a list of exceptions to a rule it did not need. They are consumed only by `script/*` drivers and by each other, so they move next to their callers and all seven `autoload_lib` calls read `%w[assets tasks]`, the generator's own default. K-659's transitional wart goes with them: the four `bound_assistant` copies reached back across the boundary with `require_relative "../script/equihash_register"`, which was worse than either the before or the after and existed only because the file could not move. Intent: an adopter copying a demo should not learn that test drivers belong in the application's load path. (K-856)

- 2026-08-21: **A PoW GATE NO LONGER MINTS A CHALLENGE NOTHING COULD SOLVE (K-843).** K-840 taught the Equihash verifier to answer `false` for a degenerate `(n, k)` instead of crashing or accepting a vacuous proof — closed, but at the wrong end: the challenge was still issued, an honest client still burned a real Wagner solve on it, and the 403 it got back blamed a proof that was correct while the operator whose configuration caused it heard nothing. A backend may now answer whether a difficulty is satisfiable at all, and both gates ask before they mint: an unsatisfiable `registration_pow_params`, or a policy returning one, is a `ConfigurationError` naming the setting, the way a missing `pow_secret` already is. The predicate is the verifier's own Step 0, so the two cannot come to disagree, and a backend that has no opinion about its parameters keeps none. Intent: a misconfiguration must fail where it was made, not on the client that obeyed it. (K-843)

- 2026-08-21: **skooti's App Clip stops advertising a domain the project does not own (K-719).** Two `.entitlements`, two Swift sources and the QR minter all named `skooti.app` while `appclip/README.md` taught `skooti.demo.kiosk.tech` throughout — and the QR half was not inert, since `bin/make-qr` produces a code a human physically scans, aimed at a host that does not resolve. Every site now names the demo host the README already documents as the production path, and the two entitlements say in place what that domain still owes (an `apple-app-site-association` file and a real team id) and that the Local Experience path documented next door does not consult the key at all. Intent: shipped code should not point a scanner at a name nobody serves. (K-719)

- 2026-08-21: **THE POLL BUDGET THE OUT-OF-BAND VERBS PUBLISH IS NOW ASSERTED BY SOMETHING THAT RUNS, AND skooti's `kyc_status` FINALLY CARRIES ONE (K-606).** The wire has no server→assistant push, so completion of a hosted card-setup or identity check is learned by re-polling and nothing else; K-477/K-595 wrote a cadence and a give-up horizon into those descriptors and K-605 made them quote the published skill's tiered schedule — and then nothing read either back, so the promise lived only in the source it was written into. `demo:schema` now fails on a served `payment_setup`/`kyc_status` description that has lost its tiering or its horizon, in all three demos that publish them, and skooti's `kyc_status` — the one descriptor the K-595 port had missed — gains the same wording its sibling has carried since. The gate asserts STRUCTURE, not the minutes, so it does not become a third place the schedule is written down. Intent: an instruction an agent is expected to obey should not be able to vanish with every gate still green. (K-606)

- 2026-08-21: **atablefor's `book_table` REFUSES A SEATING ITS OWN `availability` NEVER OFFERED (K-767).** The verb re-validated only that a chosen seating had not already STARTED, so a well-formed date beyond the rolling two-day horizon passed every guard and CONFIRMED a booking — measured at head, `date: "2036-08-18"` returned `200` with a `booking_id` for a table ten years out, held against a real restaurant by a UNIQUE index. It now re-validates horizon MEMBERSHIP through the same helper `availability` filters its own `date` argument with, so the read surface and the write surface cannot come to disagree about what is bookable, and the refusal NAMES the dates that are. A redteam beat pins it, with the legitimate booking as its positive control. Intent: a booking surface must not confirm what the discovery surface never offered. (K-767)

- 2026-08-21: **A PAYMENT-FREE ORIGIN STOPS ASKING FOR MANDATES IT COULD NEVER SETTLE (K-800).** `pay` is drawn on every mounted host and the engine's own route comment promised that a host with no `payment_provider` "answers it with the wire's own 403" — but the three mandate-argument guards ran first, so an empty `POST /kiosk/pay` at atablefor came back `400 args.intent_mandate_jws required`: an instruction to go and sign an AP2 chain, issued by an origin that has no way to charge anything. Whether an origin does payments at all is a fact about the ORIGIN, so it is now answered before anything about the request is read, and discovery's honest `capabilities` (which already drops `pay`) and the endpoint's own answer finally agree. Intent: the least informative answer should not win a race against the true one. (K-800)

- 2026-08-21: **THE DEMOS' FLOW-DRIVER HELPERS LEAVE THE RAILS APPLICATION ENTIRELY (K-659, K-663).** `equihash_register.rb` sat in `lib/` in all seven demos and was then hand-excluded from `config.autoload_lib(ignore: …)` in all seven — a CI-only helper loaded by `rails server` and then told not to be — and `prove_broker_boot.rb`, which shells out and spawns a second Rails app, was not even excluded, so every production boot of getgrocery and skooti eager-loaded a test harness. Both now live in `script/` beside the drivers that are their only callers, reached by an explicit require and by nothing else; six of the seven `ignore:` lists shrank accordingly. Phil's condition was one sentence and it is now structurally true rather than maintained by hand. The broker helper's monorepo-only sibling path is documented in its header instead of being silently assumed — accepted, because the demos are not packaged gems. Intent: a file that must be excluded from the application does not belong in the application. (K-659, K-663)

- 2026-08-21: **AN OPERATOR WHO SCALES PAST ONE PROCESS IS NOW TOLD WHAT THAT COSTS — IN THE INITIALIZER THEY ALREADY HAVE OPEN, NOT ONLY IN A README THEY MAY NEVER OPEN (K-752, Phil's option C).** The in-process default PoW spent store makes proof single-use hold PER PROCESS, which the spec calls non-conforming, and the violation is invisible by construction: a replayed proof raises nothing, logs nothing and fails nothing. A fail-closed boot was considered and REJECTED — it would turn a routine `WEB_CONCURRENCY` 1→2 into an outage, and a process-count check cannot see the case that matters at all, since on separate machines every process boots with a count of one while the requirement is violated exactly as hard. So the remedy is documentation plus one honest boot warning: the install-generator template and all seven demo initializers now carry the requirement AND the reason it cannot be discovered by observation, and kiosk-server logs a `warn` at boot when a production origin has PoW on and the default store still in place. Intent: the one MUST in the document whose breach the counterparty cannot detect should at least be unmissable to the operator. (K-752)

- 2026-08-21: **A CART MANDATE WITHOUT `line_items` IS NO LONGER A VALID CART (K-741, decision LINE-ITEMS-REQUIRED).** The field was OPTIONAL on the wire while the settlement and reconciliation path already read it — the demos' `my_orders` join and the `line_items @> …::jsonb` replace-guard (K-544, which the K-545 pay-race fix stands on) both look inside it — so an assistant could omit it, pay successfully, and leave the operator holding a capture it cannot match to any domain object, with nothing raised anywhere. `MandateVerifier.verify_cart` now refuses an absent, null or non-array `line_items` the same way it already refuses an absent amount or currency, and the vendored e2e schema moves with the normative one. Measured while landing it: every demo flow driver, every e2e fixture and kiosk-redteam's profile carts already sent the field, so nothing in the fleet had to change — the gap was purely that the wire permitted what no operator could actually reconcile. Intent: a money path must not accept an authorisation that its own audit trail cannot read back. (K-741)

- 2026-08-21: **kiosk-rls STOPS ASKING ITS ADOPTERS TO MONKEY-PATCH RAILS FOR IT (K-504).** `ActiveRecord::Migration.include(Kiosk::RLS::DSL)` sat in four demo initializers in four hand-written variants and in the gem's README as the documented way to adopt it — an application patching a framework class on a gem's behalf, at a load-order position nothing verified. The gem now injects the five migration verbs from its own `Kiosk::RLS::Railtie`, which is the extension point Rails provides for exactly this and what every comparable DDL-verb gem uses; the four initializer lines and the README's instruction are deleted, and the gem gained no Rails RUNTIME dependency doing it (the railtie is required only when `Rails::Railtie` is defined, and railties/activerecord are test-only Gemfile entries). Measured while removing it: not one migration in the tree ever called a verb the include provided, so all four copies were dead — the one place RLS is actually exercised, getgrocery's `demo:rls`, drives `Kiosk::RLS::Emitter` from a script. Pinned from both sides: `bin/check-demo-copies` now refuses ANY Rails-framework patch from a demo initializer, and `demo:rls` aborts under a real boot if the railtie did not put the DSL on `ActiveRecord::Migration`. Intent: an adopter should not have to carry a line they did not author for a capability they do not own. (K-504)

- 2026-08-21: **THE WHOLE DEMO FLEET'S DESCRIPTIONS STOP CARRYING ARGUMENT NAMES, AND THE LINT THAT MISSED IT IS WIDENED TO THE RULE ADR-0023 ACTUALLY STATES (K-852).** K-846 fixed the two atablefor descriptors that wrote a literal `params:` list and left the lint checking for that WORD — so the same duplication carried on in every other spelling: 32 descriptions across all seven demos named a param their own `input_schema` already declared, as a "pass it to X as `y`" tail, a row→field list, or a literal call signature. Every one is now semantics only — what the verb means, when to reach for it, what its refusals are, and the follow-on VERB by name, which is the sanctioned form — with the structural half left where both ends are declared and checkable. Nothing was lost in the move: each schema was read before its sentence was deleted. The lint now fails on the property §Decision 1 states (a description may not utter a name some verb's `input_schema` declares) rather than on the retired field's spelling, and its extractor stopped skipping NESTED schema properties, which its own doc-comment had claimed to read for months. The K-494 cross-reference resolution is KEPT beside it rather than retired: the prohibition sees only DECLARED names, and the live-wire bug that started all of this was an UNDECLARED one — a row field routed as an argument. Intent: prose that states a fact the schema also states is a second source that drifts, and a lint that checks the correctness of a forbidden construct is green on the violation. (K-852; 32 offending descriptions → 0, measured with the lint's own extractor)

- 2026-08-21: **THE THREE PAYING DEMOS STOP DERIVING «PAID» FROM THE SETTLEMENT ROW ALONE, AND START ANCHORING IT TO THE CAPTURE (K-853, protocol.md §11.6).** hoteling and skooti had no payment lifecycle at all: the only record that money had moved was the engine's settlement row, written AFTER the irreversible charge — so inside that window `confirm_booking` and the rental verbs answered «no settlement for this booking/reservation» about something already paid for, and `my_bookings`/`my_reservations` published nothing about money whatsoever. That is the answer §11.6 forbids, because it is the one that licenses an assistant to sign a fresh mandate chain and charge its human a second time.
