Both demos now claim the row atomically BEFORE the capture (which alone makes a second capture impossible — neither had that either), flip it to paid the instant the capture RETURNS, and publish a `payment_state` of `unpaid | pending | paid` on their per-user query, with `pending` as the third state the spec requires. getgrocery had the claim and the flip already but still published a BOOLEAN, whose `false` conflated «nothing was charged» with «a charge is outstanding» — and its own prose still taught the retired «retry pay only if it is still unpaid» inference; it now publishes the same tri-state and says the honest thing. Pinned per demo by an in-process regression that actually stands in the window with a blocking PSP, so an assertion that could not fail was not an option. Intent: absence of a settlement record is not evidence that no money moved, and a reference operator that publishes it as one teaches every adopter to double-charge. (K-853)

- 2026-08-20: atablefor descriptors + the descriptor lint — **the retired `params:` field stopped being a field and carried on as prose, in the demo ADR-0023 was written about.** `book_table` and `availability` still listed their arguments, an eleven-field row list and a hand-written row→argument mapping in `description`, duplicating schemas that already declared all of it; both are now semantics only. The lint that was green on them is the other half: it checked that a param named in prose EXISTS — the correctness of a construct the ADR forbids — and its non-vacuity floor made writing a description correctly a test FAILURE. It now asserts the prohibition and guards that it can still read the descriptors, rather than that the descriptors still name params. (K-846)
- 2026-08-20: kiosk-server — **the `pay` UNKNOWN-outcome hint no longer tells an assistant that a missing settlement record means no money moved.** The engine records the settlement AFTER the capture, so an operator's own records show an already-charged cart as unpaid for the length of that window; the hint's old "retry only if it is still unpaid" turned exactly that window into a second charge. It now demands a positive, unambiguous "not paid" and says to stop and tell the human otherwise, matching protocol.md §11.6. (K-851)
- 2026-08-20: **THE ADVERSARIAL HARNESS CAN NOW ATTACK A TOLLED VERB — UNTIL NOW, PRICING A VERB TURNED OFF EVERY SCENARIO THAT TOUCHED IT (K-760).** K-736 stopped the harness LYING about a `pow_required` 402 (it had been scoring the toll as the attack's own gate firing) but left the capability gap underneath: the solve-and-resend branch lived inside `#register_raw`, so `#query`/`#run`/`#pay` could not pay a toll and a tolled verb could only be stalled around. That gap is not theoretical — measured against getgrocery with its catalog toll ON, the pre-fix battery ABORTED on its FIRST scenario with `catalog returned empty` and produced no verdicts at all, which is worse than the row that filed it claimed. All four verbs now share ONE bounded retry with registration: solve every issued challenge, re-send the identical request once, and hand back a re-demanded toll flagged as already-paid so the could-not-test verdict says which side the failure is on. With the toll on, getgrocery reports 17 BLOCKED / 3 SKIPPED / 0 BREACH — byte-identical to the untolled run — and hoteling's rate-priced browse gate answers 7 query 402s across the battery and still comes back clean. Intent: the payment model's documented shipped configuration is a tolled verb, and an adversarial suite that silently stops testing the moment an operator adopts it is worse than no suite. (K-760; no new breach surfaced by the new coverage)
- 2026-08-20: **THE u64 INDEX BOUND IS RESTATED INCLUSIVELY, BECAUSE THE EXCLUSIVE SPELLING REFUSED THE LARGEST LEGAL INDEX IN EVERY DOUBLE-PRECISION VALIDATOR (K-845).** K-839's `exclusiveMaximum: 2**64` is exact under Ruby's JSON parser and wrong under the JavaScript one most likely to be pointed at the copy published on kiosk.tech: it rounds `18446744073709551615` onto exactly `2**64` and then refuses it, so a third-party validator built off the normative schema would have rejected a proof this implementation accepts. Both copies now say `maximum: 2**64 - 1` — the same accepted set for an exact reader, and the largest legal value survives a lossy one because it is now the bound itself rather than a value compared against a rounded neighbour. Intent: a shape gate may be conservative about what it accepts, but it must never be the thing that refuses honest work. (K-845; academic at the shipped n=168 k=7, where an index is ~21 bits)
- 2026-08-20: **AN OUT-OF-RANGE PoW INDEX IS NOW A TYPED 400 AT THE SHAPE GATE INSTEAD OF A BURNED CHALLENGE AND A 403 (K-839).** The vendored normative PoW schema left `nonce.indices.items` unbounded while its own description said u64, so a proof carrying `idx + 2**64` — the same leaf under another spelling, since the verifier packs an index little-endian u64 — passed the shape check, atomically consumed the challenge id, and only then met the verifier's range pre-check and came back `403 invalid proof of work` with `on_bad_proof` charged against the agent. The schema now states the bound the description always claimed, so a malformed index is refused as what it is: a malformed request, before the gate. Intent: a shape gate that admits what the verifier refuses is not describing the wire, and the cost of that gap lands on the honest client that hit an encoding bug. (K-839; the normative copy on kiosk.tech moves in the same pass)
- 2026-08-20: **A WRONG PROOF STOPS COSTING WHAT A RIGHT ONE COSTS — THE UNAUTHENTICATED REGISTER TOLL IS NO LONGER THE CHEAPEST WAY TO BURN THE BOX'S CPU (K-540).** `POST /auth/register` runs the PoW gate before any key verification, so anyone could take a free 402 challenge and hand it back with a valid HMAC signature and garbage indices — and the Equihash verifier hashed all 128 leaves before checking anything, so that garbage cost exactly what a real solution cost: 18.7 ms measured at the shipped n=168 k=7, roughly 54 req/s to saturate the shipped single worker. The verifier now checks the indices structurally first (count, type, u64 range, distinctness, and the canonical subtree ordering that needs no hashing at all) and then folds the Wagner tree as it hashes, one leaf at a time, stopping at the first node that does not cancel: unordered rubbish is refused in 0.012 ms, well-ordered rubbish in 0.30 ms, and a valid proof still takes ~18 ms. The accepted set is byte-identical — only the moment of rejection moved — but the economics invert, because an attacker who wants the whole hash loop must now supply an almost-complete solution against a salt that is fresh per challenge, rather than 128 random numbers. The deploy runbook's measured numbers move with the code, and its conclusion deliberately does not: the edge rate-limit is still REQUIRED, because at `WEB_CONCURRENCY=1` a plain flood of any endpoint saturates the worker and nothing in the app bounds a request RATE. Intent: a toll whose verification is as expensive for rubbish as for gold is not a toll, it is a lever. (K-540; the operator half stays open — the live VPS has no edge limit)
- 2026-08-20: **THE AGENT SIDE STOPS ASSERTING ITS IDENTITY AND STARTS PROVING IT — EIGHT COPIES OF A CLEARTEXT AGENT-IdP DELETED, AND NOTHING REPLACES THEM (T-104, from K-660; the twin of T-066's human half).** Every demo shipped `StubIdp`, which turned `agent:u-<uuid>:a-<uuid>:r-<role>` into an authenticated identity AT ANY ROLE, and `JwtOrStubIdp`, which put a `Rails.env.local?` gate in front of it — so every driver, every isolation beat and the whole e2e suite ran as principals they had written down rather than earned. The replacement was not a promotion: the adapter was already in the gem. `Kiosk::Server::IdentityResolution` has always fallen back to `DefaultAgentIdp`, which verifies the very kiosk-pop JWTs `/auth/register`, `/auth/login` and the binding ceremony mint — and verifies them more strictly than the hand-copied composite did, which never checked `iss`. So every initializer LOSES its `c.agent_idp =` line instead of gaining one, and a reader copying a demo now learns that agent authentication needs no wiring at all. What made this a separate build from T-066 is that a PoW registration mints a NEW headless account while every stub bearer named a SEEDED human, so the drivers had to run the link/claim ceremony instead — which T-066 had only just made possible everywhere. `lib/bound_assistant.rb` is that ceremony, one shared file, the agent-side twin of `lib/devise_session.rb`; `agent_id` is now a uuid by construction rather than by convention, because `/auth/register` mints it (K-829/K-830). The coverage that existed to prove the parser was gated is replaced, not deleted, the way T-066 replaced its user-side twin: a spec asserting no demo can grow the parser back or override the seam, plus an engine-side assertion that the default refuses the forged bearer in EVERY environment — which is strictly stronger than an env gate on an arm that no longer exists. Intent: a demo is what a first adopter copies, and an example whose test drivers authenticate by naming themselves teaches that naming yourself is authentication. (K-660 closed; K-539's vulnerability class deleted with the code that carried it)
- 2026-08-20: **UNLINK NOW KILLS THE TOKEN THE ASSISTANT IS HOLDING, INCLUDING THE ONE MINTED IN THE SAME SECOND AS THE UNLINK (K-835).** The spec says twice, with no exception attached, that an unlinked key's tokens stop verifying — and they did, unless the assistant's token happened to carry the same one-second `iat` as the unlink, because the revocation watermark compares `iat < watermark` on second-resolution JWT timestamps. That case was not a one-second delay: unlink also 404s `/auth/login`, so the token that slipped through was the LAST one the key would ever hold, and it kept full read and write access to the HUMAN'S account for its whole hour (measured on a booted demo: still 200 at +65s). `unlink!` now stamps the next second, which it can do and `/auth/revoke` cannot, because unlink hands back no replacement token that must survive its own watermark. The store stays a dumb comparison — who is being revoked, and whether a replacement needs to live, is the caller's knowledge — and `skill.md` stops telling an assistant only that its next login will 404. Intent: a revocation a human performs is a promise made to that human, and «stops verifying» has to mean it without a footnote. (K-835; the same aperture on the claim rebind, where a replacement token does need to survive, is measured and filed as K-836)
- 2026-08-20: **EVERY DEMO NOW AUTHENTICATES ITS HUMANS WITH REAL DEVISE, AND THE CLEARTEXT USER-IdP STUB IS DELETED FLEET-WIDE (T-066, from K-660 — Phil: «а можно stylish и остальные переделать так чтобы тоже использовали devise?»).** getgrocery, hoteling and skooti shipped no human login at all, so the account-binding surfaces they route — device verify, link mint, unlink — were authenticated by a parser that turned a self-asserted `user:u-<uuid>` bearer into a human identity, gated to dev by a `Rails.env.local?` check; stylish additionally composed a role-carrying `X-Staff-Session` SSO stand-in in front of its real Devise session. All four are gone, together with the composite that existed only to hold two, and `c.user_idp = Kiosk::UserIdentityProviders::Devise.new` is now the SAME line in all seven operator demos and in the e2e adoption walkthrough — one channel, in every environment, with no dev-only arm to gate. Roles-from-IdP survives the deletion and reads better for it: the salon's role never came from the header, it came from the provider's own `staff_role` column, which `User#kiosk_role` hands the adapter. The drivers moved with it — a driver that needs the human half of a ceremony now signs in at `/users/sign_in` for real, through ONE shared `lib/devise_session.rb` rather than the five hand-copied cookie jars that preceded it — and the redteam beats that could only prove their block in-process, against a stubbed production `Rails.env`, became over-the-wire probes in the environment the suites actually drive, each with the real session as its positive control. Intent: the demos are what a first adopter copies, and an example that authenticates its humans with a string comparison teaches the string comparison. (K-660 → T-066; kiosk-test-support's env-gate spec for the deleted stub is replaced by one asserting no demo can grow it back)
- 2026-08-20: **THE AGENT-IdP SEAM STOPS ADVERTISING A PLUGGABILITY IT DOES NOT HAVE: `agent_id` MUST BE A UUID, AND NOW SAYS SO (K-830).** `AgentIdentityProviders::Base` invites an operator to front Entra / Okta / Passport-style issuers and `Kiosk::Identity` validates only that an agent's id is PRESENT — while every `agent_id` column the canonical schema creates, and the `kiosk.current_agent_id()` helper policies call, are typed `uuid` with no `user_id_type`-style knob to widen them. An adapter returning an opaque foreign id therefore built a perfectly valid Identity that failed five layers down, at a settlement or an RLS cast. The constraint is now stated where an implementer meets it: the `Identity` attribute doc, the adapter base class, kiosk-core's README, the install generator's own initializer comments, and the demo README that tells a reader to subclass the base — each naming the fix, which is to map the foreign identifier onto a local uuid inside the adapter. Documented rather than enforced: raising in `Identity` is a behaviour change to a value object every gem constructs, and it is not what a reader needed today. The shared-cache refusal message in `kiosk-server` moves in the same pass to match the amended §3.7.3, which now names all three RFC 9111 directives the code has always blocked. (K-826)
- 2026-08-20: **THE TWO STALE `rake demo` TRANSCRIPTS IN THE BEFORE/AFTER DOCS ARE RE-RECORDED FROM BOOTED DEMOS RATHER THAN HAND-PATCHED (K-817, K-818).** hoteling's block printed `using id=` where the script prints `using property_id=`, priced a room in `15000c` where it renders `€150.00`, and counted 5 properties against a seed file that lays down 100; getgrocery's showed an `http_schedule` step and a `scheduled_at` field the flow stopped emitting, and listed three assertions that no longer exist. Editing the values inside a recording would have produced a block that reads as freshly verified while never having been run — the worse failure, because the next reader cannot tell — so both were re-run and pasted verbatim, with the date of the run and, for getgrocery, an explicit statement that this is the secret-free `stripe-mock` path and that its `settled_amount_cents` is the fixture's constant zero. The surrounding prose is re-derived from the same runs. Two count-and-literal corrections ride along: the catalog returns **16** in-stock products and not 15 (the seed header carried the same off-by-one, and the row it loses is the age-gated wine the KYC beat depends on), and atablefor's two snippets declared `ActionController::API` where the shipped controllers subclass `ApplicationController` — which is also the base class the mixin's `rescue_from` seam expects. Intent: a document whose whole job is to show what the demo does must be checked against the demo, not against another document. (K-819, K-820)
- 2026-08-20: **THE KIOSK MIGRATION SET IS REBUILT FROM SCRATCH — SIX `create` MIGRATIONS, RENUMBERED 001-006, AND THE DEMOS ARE NOW LITERALLY GENERATOR OUTPUT (K-646, Phil).** «Поменяй шаблон генерации миграций и сделай миграции начисто в демо. прод базы можно дропнуть.» `kiosk:install` emitted ten ordinals of which one was retired, one created a `device_authorizations` table the next-but-two DROPPED and rebuilt, and three added columns to a table created two files earlier — so a fresh adopter ran a migration HISTORY to reach a schema the templates can state outright; each amendment is now folded into the `create` it amended, and all seven operator demos dropped their 46 kiosk-owned migrations for ONE `kiosk:install` run copied across at identical timestamps. Renumbering was MEASURED rather than assumed — eight live references to an old ordinal existed outside the rewrite, two of them already stale — and the demo databases were reset, which is what makes deleting a shipped history safe. The lasting half is the guard: `bin/check-demo-copies` loses BOTH of its template-drift escape hatches (all four exceptions recorded when the rule shipped were still open three months later, which is what a recordable exception actually buys) and gains a rule that every operator demo must ship every canonical migration, so a template that moves without its copies — or a demo scaffolded from a stale engine — reddens the build the day it happens. Intent: Phil could not see why the templates had diverged at all, and the answer was that nothing forced a re-emit and the check offered a way to write the divergence down instead of fixing it.
- 2026-08-20: **KYC ATTRIBUTES MOVE OUT OF A JSONB COLUMN INTO THEIR OWN TABLE, AND THE GRANT BECOMES A ROW'S EXISTENCE (K-656/T-061, decision KYC-ATTRIBUTES-TABLE).** A map had to carry a value and a value has spellings — JSON `true`, the string `"true"`, `1` — so every reader had to decide which ones count, which is precisely why both KYC-gated demos had independently pushed the boolean test into Postgres rather than trust a Ruby `== true` inside a gate that decides whether alcohol is sold or a motorcycle unlocks. `kiosk.kyc_attributes` has no value column at all: an attribute is granted iff its row exists, so a gate is an `EXISTS` that cannot answer NULL and cannot be fooled by a truthy-but-not-`true` value, and the single remaining spelling judgement lives on the WRITE, in Postgres, once for every operator. Proven on booted origins rather than argued: a genuinely broker-signed attestation spelling the booleans `"true"`/`1` is ACCEPTED as an attestation, grants nothing, and sends an agent that had just passed the gate back to 403 — in both demos. Intent: remove the ambiguity class instead of asking every reader to re-derive the same defence.
- 2026-08-20: **COURSE REVERSAL — KIOSK STOPS STORING AN AUDIT TRAIL AND STARTS OFFERING ONE (K-828, Phil).** Two days after the audit log landed, the direction changed: «Хранить в БД в рамках kiosk reference impl/demo не будем. Дадим интерфейс для возможности их куда-то выливать по желанию оператора, и на его ответственность по PII.» So `kiosk.actions` and `kiosk.action_log` leave the canonical migration set with their writer — canonical 003 is retired, the generator emits nine migrations, and every demo's schema drops both tables — and `c.audit_sink` replaces them: a callable the operator sets, receiving ONE event per action invocation, success and failure alike, with the same facts the columns held. Two things are deliberate and stated rather than discovered: the default is NO SINK, so an origin that does not ask emits nothing and stores nothing; and the ARGUMENTS ARRIVE IN FULL, unredacted, because a redaction Kiosk chose would be a retention policy Kiosk invented for somebody else's customers — redaction is offered at the seam (`with_arg_types`, `without_args`) and the README says in as many words that whatever an operator writes, they are the controller for. A sink that raises is logged and never fails the action. Intent: give the capability, keep none of the data, and put the PII decision where the responsibility already is. (K-828 supersedes the 2026-08-17 K-791 decision; T-088 reversed, T-102 is the new interface)
- 2026-08-20: **THE AUDIT LOG EVERY ADOPTER HAS CARRIED SINCE 0.1 IS FINALLY WRITTEN (T-088, K-791).** Canonical migration 003 lays `kiosk.actions` and `kiosk.action_log` down for every adopter — eleven columns, an FK, three indexes — and nothing in the tree ever read or wrote either, so an operator shipped an audit surface whose emptiness read as «no actions were invoked»; the engine now records one row per `run` invocation, SUCCESS OR FAILURE, satisfying the columns those tables already declared rather than redesigning them. Four things are decided in the open rather than left to a reader: a failed action logs (the `error_class`/`error_message` columns are meaningless otherwise, and the refusals are the interesting events), the row is written AFTER the action's transaction (so a rollback cannot erase the record of what rolled back, and a jammed audit table cannot fail a good booking), it is written OUTSIDE the identity-scoped session (the log crosses principals by design, and a row written with the caller's privileges is one the caller could suppress), and argument VALUES are WITHHELD by default — `args` records each argument's name and JSON type, with verbatim capture an explicit `audit_log_args = :full` the operator chooses. Intent: Phil's decision was that the audit capability is intended and the defect is that nothing writes it, and an audit trail that only records what succeeded is the wrong shape for the one dimension it exists to serve. (T-088; K-791 decided by Phil 2026-08-17; the `args` retention policy is filed for Phil as K-828)
- 2026-08-20: **THE DEMOS' `lib/` STOPS BEING A DUMPING GROUND FOR APPLICATION CODE, AND ALL 70 HAND-WRITTEN `require Rails.root.join("lib/…")` LINES GO (K-502).** Domain modules and value guards move to `app/models`, the adapters and service objects the initializer wires to `app/services`, and the telemetry cluster splits into three path-matching files so Zeitwerk can reach each constant by name; the e2e adoption walkthrough stages its four stubs the same way. The requires did NOT exist because "lib/ is not autoloaded" — `config.autoload_lib` already covered it — but because Rails sets the reloadable autoloader up AFTER `config/initializers` run, so `config.autoload_once_paths` is what replaces them, and it also stops a dev-mode reload swapping a class out from under the instance `Kiosk.configuration` is holding. Intent: the demos are what a first adopter copies, and a Rails-based reference whose example apps load their own code by absolute path teaches the workaround instead of the framework.
- 2026-08-20: **THE PUBLISHED JSON SCHEMAS NOW RUN AGAINST THE WIRE, NOT AGAINST HAND-WRITTEN EXAMPLES (K-822).** `e2e/run.sh` ends by validating the bytes a booted origin actually served — the discovery document, the catalog, four problem documents across four codes, the pay request it accepted, all three mandates' claims and the settlement it answered — against the normative schemas from kiosk.tech, vendored at `e2e/schemas/` and held against the published originals by a new `bin/check-spec-schemas`. Every check is paired with a mutated copy of those same bytes the schema MUST refuse, so a schema that quietly went vacuous fails instead of passing everything. It found two defects on its first run: the e2e origin's `my_appointments` and `book_appointment` descriptors published an `example_row` their own `output_schema` rejects — an integer id for a uuid column — which is corrected here (K-825). Intent: the schemas were checked against a fiction and the code against its own specs, and nothing ever joined the two, so the wire could drift from what implementers read with every suite green — measured: making a problem document's `status` a string leaves all 195 harness assertions passing and fails four schema checks.
- 2026-08-20: **THE BEFORE/AFTER SNIPPETS NOW RETURN WHAT THE CONTROLLERS RETURN (K-812).** The `output_schema` blocks T-068 slice 7 added to the three walkthrough demos were declared but never checked against the handlers they claim to show, so hoteling, getgrocery and atablefor published verbs whose rows carry a bare `id` where the shipped code renders `property_id`/`room_type_id`/`booking_id`/`order_id`/`delivery_slot_id`, invented `start_time`/`end_time`/`created_at` fields no verb has, and — worst of the set — an `additionalProperties: false` `book_table` that omits the `party_size` its own walkthrough sends and the shipped verb REQUIRES. Every field name, type and `required` list in every snippet is now the shipped one verbatim, the handler bodies build exactly the rows they declare and call the Operations that actually exist, and each snippet says in one sentence which shipped verbs and per-property descriptions it elides. Intent: the descriptor house style exists because a summary row that names an id differently from the verb consuming it forces a cold assistant to GUESS, and a reader copying our teaching snippet copies the guess.
- 2026-08-19: **THE PROSE CATCHES UP WITH THE WIRE — 45 FALSE STATEMENTS ACROSS THE READMEs, THE DEPLOY GUIDE, THE BEFORE/AFTER WALKTHROUGHS AND TWO LIVE RESPONSE BODIES (T-068 slice 7).** The 0.4 cutover changed the wire and the code with it; what it did not change was every sentence describing them, so the tree shipped a correct implementation wrapped in a retired explanation. Three of these were not documentation at all: the `/auth.md` EVERY operator serves taught the deleted `{ ok: false, error: {…} }` envelope instead of the RFC 9457 problem document a refusal actually is (K-808); the wrong-door signpost both human pages return to a JSON-dialing assistant pointed it at `schema/query/run/pay` endpoints, two of which have no route; and `deploy/README.md`'s copy-paste curl — the one a reader runs against a LIVE demo host — posted a `create_order` body that answers a typed 400, with a sku that is not seeded. The rest is teaching prose held to the same standard: `output_schema` is now stated as the protocol REQUIREMENT it became (fourteen verb declarations in the before/after docs had none, so a reader copying one got an app that would not boot), `input_schema` as unconditionally enforced, the toll's proof as riding the `Kiosk-PoW` header and not a `pow` body field, and philslist's and skooti's surfaces as the named endpoints their own routes serve. Intent: rule 1 says a README may claim only what the code demonstrably does, and a first adopter reads the prose before the code — a stale curl block is the most expensive kind of wrong, because it is executed. (T-068 slice 7; K-805, K-807, K-808 closed)

- 2026-08-19: **THE LAST GATED DESCRIPTION OF THE WIRE OPENS, AND THE POINTER DOCUMENTS EXPIRE IN A MINUTE (K-804, and Phil's `max-age` call)** — `GET <endpoint>/openapi.json` loses its Bearer gate and its toll and takes the caching `GET <endpoint>/schema` already had: `public`, a strong `ETag`, a `304` on `If-None-Match`, and a year at the `?v=<version>` url the api-catalog now links for BOTH descriptions. It is the same registry in another dress, so gating it while the catalogue, the api-catalog and every discovery surface stood open withheld nothing; the toll went with the gate because a toll is charged against an identity the endpoint no longer resolves, which leaves nothing tolled as `:schema` and collapses `Executor::POLICY_VERBS` into `VERBS` rather than leaving a constant that was a copy of another under a second name. Separately, every fixed-path public document — the bare `/kiosk/schema`, `/.well-known/kiosk.json`, `/.well-known/api-catalog`, `agents.json` — drops from `max-age=300` to `max-age=60`, and all six discovery documents stop emitting the `Vary: Accept` Rails stamps on a negotiated render. Intent: the number is the post-deploy staleness an operator must live with, not a load knob (the load is absorbed by the immutable versioned url), and a public document that varies on a header it never reads is one no shared cache will ever reuse. (K-804 and the `max-age` call, Phil 2026-08-19; spec §3, §4.1, §4.5, §4.6)
- 2026-08-19: **THE THREE-WAY STATUS RULE, APPLIED FLEET-WIDE (T-090)** — a bad argument now gets one of exactly three answers, and which one is decided by what the argument DOES: a value outside its domain is `400 bad_request` NAMING the valid values, a well-formed identifier that ADDRESSES a resource which does not exist is `404 not_found`, and a filter that matched nothing stays `200 []`. Five verbs move. hoteling's `hotel_detail` answers 404 again for an unknown `property_id` (K-794 removed it along with the object response shape; the ARRAY shape stays and only the empty-array-for-a-miss half is withdrawn) and `availability` joins it, so the two verbs that take the same argument stop disagreeing; getgrocery's `delivery_slots` refuses a date before today instead of returning the same empty list a fully-begun today returns; atablefor's `availability` refuses an unserved `neighborhood` naming the ones it serves; and philslist's `browse_listings` stops silently CLAMPING an out-of-enum `status` to "open" — the worst of the four, because it answered a different question and looked successful. Intent: an empty list, a refusal and a miss are three different facts, and an assistant that cannot tell them apart either retries forever or relays a confident wrong answer. (T-090, decided by Phil 2026-08-19; spec §9.1)
- 2026-08-19: **PAGINATION MOVES OFF THE BODY AND ONTO RFC 8288 (T-092)** — a paginating query now answers the SAME bare JSON array as every other query; the opaque next-page cursor travels as a `Link: <…?cursor=…>; rel="next"` response header (RFC 8288, Web Linking) and the count of matching rows as `X-Total-Count`, a de-facto convention we adopt by choice and do not dress up as a standard. The `{rows, next}` object is gone from the wire, from the one descriptor in the fleet that declared it, and from the shape table in both specs — a query answers an array, everything else answers its own object. Intent: pagination is transport metadata, and HTTP already has a place to put it; taking that place removes an entire response shape and the branch every assistant had to carry for it. (T-092, decision `PAGINATION-RFC8288`)
- 2026-08-19: **PROTOCOL 0.4 IS THE WIRE (T-068 slice 6, the cutover — T-074 = A)** — `POST <endpoint>/query` and `POST <endpoint>/run` are DELETED, with no tombstone and no compatibility mode: every verb is its own endpoint (a GET for a query with its arguments in the query string, a POST for an action with a JSON body), `schema` and `pay` and the auth plane leave the response envelope with them so there is one success shape (the payload verbatim) and one error shape (an RFC 9457 problem document) everywhere, the PoW fingerprint binds a proof to the method and the path segment rather than to a name smuggled into the arguments, and all fourteen gems, every inter-gem constraint and every operator's skill pin move to 0.4 in the same change. All seven operator demos and the e2e harness now serve and speak it; `json_schemer` becomes a real runtime dependency, because an origin that cannot validate a request against a declared `input_schema` cannot serve a conformant 0.4 wire at all. Intent: one wire is one conformance surface — carrying a second for a compatibility nobody is asking for would have doubled the redteam surface, the spec and the skill, permanently. (T-074 = A, T-072 = C, T-073 = A, ADR-0012)
- 2026-08-19: AN ORIGIN NOW DESCRIBES ITSELF ONCE (T-068 slice 5 of the 0.4 build) — `/.well-known/kiosk.json` advertises the MODULES an origin serves (`schema`, `queries`, `actions`, `pay`) instead of verb names, and `GET <endpoint>/schema` answers `verbs` with that same array instead of a constant that listed `pay` on origins with no payment provider. The two documents can no longer disagree, so the spec paragraph that told readers which of them to believe is deleted rather than rewritten. The public documents deliberately do NOT name the registered verbs: the catalog is Bearer-gated, the derived OpenAPI document is Bearer-gated, and the per-verb wire resolves identity before it resolves a name, so publishing the list in an unauthenticated GET would have undone all three — `agents.json`'s `x-kiosk` block stops echoing the capability list and carries pointers instead. Intent: what an assistant needs before it holds a token is which branches of its instructions apply, not a catalog it cannot call yet; the names are what it reads after registering, from the catalog, where they always belonged. (T-075 = A, K-740, ADR-0025)
- 2026-08-19: AN ORIGIN NOW DESCRIBES ITSELF TO GENERIC TOOLING TOO (T-068 slice 4 of the 0.4 build) — `GET <endpoint>/openapi.json` serves an OpenAPI 3.1 description of every verb the origin registers, so a porter can point a mock server, a request validator or a code generator at a Kiosk origin without first learning a bespoke catalog format; `/.well-known/api-catalog` advertises it as a second `service-desc` beside the canonical `schema`. It is a SECOND RENDERER over the SAME registry `GET <endpoint>/schema` renders — the shape the discovery documents already use so they cannot drift — so it holds no declaration of its own: a verb's prose semantics and both of its schemas travel into it verbatim, and a verb leaving the registry takes its endpoint with it. It is Bearer-gated and priced exactly as the catalog it re-renders, it is named nowhere in the skill so no assistant pays cold-start context for it, and it is PROVISIONAL by the decision that authorised it. Intent: 0.4's per-verb endpoints make the mapping to OpenAPI total rather than lossy, so the convenience is now free — but the assistant's contract stays the prose-and-schema catalog it always was, which is why this is an additional description surface and not a second contract (ADR-0024, narrowing ADR-0021).

- 2026-08-19: EVERY VERB NOW DECLARES BOTH OF ITS CONTRACTS, AND THE ENGINE CHECKS THEM (T-068 slice 3 of the 0.4 build) — all 52 verbs across the seven demos and the e2e origin publish an `output_schema` describing what they actually render, `input_schema` validation runs unconditionally on the per-verb wire instead of behind a flag, and a declaration missing either schema, carrying an illegal or reserved verb name, or colliding with the other kind is REFUSED at boot rather than discovered later. A new opt-in `validate_responses` — on in every demo and in the e2e origin, off by default — validates each answer against the schema that verb published, which is what turns a descriptor from a claim into an assertion; it caught a published field type that was wrong and a nullable column declared non-null on its first run. atablefor's `availability` stops answering an invalid `time` or `date` filter with an empty list and answers a `400 bad_request` naming the valid values (K-717), which is the house rule for every filter-shaped query in the fleet. Intent: with the response envelope gone, `output_schema` is the only machine-readable statement of what a call returns, and a contract nothing executes drifts from the handler the day after it is written.

- 2026-08-19: the per-verb wire STOPS WRAPPING its answers (T-068 slice 2 of the 0.4 build) — a query returns the handler's rows as a bare JSON array, a paginating one `{"rows":…,"next":…}`, an action its own object, so `render json: Product.in_stock` reaches the assistant unchanged and the `ok`/`kind` envelope that re-derived what the status line already said is gone. Errors on those endpoints become RFC 9457 problem documents under `application/problem+json`, with the closed error vocabulary surviving both as the problem `type` URI and as the `code` member an assistant branches on; the vocabulary gains `method_not_allowed`/405 with an `Allow` header, which is the honest answer when a verb that exists is called with the other method. Every wire response now carries the cache policy an identity-scoped, tolled resource needs (`Vary: Authorization, Kiosk-PoW`, `no-store` on a 402), so a private cache can no longer serve a paid 200 to an unpaid retry. `POST <endpoint>/{query,run}` and `<endpoint>/{schema,pay}` deliberately keep the 0.3 envelope until the demo fleet migrates in one wave; 0.4's cut remains hard and lands in the cutover slice.

- 2026-08-19: an origin now serves ONE ENDPOINT PER VERB (T-068 slice 1 of the 0.4 build) — a query is a `GET <endpoint>/<query-name>` carrying its arguments in the query string, an action a `POST <endpoint>/<action-name>` carrying them in a JSON body, so `curl -H "Authorization: Bearer …" https://…/kiosk/catalog` is the whole invocation and the HTTP method carries the read/write semantics the old wire spelled out in a `name` field. Arguments are decoded and typed from the verb's own `input_schema` before the handler sees them, and a value that cannot be that type is a `400 bad_request` naming the parameter rather than a filter that silently did nothing. The 0.3 `POST <endpoint>/{query,run}` endpoints are deliberately still served and byte-identical until the demos migrate; 0.4's cut remains hard and lands in the cutover slice.

- 2026-08-17: the write seam is fleet-wide and the human web UI stops borrowing the assistant's door (T-083, T-082) — atablefor, philslist and stylish gain `app/operations/`, joining tudu, skooti, getgrocery and hoteling, so all seven operator demos declare their writes the same way (`prove` registers no wire verbs and correctly has none); and tudu's human READ path, the last place in the fleet where a browser request travelled through the wire dispatcher, now reads model scopes directly — the pattern getgrocery's back office already proved, where the two surfaces share a model projection rather than a door. Splitting the refusal renderers out to `app/controllers/concerns/kiosk_refusals.rb` in every demo made a latent gap visible rather than creating one: tudu had shipped the same renderer pair inside `KioskMembershipGate` under a name only tudu used, and because the lockstep copy-check pairs files by relative PATH, that copy was being compared to nothing at all. `app/operations/operation_result.rb` now exists in seven copies and stays `:per_demo` deliberately — the `STATUSES` table is exactly the part that legitimately differs, in all seven copies rather than in one — so the "should the shared half be a gem" question is filed (K-792) rather than answered, with the manifest entry stating plainly that `:per_demo` means those shared ~40 lines are not compared by anything.
- 2026-08-17: kiosk-server's AUTH and BINDING plane stops building SQL by hand (K-782), so no gem file left in the engine concatenates a value into a statement: register, login, the claim/link/unlink ceremonies, the manage-assistants page, the KYC attestation, the device-authorization and PoW-spent stores and the agent-IdP's four lookups all carry bind parameters, `connection.quote` is unreachable from this gem, and every acquisition is `lease_connection` — the one Rails 8.1 does not soft-deprecate and does not raise under `permanent_connection_checkout = :disallowed`, which the auth plane could not afford to 500 on. Behaviour is unchanged and now provably so: the plane had no real-database coverage at all, so a Postgres-backed characterisation spec was written and run green against the interpolated code first. It immediately paid for itself — a provider that configures no `registration_role` cannot register any assistant, because the code writes `NULL` into a `NOT NULL` column and every existing test accepted it through a fake (filed, not fixed).
- 2026-08-17: re-linking an assistant to the human it is ALREADY bound to no longer fires `assistant_claimed` (K-783) — the hook is a notification that a key's holder CHANGED, and the engine was firing it on a re-bind that changed nothing, so a host acting on it acted on a lie. tudu, the only demo that implements the hook, was deleting every list membership the human held: she kept owning her lists and could reach none of them. The engine guard is the fix, tudu's hook guards the same condition anyway (a destructive hook that is safe only because its caller is careful is not safe), and nothing an assistant can observe on the wire changed.
- 2026-08-17: the last two hand-quoted SQL sites in the demo fleet are gone (K-781) — atablefor's `reputation_factors` and tudu's `assistant_claimed` are `Kiosk.configure` HOOKS, not verbs, so seven passes of handler migration walked straight past them; both read and write through their models now. The point is that "the demos write no raw SQL" becomes true of the whole app rather than only of the wire surface an audit keyed to handlers can see.
- 2026-08-17: kiosk-server's own pay path stops building SQL by hand (K-654, engine half): the four mandate/settlement INSERTs and the spending-cap tally now carry bind parameters, so no value is ever concatenated into a statement and there is no `connection.quote` left to forget — the engine had been shipping the exact idiom the seven demos were just converted away from. Behaviour is unchanged and now provably so: the helpers had zero coverage, so a real-Postgres spec was written and run green against the interpolated form first.
- 2026-08-17: `Kiosk::Server::Queries.register` and `Kiosk::Server::Actions.register` are REMOVED — a shipped public API of the 0.3 series is gone, and an operator's initializer that calls one now raises NoMethodError. Declaring a verb has exactly one shape: a controller that includes `Kiosk::Query` / `Kiosk::Action`, named in `c.handlers`. The second shape could not be reloaded, could not be reached by Rails' filters, `rescue_from` or strong parameters, and taught operators — in the very file an adopter copies — that Rails does not apply to the surface they expose to assistants. The wire is untouched: verb names, request and response shapes, error codes and every discovery document are byte-identical across all seven demos, including the retired `params` descriptor slot, which keeps publishing null.
- 2026-08-17: getgrocery — the LAST of the seven — moves to the final shape in one pass: its eight wire verbs become Rails controllers, its handlers stop writing SQL, and its three writes become Operations (T-057 slice 7 + K-654), leaving a 969-line initializer at 300 lines of configuration with `c.handlers = %w[Kiosk::StorefrontController Kiosk::OrdersController]` (four queries, four actions), both on `ActionController::API`. THE FLAGSHIP'S PAY-RACE GUARD WAS RE-PROVEN AS A LOCK, NOT ASSUMED: K-544's replace guard is now `Order.replaceable` + `CartMandate.referencing` + `.lock`, and holding the order row uncommitted on a second Postgres connection blocks the handler for the life of the lock and then answers identically (18 ms unblocked, 2.23 s blocked, same order_id, same total); with the settled cart's containment isolated from `status`, a paid order forced back to `created` still refuses to be replaced and its items stay untouched, which is the K-544 swap closed. The containment itself is written ONCE for the whole origin — value-bound as Arel in `CartMandate.referencing` for the three verb call sites and the pay path, correlated as a frozen `Arel.sql` literal in `Order` for the two that answer "paid?" for a whole list — and that second form is what makes the operator's back office at GET /admin/orders and the wire's `my_orders` ONE behaviour with two authorities (the caller passes the settlements it may read), where they were two hand-written copies of the same SQL. The alcohol age gate keeps its `kyc_attributes ->>` extraction in Postgres for the reason skooti recorded — the engine stores JSON booleans, so a Ruby comparison would accept one spelling of true and silently refuse the other inside a KYC gate — and `Product.age_restricted?` is now fail-closed and read by BOTH the shelf and the gate, so a catalogue row and the refusal cannot disagree. Four hostile shapes per verb that used to be 500s (`delivery_slot_id`/`qty` given `true`, `false`, an array or an object, where a bare `.to_i` raised NoMethodError) are typed 400s naming the same constraint a bad string already got. Stripe's credentials leave the initializer for `config/environments/*` as `Rails.configuration.x.kiosk.stripe_*` (K-700, the last ENV-CONFIG-PLACEMENT instance), so no initializer in the fleet branches on `Rails.env` any more. `register` now has exactly one consumer left in the tree: the e2e fixture, deliberately, because it is the merge gate.
- 2026-08-17: skooti's eight wire verbs become Rails controllers, stop writing SQL, and its four writes become Operations — one pass straight to the final shape (T-057 slice 6 + K-654), so a 778-line initializer is now 243 lines of configuration and the verbs are `Kiosk::FleetController` (three queries) and `Kiosk::RentalsController` (five actions), named in `c.handlers` because a verb registers only when its class loads and nothing loads a handler on its own (K-761); both inherit `ActionController::API` directly, which is hoteling's recorded decision applied to the demo it named next. THE GATE THIS DEMO EXISTS TO HOLD SURVIVED, AND WAS RE-PROVEN RATHER THAN ASSUMED: K-687's Gate 1b — `start_rental` refusing the KYC-gated motorcycle instead of handing an unlock token to an agent that attested nothing — is now `Scooter#licence_free?`, and `rent_motorcycle`'s Gate 2 is `Scooter#licence_required?`, ONE column read by two verbs in opposite directions so it is no longer possible to change one reading without the other; both keep K-724's `ActiveRecord::Type::Boolean` coercion and are fail-closed by construction, so a value that is neither literal true nor literal false opens neither door — "ActiveRecord already casts it" is the same assumption K-724 was filed on, in a new coat. Gate ORDER is unchanged and probed: ownership → vehicle kind → payment, so an agent that reserved the wrong vehicle is still told to change VERB before it is told to pay, and `rent_motorcycle`'s KYC gate still runs BEFORE the argument guards, so an un-attested caller gets `kyc_required` even for a missing `reservation_id`. `demo:redteam` is 23 BLOCKED / 0 SKIPPED / 0 BREACH with `MotorcycleViaStartRental` blocked (HTTP 400, no rental_token) and its licence-free positive control still 200. The KYC attribute test deliberately STAYS IN SQL as an Arel `->>` on `Agent.kyc_granted?`: the engine stores JSON booleans, so `->> 'age_over_18'` is the text "true" and so is a `"true"` string, while any Ruby comparison on the decoded jsonb accepts one spelling and silently refuses the other. The Ed25519 activation both rental verbs share is ONE `RentalActivation` — two call sites for the bytes `demo:kat` pins and three firmware/simulator copies reproduce is two places a byte can change, and a change made in one signs tokens no provisioned lock will open, for one vehicle class only; the caller's uuid spelling is signed verbatim rather than re-canonicalised off the row, because `UuidCheck` accepts either hex case and a conversion does not get to change what a signature covers (`demo:kat`: 35/35). The identity predicate did NOT move: `user_id = kiosk.current_user_id()` survives as `Reservation.owned_by_current_principal`, `Settlement.of_current_principal`, `KycVerificationRequest.owned_by_current_principal` and `Agent.acting` over frozen `Arel.sql` literals, so the app-layer predicate and an optional RLS policy stay the same expression (spec §7). Every write is `insert!`/`update_all` and never `create!`/`find_by!`, measured on these models rather than assumed: `Reservation.insert!` raises `InvalidForeignKey` (unmapped, so the 500 the raw INSERT gave) where `create!` raises `RecordInvalid` (422, which the mixin's `rescue_from` floor would render 400). POST /kyc/callback was CHECKED for shared behaviour and has none — it approves a request no verb can approve and looks its row up unscoped, because the broker is not a principal — so it shares the model and nothing else. Measured against a byte-diffed before/after capture — six discovery documents, the full schema, and 144 probes covering every happy path and every 4xx on all eight verbs, hostile argument shapes, the cross-principal and KYC-callback surfaces and DB ground truth (84 distinct response payloads; captured twice before and three times after, each side diffing to zero first) — with 139 probes identical and 5 deltas, every one accounted for: the schema document's forced change (`params`, retired by ADR-0023 with no macro, becomes null and `input_schema` appears where it was missing; descriptions, both existing input_schemas and both examples byte-identical, nothing enriched) and three K-765 spellings where a handler `.to_s`es an object-shaped argument and now gets `params`' string keys instead of the wire's symbol ones — same status, same code, message text only.
- 2026-08-17: hoteling's eight wire verbs become Rails controllers, stop writing SQL, and its two writes become Operations — one pass straight to the final shape (T-057 slice 5 + K-654), so a 723-line initializer is now 219 lines of configuration and the verbs are `Kiosk::HotelsController` (five queries) and `Kiosk::ReservationsController` (three actions), named in `c.handlers` because a verb registers only when its class loads and nothing loads a handler on its own (K-761). They inherit `ActionController::API` directly rather than an `ApplicationController`: hoteling is api_only with no Devise, so the base class the four earlier slices inherited exists in those demos only to carry a CSRF signpost for a page this one does not serve — skooti and getgrocery are shaped the same way and should follow. hoteling is the fleet's ONLY paginating verb and the first real exercise of the mixin's `render_kiosk_page`, which held: `search_hotels`'s `Page` + cursor round-trip is byte-identical through the new seam, and the correlated scalar subqueries it needs in three places at once (projection, `max_price_cents` filter, ORDER BY) are Arel on `Property` rather than a hand-written SELECT. The identity predicate deliberately did NOT move: `user_id = kiosk.current_user_id()` survives as `Booking.owned_by_current_principal` (and `Settlement.of_current_principal`) over a frozen `Arel.sql` literal, so the app-layer predicate and an optional RLS policy stay the same expression (spec §7). Both halves of K-690's double-booking guard were re-proven rather than assumed — the pre-check by serial probes and the redteam beat, and the `EXCLUDE USING gist` constraint by holding an uncommitted conflicting room-night on a second connection so the pre-check passes and the handler's `insert!` blocks on the index (measured: it waited 3.0 s, then answered the same 409 `conflict`) — with the abutting-nights positive control still 200, and K-698's confirmation code still written under `COALESCE` in the confirming UPDATE and read back from the row. Every write is `insert!`/`update_all` and never `create!`/`find_by!`, measured on this model rather than assumed: `insert!` raises `InvalidForeignKey` (unmapped, so 500) where `create!` raises `RecordInvalid` (422, which the mixin's `rescue_from` floor would render 400). Measured against a byte-diffed before/after capture — six discovery documents, the full schema, and 185 probes covering every happy path and every 4xx on all eight verbs, hostile argument shapes, the four pagination edges and DB ground truth, each side captured twice and diffing to zero first — with 142 probes identical and 43 deltas, ALL of one story: the shape check that used to be Postgres. `property_id: "abc"` and `check_in: "nope"` reached the database as `::integer`/`::date` casts and came back 500 `action_failed` with the PG message attached; ActiveRecord does not raise on junk, it CASTS (`"abc"` → 0, `true` → 1, so `true` would have quietly meant property 1), so the K-581/K-582 answer for uuids is now written for integers and dates too and those 41 probes are typed 400s with no SQL internals. The schema document's only change is the forced one: `params` (retired by ADR-0023, no macro) becomes null and `input_schema` appears where it was missing; descriptions and the two existing input_schemas are byte-identical.
- 2026-08-17: tudu's ten handlers stop writing SQL, and its six WRITES move out of the controller into an Operation each (K-654, the last migrated demo) — tudu is the one demo whose human web UI drives the same writes the agent wire drives, and while that logic lived in the handler the only way the web form could reach it was to dispatch a synthetic Rack sub-request at the wire handler; `app/operations/*_operation.rb` is now the seam both surfaces call, so `KioskSessionable` runs writes directly under the same GUC transaction and `kiosk_run` is gone with its last caller (reads stay in the query handlers — a scope plus a `pluck` has nothing to extract). Business logic deliberately did NOT go on the models: they keep persistence and query scoping, which is where the identity predicate stays as a `Membership.of_current_principal` scope over a frozen `Arel.sql` literal, so the app-layer predicate and an optional RLS policy remain literally the same expression (spec §7). The two database guarantees that gate invite redemption survive verbatim and were proven, not assumed: `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` is `Invite.lock` (two concurrent redemptions of one code, exactly one winner — without the lock both win), and `ON CONFLICT (list_id, account_id) DO NOTHING` is `insert_all(unique_by:)`, which has to be DO NOTHING rather than an upsert because the owner redeeming her own code would otherwise be DOWNGRADED to 'member' and silently stripped of her invite/remove authority. Every write is `insert!`/`update_all`/`delete_all`/`update_columns` and never `create!`/`update!`/`find_by!`, because tudu's `rescue_from` floor is inert today and each of those would wake it — `create!` turns Postgres' `InvalidForeignKey` (500) into `RecordInvalid` (400), `find_by!` turns a deliberately uninformative 403 into a 404 that tells a prober the code is unknown. Measured wire-identical against a byte-diffed before/after capture of five discovery documents, the full schema and 199 probes — every happy path and every 4xx on all ten verbs, hostile argument shapes, DB ground truth, and forty-odd probes of the human web UI including the four malformed-id paths where a refusal re-raises out of the controller — with ZERO deltas on both surfaces.
- 2026-08-17: atablefor's four handlers stop writing SQL and start using ActiveRecord (K-654) — this is the demo where the eight `conn.quote` calls actually guarded something, so the injection surface an operator would copy is what goes: `where_nbhd = "AND r.neighborhood = #{conn.quote(nbhd_filter)} "` concatenated into a SELECT, and an `IN (…)` list BUILT by joining quoted timestamps, both become ordinary conditions with bound values; `my_bookings` drops a `to_char(… AT TIME ZONE 'Europe/Lisbon')` that spelled the operator's zone a second time where a change to lib/seatings.rb could never reach it, and now reads `Seatings.zone`; and `cancel_booking`'s SELECT-then-UPDATE collapses into one owner-scoped `update_all` whose row count IS the answer, so the ownership test and the write can no longer be separated by another transaction. The identity predicate deliberately did NOT move: `user_id = kiosk.current_user_id()` survives as a `Booking.owned_by_current_principal` scope over a frozen `Arel.sql` literal, keeping the app-layer predicate and an optional RLS policy the same expression (spec §7). The double-booking 409's INSERT is `Booking.insert!` and NOT `create!`, deliberately: the `rescue ActiveRecord::RecordNotUnique` depends on which exception class arrives, and `create!` would interpose validations that turn a principal with no `users` row from Postgres' `InvalidForeignKey` into a `RecordInvalid` — a changed answer for an unrelated input. Both halves of that 409 were re-proven rather than assumed: the pre-check by a serial repeat, and the unique partial index by holding an uncommitted conflicting row on a second connection so the pre-check passes and the handler's INSERT blocks on the index (measured: it waited 2.5s, then answered 409 `conflict` with the same sentence the pre-check gives). Measured wire-identical against a byte-diffed before/after capture of six discovery documents, the full schema and 124 request probes across all four verbs, with ZERO deltas.

"TRUE" : "a.user_id = kiosk.current_user_id()"`, spliced into `execute`) — the idiom a reader copies into a query that DOES take caller input — and branches between two relations instead. Both GUC-borne assertions stay SQL-side for the pilot's reason: `Appointment.owned_by_current_principal` is a scope over a frozen `Arel.sql` literal and the role gate is `Appointment.current_principal_role`, so the app-layer predicate and an optional RLS policy remain literally the same expression (spec §7) and the handlers themselves now contain no SQL. `book_appointment`'s `SELECT kiosk.current_user_id()` becomes `kiosk_identity.user_id`, converging on the pilot's answer that an INSERT has no predicate to hide the principal in. Measured wire-identical against a byte-diffed before/after capture of six discovery documents, the full schema and 116 request probes (76 of them 200s, so every write happy path is exercised, not just the refusals) covering all six verbs plus hostile argument shapes, with ZERO deltas. Two pre-existing non-determinisms had to be neutralised before the harness was honest and are worth knowing about: `my_appointments` is `ORDER BY id` where `id` is a `gen_random_uuid()`, so the demo's own row order is not stable run to run, and `salon_calendar`'s `ORDER BY slot` has no tie-break.
- 2026-08-16: philslist's five handlers stop writing SQL and start using ActiveRecord (K-654 pilot, the third-party review's C2) — moving a handler into a controller did nothing about the idiom the review calls "the reference pattern the whole ecosystem copies", so the four hand-quoted statements the T-057 slice carried across verbatim are now ordinary relations, and the injection surface an operator would copy (`sql << " AND c.slug = #{conn.quote(...)}"`, an `UPDATE` assembled from `set_fragments`) is gone: filters are conditions, the keyword search is an Arel `matches`, the two owner-scoped UPDATEs are one `update_all` each, and the editable columns are a strong-params allowlist rather than a loop over caller keys. The identity predicate deliberately did NOT move: `owner_id = kiosk.current_user_id()` survives as a `Listing.owned_by_current_principal` scope over a frozen `Arel.sql` literal, so Postgres still reads the principal from the transaction GUC that `SET LOCAL` set — the trust boundary stays at kiosk-server's SessionContext instead of relocating into the handler, and the predicate stays written in the terms an RLS policy is written in (spec §7). Measured wire-identical against a byte-diffed before/after capture of six discovery documents, the full catalog and 97 probes covering every happy path and every 4xx on all five verbs, with ONE delta: `edit_listing` given an Array or Object where `input_schema` declares a string used to answer 500 `TypeError: can't quote Array` and now answers 200 with the value dropped, because `conn.quote`'s refusal has no ActiveRecord equivalent.
- 2026-08-16: tudu's ten wire verbs — the most of any demo — follow atablefor out of the initializer and into two Rails controllers (T-057 slice 4), and this is the slice that had to answer the question the mixin's "a controller declares queries OR actions, never both" rule poses: tudu's membership guard is called from two queries AND three actions, so it is split rather than duplicated — the access decision is `Membership.reachable?`, a model predicate with no request in it that a console or a model test can exercise, and the 400/403 refusal around it is one `KioskMembershipGate` concern both handler controllers include. `whoami`/`my_lists`/`list_todos`/`list_members` are `Kiosk::HouseholdController`, the six actions are `Kiosk::TodoListsController`, the ten `Errors::` raises become five `render json:, status:` refusals, and the initializer drops from 596 lines to 184 of configuration plus one `c.handlers` line. K-701 closes with it: the three top-level `def`s that monkeypatched `Object` are gone — the mixin's `kiosk_identity` carries the principal and acting agent a boot-time block had to fetch from the GUCs — and no demo initializer defines a top-level method any more; K-702's surviving wrong comment went with the code it mis-described. tudu is also the one demo whose human web UI runs the SAME handlers, so `KioskSessionable` now publishes the identity the way the wire does and reads a handler's answer as the rendered JSON it is. Measured wire-identical against a byte-diffed before/after capture of six discovery documents, the full catalog and 100 request probes covering every 4xx path and every happy path — plus a twenty-assertion web-UI probe that is unchanged too — with two delta classes and nothing else: the retired free-text `params` hint is gone (its contract lives in `input_schema`, which all ten verbs now publish), and eight sites render a caller-supplied object as `ActionController::Parameters` spells it rather than as a symbolized Ruby `Hash` did. The `rescue_from` floor changes no answer here either: tudu's handlers are raw SQL end to end, so despite its five ActiveRecord models they raise none of the exceptions Rails registers a status for.
- 2026-08-16: atablefor's four wire verbs follow stylish out of the initializer and into two Rails controllers (T-057 slice 3) — `availability` and `my_bookings` are `Kiosk::DiningRoomController`, `book_table` and `cancel_booking` are `Kiosk::BookingsController`, and the sixteen `Errors::BadRequest`/`Conflict`/`Forbidden` raises this demo carried (the most per verb of any) become five private `render json:, status:` refusals; the initializer keeps configuration and one `c.handlers` line, and K-691's `next []` — the workaround for a stored block where `return` raised LocalJumpError — becomes the plain `return` a controller method makes correct again. Measured wire-identical against a byte-diffed before/after capture of six discovery documents, the full catalog and 84 request probes covering every 4xx path, the double-booking 409 and the owner-scoped cancel, with two deltas and nothing else: the retired free-text `params` hint is gone (its contract lives in `input_schema`, which all four verbs now publish), and two 500 `action_failed` messages name `ActionController::Parameters` where they used to name `Hash`, because a handler now reads the wire args as Rails params. - 2026-08-16: stylish's six wire verbs follow philslist out of the initializer and into two Rails controllers (T-057 slice 2) — the five queries (including the role-gated `salon_calendar` forecast) are `Kiosk::FrontDeskController` and `book_appointment` is `Kiosk::AppointmentsController`, both with the class-level macros and, for the action, `render json:, status:` refusals in place of the five `Errors::BadRequest` raises; the initializer keeps configuration and one `c.handlers` line. Measured wire-identical against a byte-diffed before/after capture of the discovery documents, the full catalog and 40 request probes covering every 4xx path and both happy paths, with the two deltas the mixin forces and nothing else: the retired free-text `params` hint is gone (its contract lives in `input_schema`, which all six verbs now publish), and the `rescue_from` floor the include installs maps a Rails-registered raise to its status' wire code, so the one path that reaches `Appointment.create!` with an identity that has no `users` row answers 400 `bad_request` instead of a 500 whose message quoted `ActiveRecord::RecordInvalid`.
- 2026-08-16: philslist's five wire verbs move out of `config/initializers/kiosk.rb` and into two ordinary Rails controllers (T-057 pilot on K-495) — the demos shipped their whole operator-facing application layer as `Kiosk::Server::Queries.register("name") { … }` blocks in a boot-time initializer, which is the shape an operator copies, so what the reference teaches was "Rails does not apply here"; philslist's queries and actions are now `app/controllers/kiosk/{board,listings}_controller.rb` with `include Kiosk::Query` / `Kiosk::Action`, class-level descriptor macros and plain `render json:, status:` refusals, and the initializer holds configuration only. The wire is unchanged — same verbs, requests, responses, error codes and statuses, same discovery documents — with ONE deliberate descriptor change the mixin forces: the free-text `params` hint ADR-0023 retired is gone, and what it said now lives in `input_schema`, which all five verbs publish.
- 2026-08-16: the redteam harness stops reading HTTP 402 as "this attack was refused" (K-736) — `blocked?` admitted the bare status as an explicit auth/authz rejection, but `kiosk-server`'s wire contract shares 402 between THREE codes and refuses on its own terms to guess which ("`Errors::STATUS_CODES` deliberately omits 402"): `pow_required` means "pay the toll and retry" — which this harness does only during registration, `#query`/`#run`/`#pay` never retry — and `payment_setup_required` means the attacker never put a card on file, so a tolled verb printed `BLOCKED ✓ … (HTTP 402)` and `all_blocked?` answered true for an attack that never executed. LATENT rather than exploited — the per-verb toll is opt-in and unset in every battery, and all three consuming demos measurably answer 403 at those sites, so no verdict moved — but it arms the day an operator prices a real verb, which is the payment model's documented shipped configuration; a 402 now produces a could-not-test verdict naming which of the three answered and fails the battery instead of passing it, and a scenario that genuinely means a payment gate names the code with `verdict_from(expect_code:)`.
- 2026-08-16: the two throwaway keypairs the production eager-load gate needs are minted inside that step instead of exported to the whole job (K-756) — written to `$GITHUB_ENV`, `KIOSK_UNLOCK_SIGNING_KEY_PEM` reached every later step of the skooti job, so the demo server signed rental tokens with a per-run ephemeral key (dev/test resolve `ENV.fetch(...)` before the baked PEM) while the lock simulator the flow drivers provision verified against `config/dev_unlock_key.pem`, which never reads ENV by design; `demo:rideflow` had been red on `unlocked expected true, got false` since K-686 landed, and the green runs that seemed to contradict that were the scheduled and Dependabot workflows, which run no demo. A variable one step needs belongs in that step's `env:`; the K-686/K-673 refusals to boot production without a real key, or with the shipped dev key, are untouched.
- 2026-08-16: all 14 gems move to 0.3.0 and a fifth `bin/check-*` guard keeps them there (K-748) — `protocol.md` §14.1 promises that the protocol, this implementation and the AI assistant skill share their MAJOR.MINOR version, and the implementation was the one subject it was false about on every request: the wire said `Kiosk-API-Version: 0.3.0` while `Kiosk-Server-Version` said `0.0.1`, from thirteen gems at 0.0.1 and kiosk-pow-equihash at 0.1.0. Phil chose to honour the published rule rather than weaken it, so the gems, their `~> 0.3.0` inter-gem constraints and every lockfile now sit on the protocol's line, and `bin/check-version-parity` (its own CI job) makes a future drift a red build rather than a ledger row — MAJOR.MINOR only, since per-gem PATCH releases stay legitimate.
- 2026-08-16: kiosk-server ships `PowSpentStores::ActiveRecord`, the shared spent-id store a multi-process operator now owes the protocol (K-738) — PoW single-use was enforced by an in-process Hash, so `WEB_CONCURRENCY > 1` accepted the same proof once per worker while the spec claimed single-use flat, and the requirement to override existed only as a comment with nothing to point at. One optional table (`SchemaDefinitions.pow_spent_sql`, deliberately outside the ten canonical migrations since a single-process operator needs no table), a `claim` that is one `INSERT … ON CONFLICT` so the primary key and not application code picks the winner, and the operator obligation stated where it is hit: `PowGate`, the config slot, kiosk-server's README and the deploy runbook. The default is UNCHANGED — this adds the referent and the instruction, it does not decide that in-process should start refusing to boot.
- 2026-08-16: the seven operator demos and the kiosk-server default `skill_url` re-pin to `skill-v0.3.11` (URL + sha256 moved together, ADR-0012) — the immutable cut in which the skill stops telling assistants that HTTP 402 carries only two errors and that a `WWW-Authenticate` header is a safe substitute for `error.code`, which `payment_failed` (carrying no such header, `wire_controller.rb#www_authenticate_for`) makes false on every declined card. Intent: what each provider's discovery document advertises must be the skill that matches the wire it serves.
- 2026-08-13: a BLOCKED verdict in the shared redteam harness now names the gate it proved, so a green `demo:redteam` means what three demos' CI gates claim it means (K-728…K-734) — the harness could only ask "was this refused by anything?", and six scenarios were passing on refusals that had nothing to do with their attack: `CrossTenantRead` counted a 404, a 402 or a 500 on B's query as isolation (a crash masquerading as a block, which this gem's own invariant forbids twice in prose) and had no control proving the query returns anything for its owner, so it passed identically against a provider with no isolation logic; the two registration scenarios both printed BLOCKED against a server answering 404 on every path, exit 0; the KYC scenarios threw away the payment they staged, so a 402 there left the PAYMENT gate to refuse the gated action and be credited as the KYC gate; `PayForOtherUseSelf` reported a card decline or an expired token as "blocked at pay step (early ownership check)" without ever attempting the attack; and a battery in which every scenario skipped exited 0. Scenarios now demand the status and denial code that constitute a refusal of their own attack (`verdict_from(expect:, expect_code:)`), assert the state they staged actually took, and carry positive controls; the runner prints the status on each BLOCKED line and refuses to go green with no proofs at all. Expect verdicts to move: this changes what a green battery means.
- 2026-08-13: skooti's licence gates read `needs_licence` through ActiveRecord's own boolean cast and fail closed by construction (K-687 follow-up) — the gate that keeps the KYC-gated motorcycle away from the licence-free `start_rental` enumerated by hand the truthy spellings it happened to know (`== true || == "t" || == "true"`), which is correct against today's pg adapter and fails OPEN against anything else: one adapter, cast or schema change spelling the same fact `"TRUE"` or `1`, and the unrecognised value reads as licence-free — a signed unlock token for the motorcycle with no KYC. Each gate now casts and closes in its own direction: only a value Rails recognises as FALSE lets `start_rental` proceed, only a literal TRUE lets `rent_motorcycle` unlock, so no reading of the column opens both doors.

It still fails closed; it now names the file, the environment that resolved it, and both ways out.
- 2026-08-13: production now refuses the burned dev keys THEMSELVES, not merely the silent fallback to them (defence-in-depth on K-686 and K-673) — requiring `KIOSK_UNLOCK_SIGNING_KEY_PEM` and `PROVE_KEY_PEM` only proved that a supplied value parsed as a private key of the right algorithm, so pasting the world-readable `config/dev_unlock_key.pem` or `config/dev_prove_key.pem` straight into the variable booted production signing unlock tokens and KYC attestations anyone with a clone can forge: the exact outcome those two rows closed, re-opened by hand, and a plausible reaction to a boot that demands a PEM nobody has generated yet. Both apps now compare the supplied key's public half in DER against the shipped one — so a re-serialised copy cannot slip past on PEM text — and abort naming the file and the fix; a freshly generated key is unaffected, and dev/test still load the shipped key deliberately.
- 2026-08-13: prove's "Confirmed" page no longer tells a human their answer was delivered when it was not (K-706) — `approve!` discarded `CallbackPoster.deliver`'s return (raw HTTP status, or `nil` on a transport error) and rendered the same "we sent it" text regardless, on a row already flipped to `confirmed` and therefore unretriable; the page now branches on whether delivery actually succeeded and says so honestly. This is the minimum honest fix (correct the claim); making an undelivered row retryable is K-705's separate, still-open fix.
- 2026-08-13: skooti's and getgrocery's KYC callback controllers now check `aud` on the broker's claim, closing the gap between their comment's parity claim and what they verified (K-704) — both said "exactly as the engine KycVerifier would" while omitting the operator-binding `aud` check the engine requires, and while it was not exploitable (both already checked a demo-layer `operator` claim, and the engine enforces `aud` independently on the hot path), a false parity comment on a security-critical path is worth closing rather than merely rewording. `required_claims` now includes `aud`, and a mismatch renders 403 "audience mismatch", matching the engine's own check byte for byte.
- 2026-08-13: the confirmation code hoteling hands out is one the hotel actually has (K-698) — `confirm_booking` minted a fresh `SecureRandom.uuid` for the response while the UPDATE wrote only status and updated_at, against a `bookings` table with no such column, so the demo issued a booking reference it kept no record of: a guest quoting it at the desk could not be matched, and the published before/after narrative promised it as the outcome of the confirm step. It is PERSISTED rather than withdrawn (the narrative is the reason, and the fix is one column): written in the same UPDATE under a `COALESCE` so an already-coded booking can never be re-coded, read back out of that statement's `RETURNING` so the assistant is handed what was stored, and listed by `my_bookings` so it can be re-read later; `demo:book` now asserts the returned code, the `my_bookings` code and the database column are the same string.
- 2026-08-13: atablefor's `availability` answers an empty list instead of crashing on it (K-691) — the empty-result path was a top-level `return [] if seatings.empty?` inside the block the query registry stores and the Executor calls long after this file's frame is gone, so it raised `LocalJumpError` → `ActionFailed` → HTTP 500, on input the descriptor's own `input_schema` ACCEPTS (`time: "18:00"` matches the declared pattern but is not one of the three seatings; any date past the rolling horizon is a valid `format: "date"`) and which nothing validates server-side. It is now `next`, the block-legal form, deliberately fixed at the site rather than by hoisting the body into a method — a later refactor to controller/mixin methods (T-063/T-068) would make `return` legal again and dissolve this row with no commit to point at, so the guarantee is pinned by a redteam beat asserting 200-with-empty-rows for three schema-valid filters, which holds under any later shape.
- 2026-08-13: getgrocery's `create_order` guard now checks the type its own message promised (K-693) — it read `raise BadRequest("items must be a non-empty array") if items.empty?`, an EMPTINESS check wearing a type check's words, and since `input_schema` is not enforced at the wire, `items: "x"`, `items: {sku:…}` and `items: ["bread"]` each walked past it into `.map`/`it[:sku]` and raised a raw NoMethodError or TypeError that surfaced as a 500 — a client type error reported as an operator crash, on the flagship demo's headline action and the one the onboarding page is modelled on, which is how K-645 came to cite this handler as the correct contrast it was not. `items` must now be an Array and each element an object, both with typed 400s that name the shape wanted, and a redteam beat drives eight mis-shaped carts plus a well-formed control.

- 2026-08-13: hoteling stops selling the same room twice (K-690) — `reserve_room` validated exactly two things, room-type↔property and check_out > check_in, and never re-applied the overlap exclusion its own `availability` query defines, with no database constraint behind it either, so two assistants reading the same availability page could both reserve and both SETTLE for one physical room and the operator would owe two guests one bed; `hotel_detail` compounded it by listing every room type with no date filter at all, so search → detail → reserve read a catalogue as if it were an offer. The invariant is now stated where it cannot be skipped — a Postgres EXCLUDE constraint over the half-open night range, scoped to the live statuses — with the pre-check and `rescue` that turn both the slow and the raced case into one 409 an assistant can act on, `hotel_detail` takes optional dates and says in its own response which of the two lists it returned, and a redteam beat drives same-nights, overlapping-nights and the abutting-nights positive control so the hole cannot reopen unseen.
- 2026-08-13: the claim this file already made about the solver's location is now true (K-696) — the K-627 entry below ends "the only place in the demos that knows where solve.py lives is the gem that packages it", and skooti's `demo:rideflow` contradicted it twice with `File.expand_path("../../../kiosk-pow-equihash/solve.py", __dir__)`, a path that resolves in a monorepo checkout and nowhere else, wrapped in two hand-rolled copies of the registration handshake that were weaker than the shared helper: one raised `NoMethodError` on any 402 whose body carried no `error.challenges`, and the other read `access_token` with no status check, so a failed register sent `Bearer ` and the 403 it earned was reported as the expected 403. Both now call the shipped `equihash_register`, which asks the gem where its own solver is and refuses anything but a 201.
- 2026-08-13: the demo rake harnesses stop telling developers to add an `/etc/hosts` entry that breaks the demo (K-695) — atablefor, getgrocery, hoteling and skooti still resolved and dialled `<demo>.app` and printed it as the realistic-domain instruction, while their `config/environments/development.rb` had moved to `<demo>.demo.kiosk.tech`, so the one branch the block exists to offer was the one that 403'd on Rails 8 HostAuthorization and then burned the 200-only readiness poll's timeout before aborting with the wrong cause; CI never saw it because with no hosts entry everything falls back to 127.0.0.1. The harnesses now name the domain Rails actually permits, print the hint whenever they fall back rather than only when the lookup errored, and keep accepting 127.0.0.1 alone — that name now resolves publicly to the live demo box, and no local harness may be steered onto it.
- 2026-08-13: an operator app no longer picks its KYC-broker credential out of an `||` chain of other operators' variables (K-694) — the seven demos' byte-identical `config/environments/production.rb` states that nothing in its env block may name a single demo and then named two, `ENV["KIOSK_PROVE_GETGROCERY_SECRET"] || ENV["KIOSK_PROVE_SKOOTI_SECRET"]`, so a process carrying both secrets authenticated to the broker as the wrong operator, silently and with nothing structural preventing it; every operator app now reads ONE role-named `KIOSK_PROVE_INTAKE_SECRET` in all three environments, which restores byte-identity without naming anybody, while the broker keeps its per-operator registry names — the two sides pair by value, and the broker resolves the operator from the `operator_id` in the intake body. **Deploys must rename the variable in each operator env file before the next restart.**
