- 2026-08-13: the `/auth.md` discovery document every operator serves now teaches the registration toll's real wire (K-675) — it advertised the pre-ADR-0022 body shape `{ public_key, signed[, pow] }` and never named the `Kiosk-PoW` header the server actually reads proofs from, so an assistant obeying our own published instructions on any pow-tolled operator (all seven demos toll registration unconditionally) could not satisfy the 402 and looped forever; the served text now states the header transport, the `WWW-Authenticate: Kiosk-PoW` pairing and that a body `pow` field is ignored, with a rendered-output assertion pinning it so the retired shape cannot regrow.
- 2026-08-13: `UuidCheck.valid?` (the six demos' K-579/K-581/K-582 wire-shape guard) now delegates uuid recognition to the `uuid` gem's `UUID.validate` instead of hand-rolling it (K-661), ANDed with the existing canonical-form `PATTERN` so the looser compact/`urn:uuid:` spellings the gem alone would accept still fail closed with a typed 400 — `JSON_SCHEMA_PATTERN` (K-596) and the public API are unchanged.
- 2026-08-12: the KYC broker can no longer silently sign attestations with the world-readable dev key (K-673, closing the broker half of the hole whose operator half K-650 closed) — outside dev/test it refuses to boot unless PROVE_KEY_PEM is set to a parseable RSA private key, instead of falling back to the keypair whose private half ships in this public repo; dev/test keep the baked key, and the broker's env reading joins the K-650 config/environments placement, dropping the dev intake-secret defaults that had been orphaned when the operator side dropped its matching half (K-672).
- 2026-08-12: each environment's configuration posture now lives in that environment's file (K-650, ENV-CONFIG-PLACEMENT) — the demos' env-var reading, dev/test fallbacks and crash-if-absent fetches move out of initializers and lib files into `config/environments/{development,test,production}.rb` as Rails custom config, and along the way the pinned dev KYC-broker key that production would silently trust (its private half ships in this public repo) is gone: the broker key and intake secret are now supplied explicitly or the KYC flow fails closed.
- 2026-08-12: the PoW demos' bad-proof counter is now per-identity, stored in sqlite (K-498) — one abusive assistant's rejected proofs no longer inflate anyone else's count (the old flat /tmp file was one shared tally, and concurrent processes fought over it), while the labelled toy aspects (boot truncation, no TTL/decay) deliberately remain; both `demo:pow` flows now prove an innocent second identity stays at zero.
- 2026-08-12: every claims surface now names only domains that exist (K-652 REAL-DEMO-DOMAINS, K-662 PROVE-MY-DOMAIN) — the invented operator domains (help@getgrocery.com, *.app support emails and URLs, the atablefor.us apex notes) become the real hosted demo domains (`<demo>.demo.kiosk.tech`, support `demo@kiosk.tech`), and the KYC broker demo stops being called "prove.my" everywhere in prose and on its own served pages: it is the broker at `kyc.demo.kiosk.tech`, which is where its runtime defaults already pointed.
- 2026-08-12: the kiosk-server engine now owns the routes (K-505, T-055) — one `mount Kiosk::Server::Engine => Kiosk.configuration.mount_path` line serves the full surface (wire/auth/JWKS/KYC/binding under the mount, plus the root-relative discovery documents installed only when the engine is actually mounted), turning the ~28 hand-copied route lines each demo carries into an escape hatch rather than the adoption path; the demos and the e2e fixture keep their hand-drawn routes byte-identical until T-057 migrates them, and a fresh-host probe proves mount-only, unmounted-inert and double-draw (hand-drawn wins by first match) end to end.
- 2026-08-12: atablefor's auxiliary PoW flows stop squatting sibling demos' dev ports (K-649, the K-644 residue) — `demo:reputation` ran on 3004 (skooti's dev port) and `demo:backoff` on 3006 (philslist's), so running either while that sibling was up on one box meant a bind failure or the wrong app answering; both move outside the 3001–3008 demo band (3104/3106), keeping the deliberate "distinct from atablefor's own dev port" property that motivated the original picks.
- 2026-08-12: the demo copy check closes its last db/migrate blind spot (K-648) — a hand-written migration shipped by two demos at demo-specific timestamps was compared to NOTHING (each copy a singleton path, no template emitting it), so `bin/check-demo-copies` now holds such pairs in declared code-agreement by timestamp-stripped basename, an undeclared pair fails the build, and the one real divergence found — getgrocery's `create_kyc_verification_requests` declares `broker_nonce` inline where skooti adds it in a follow-up migration — is recorded as drift pending triage rather than silently blessed.
- 2026-08-12: a fresh `rails g kiosk:install` now emits the agent-governance migration (K-647) — the K-436 per-assistant spending-cap surface read columns (`spending_cap_cents`, `human_label`) that no generator created, so every demo worked only because it carried a hand-written copy while a fresh adopter enabling governance hit missing-column failures at runtime; the template was lifted verbatim from the demos' byte-identical copies and is held against them by `bin/check-demo-copies`, closing the loop K-640 opened.
- 2026-08-11: kiosk-server's error taxonomy becomes the wire contract instead of a parallel framework (K-495, T-054) — the `error.code` vocabulary now lives in one table and handlers speak Rails' own idiom (a rendered status becomes its wire code, Rails-registered raises map through a single `rescue_from` the mixin installs, an explicitly rendered vocabulary code — including a specific 402 — travels verbatim), so an operator never needs a Kiosk exception class for what a bare `render json:, status:` already says; the class-per-status duplicates are deprecated for handler code and the unused `QuotaExceeded` class is deleted with its code kept reserved.
- 2026-08-12: philslist and stylish boot under production eager-load again — K-627 shipped both demos the shared `lib/equihash_register.rb` helper (top-level flow methods, no constant) without the `autoload_lib` exclusion the other five carriers already have, so `RAILS_ENV=production` raised `Zeitwerk::NameError` on boot (the exact K-422 class; the zeitwerk:check gate and the production smoke both caught it on the pushed head). Both demos now exclude the helper like their siblings, since it is loaded explicitly by flow scripts and never at runtime.
- 2026-08-11: the six skeleton files that were byte-identical across all seven operator demos with nothing guarding them are now guarded, and the T-048 statics ship declared rather than merely present (K-643) — `public/robots.txt`, `public/favicon.ico`, the three static error pages, `config/puma.rb` and the test/production environment files move from the copy check's recorded-but-unenforced `SKELETON_NOT_COMPARED` list into declared `:identical` lockstep (prove, the API-only broker, is the stated exception on the six it legitimately differs in), because a recorded exclusion still lets a seventh copy drift silently and one of the six — `public/422.html` — carries the CSRF signpost whose false claim already had to be corrected across all eight copies once; the production-boot smoke now also asserts `/robots.txt` and `/favicon.ico` answer 200 in both of its surfaces, pinning the T-048 statics that turned every crawler hit from a logged `ActionController::RoutingError` into a served file.
- 2026-08-11: one solver location, one solve helper (K-627, on the K-632 accessor) — seven demo flow drivers each carried their own checkout-relative `SOLVE_PY` constant and a private copy of the six-line solve function, and five of them ALSO loaded the shared `lib/equihash_register.rb`, so two copies of the identical helper sat in one process under different names; every flow now solves through the shared helper, the helper (now shipped by all seven registration-gated demos and held in lockstep by `bin/check-demo-copies`) asks `Kiosk::Pow::Equihash.solver_path` for the solver instead of reaching into a sibling directory, and the only place in the demos that knows where solve.py lives is the gem that packages it.
- 2026-08-11: the demo copy check now holds the generator-output migrations against the engine's own templates, not only against each other (K-640) — all seven demos could drift from kiosk-server's install generator TOGETHER and stay green, because the `db/migrate` files were only ever compared to their siblings; every `.tt` template is now rendered with the generator's defaults and must byte-match every demo copy (matched by timestamp-stripped basename, which also brings the four copies sitting at demo-specific timestamps into any comparison for the first time), so regenerating the engine's schema without regenerating the demos fails the build. The four template↔copy divergences that already exist — two comment-only, plus the two kyc migrations every demo ships renamed with a different migration class — are recorded as drift pending triage rather than silently blessed, and each such record errors the day its copies converge.
- 2026-08-11: each demo's development port is now the port it deploys on (K-644) — every demo's docs and defaults named a dev port different from its `deploy/env` `PORT`, and for two pairs the numbers were crossed (hoteling's dev port was skooti's deploy port and vice versa; getgrocery's dev port was stylish's deploy port), so a reader following one demo's own instructions on a box laid out per `deploy/` did not get connection-refused, they got a DIFFERENT demo answering; philslist, stylish and tudu also all defaulted to 3001, so those three could not run side by side out of the box. One numbering now holds in both worlds — getgrocery 3001, atablefor 3002, hoteling 3003, skooti 3004, stylish 3005, philslist 3006, tudu 3007 — across each demo's rake tasks, initializer issuer, `bin/demo`, flow-driver headers and the cross-demo references to skooti's example URL.
- 2026-08-11: the two gems that shipped no changelog now ship one, and the conditional that papered over the absence is gone (K-634) — `kiosk-pow-equihash.gemspec` listed `CHANGELOG.md` behind a `File.exist?` guard, the same fail-open just removed from kiosk-pow-cuckoo, and it was telling the truth only by accident because the file did not exist; both gems get a `CHANGELOG.md` reconstructed from the repository's own history — for equihash the verifier rewrite, the 192/7→168/7 retune, the production-parameter KAT and the solver pin; for kiosk-redteam the Equihash-not-hashcash correction, the header-borne proof and the three ways its harness could report green without proving anything — and neither claims a release that has not happened. The file list is now unconditional, so a file the gemspec names and cannot find breaks the build instead of going quiet, and kiosk-redteam finally has something to point `changelog_uri` at.
- 2026-08-11: the shipped default PoW backend stops being the emptiest page of the set (K-633) — `kiosk-pow-equihash` declared no description, no homepage and no metadata at all, so the gem a reader lands on straight after the README says «one PoW backend, Equihash» rendered on RubyGems as a single summary line with no prose and no link to the project, the source or the issue tracker, while its thirteen siblings all carried them. It now describes what it actually is — a zero-dependency pure-Ruby VERIFIER, the 168/7 defaults and why the benchmark chose them, and the asymmetry it sells (a request gets priced; hardware does not get equalised, and Equihash was ASIC'd on Zcash) — with the four `*_uri` keys its siblings declare.
- 2026-08-11: shipped comments and headings stop dating the code to a milestone number that no longer exists (K-580) — «pre-v0.1», «post-v0.1», «the 0.2 seam» and «roles are hook-or-absent in 0.1» told a reader the code was an old milestone of a protocol whose wire has said 0.3.x for a month, and in two places the number was carrying a claim rather than a date: kiosk-server's README headed «What's in this release (pre-v0.1)» two lines above «the full host-side surface is shipped», and both the e2e fixture and the stylish demo told providers to use a richer `Kiosk::Action` DSL «in production» that does not exist. The milestone is the tech preview and carries no number, so the qualifier is dropped and the two claims are replaced by what is actually true — a registered name and block IS the shipped Action API, and adapter-supplied token minting is a seam nothing calls yet.
- 2026-08-11: kiosk-pay-stripe's published description stops describing half the adapter (K-636) — it advertised only `#capture` over PaymentIntents, so a reader on RubyGems concluded the gem charges a card it is somehow already handed, and missed that ACQUIRING the card is the other half of what ships: the hosted setup session that saves a card on the provider's own Stripe account, the readiness signal the wire uses to ask a human for one, and the host-injected customer mapping that keeps the gem out of application tables. Nothing it said was false; it just left out the part that makes assistant-orchestrated, provider-acquired payment work.
- 2026-08-11: four shipped gem READMEs stop pointing readers at directories that only exist if you cloned the monorepo (K-635) — `kiosk-pow` and `kiosk-pow-cuckoo` sent anyone looking for the default PoW backend to `../kiosk-pow-equihash`, and the two RLS harnesses cross-linked each other and `kiosk-test-support` the same way, so on RubyGems (or in an installed gem, where `../` is bundler's gems directory) every one of those six links was dead; a gem cannot package another gem, so they become absolute repository URLs, which is the only form that resolves for a reader who has just the one gem.
- 2026-08-11: the eight demo `.gitignore` files are one file again, and what the copy check does NOT compare is now written down (K-629) — the ignores had grown four variants (getgrocery repeating the root ignore's `mise.toml` under a comment calling it a Stripe secret, tudu alone ignoring `/config/*.key`, prove missing three blocks) in the one skeleton file that has no per-port or per-host dimension and where drift already cost this project a CI break (K-404, a `credentials.yml.enc` committed without its key); it is now declared `:identical` and guarded. The rest of the Rails skeleton stays deliberately uncompared, but the exclusion is no longer an assumption a reader has to re-derive from silence: forty duplicated paths are recorded with what their copies are allowed to differ by, and the record is checked for staleness — which is how hoteling's development.rb was caught telling the reader to browse skooti's host on skooti's port.
- 2026-08-11: the one comment that changes how a file runs is no longer the one comment the copy check ignores (K-628) — `:code` normalises comments away with Ripper, so three of the four `application_controller.rb` copies had silently lost `# frozen_string_literal: true` and compared equal to the one that kept it; the check now extracts and compares the leading magic-comment block (the recognised Ruby set only, so ordinary prose still varies freely), watched red on exactly that divergence, and the thirteen demo files that had drifted off the convention every other Ruby file in this repo follows are back on it.
- 2026-08-11: the deploy checklist's account of the push-to-deploy hook stops omitting the one step that keeps the demos populated (K-626) — it described a hook that runs `db:prepare` and restarts, which is the exact hook K-464 had to fix after live hoteling served 5 properties instead of 100, and with `prune.sh` deleted the runbook's step 5 was left as the only place the re-seed story was told; the checklist now lists `db:seed` with the reason it is there, so an operator rebuilding the hook from it cannot silently re-ship that defect on a fresh box.
- 2026-08-11: what a `gem install` actually delivers is now checked, and for kiosk-server it had been broken (K-580 gemspec half) — the gemspec packaged `lib/**/*` only, so the three ERB templates under `app/views/kiosk/server/` were absent from the built gem while two controllers `append_view_path` at exactly that directory: anyone installing from RubyGems got `ActionView::MissingTemplate` on BOTH HTML pages of the account-binding ceremony, and nothing here could have noticed, because every consumer in this monorepo uses `path:`, which serves the working tree rather than a package. The two PoW gemspecs lose the same trap in its other shapes (a `File.exist?` select that would have packaged a renamed solver silently and green, an `*.rb`-shaped glob waiting to drop the first data file anyone puts under `lib/`, a shipped README linking bench evidence that was not in the gem), and `bin/check-gem-packaging` — its own CI job, stdlib-only — now builds all 14 gems with the real builder, reads the file list back out of the `.gem`, and fails when a tracked file is neither packaged nor declared scaffolding with a reason, or when packaged Ruby resolves a `__dir__`-relative path to something the package does not contain; it was watched red on four separate paths, the original omission among them. The kiosk-server and kiosk-all descriptions stop advertising a pre-controllers subset of a gem that ships nine controllers, the Executor and the registration endpoints.
- 2026-08-11: `deploy/prune.sh` is deleted (K-630, Phil's call) — the file kept a name for work it explicitly did not do, and neither of its halves had a job left: pruning was a permanent no-op and re-seeding is already covered twice over, by `demo-reset.sh` for disk and by the push-to-deploy hook for the catalog (K-464), with the cron deliberately never installed (K-593); the runbook now names those two tools where it used to point at the script, so an operator reads what actually does the housekeeping instead of a promise nobody installs.
- 2026-08-11: kiosk-server stops depending on Rails by accident (K-495 / T-052) — it ships a Rails engine, nine ActionController subclasses, an install generator and nine ActiveRecord migration templates, yet its gemspec declared none of that and every install merely happened to be a Rails app that had the framework loaded already; it now declares railties, actionpack, activerecord and activesupport at `~> 8.1` (the four components it actually references — not the `rails` meta-gem, which would make an operator install Action Mailer, Action Cable, Active Job and Active Storage for nothing), and the ten `if defined?(::ActionController::API)` / `if defined?(::Rails::Engine)` wrappers that quietly defined nothing when the framework was absent are gone, so a missing dependency is now a LoadError at require time instead of a NameError somewhere in production.
- 2026-08-11: the code the demos hand-copy between each other is now held together by a check rather than by habit (K-623/K-607/K-624) — the demos are separate Rails apps, so thirty-eight files (the telemetry module and its controller in seven, the PoW difficulty map in seven, the uuid guard in six, the engine's own migrations) agreed only because whoever last edited one remembered the rest; a stale comment survived in six of seven telemetry copies until a fix propagated it by accident, and a constant rename in the register helper reached three of five copies and stopped there unnoticed for three weeks. `bin/check-demo-copies` fails the build when copies that must agree do not, when a NEW duplicate appears that nobody declared, and when a demo ships the telemetry module without the route, controller, load-bearing require and middleware that make it record anything — which is what extends getgrocery's two telemetry gates to the six copies no gate boots.
- 2026-08-11: getgrocery's `demo:reconcile` now says in its own description, where a reader meets it, that it reports rather than asserts and cannot go red (K-616) — it shares the `demo:` namespace with tasks that ARE gates, and the only record of that distinction was a comment in the CI workflow.
- 2026-08-11: the deploy runbook stops promising an account prune nobody ever wrote (K-615/K-610) — `prune.sh` gated both its halves on `demo:prune`/`demo:seed` rake tasks that no demo defines, so every night it would have logged "no demo:prune task yet" and reclaimed nothing, while the runbook advertised retention as a feature; the promise is withdrawn rather than implemented (per-agent isolation makes stale accounts harmless, and `demo-reset.sh` already reclaims everything in one shot), the script keeps only the re-seed it can actually perform and says plainly that it prunes nothing, and the runbook's file table now describes all ten files instead of six — including the CI-only smoke that drops databases and refuses to run on a deploy host.
- 2026-08-11: a telemetry failure can no longer replay the request it was observing (K-622) — the demo middleware's "never break the request" recovery re-entered `@app.call(env)` from a handler that also covered everything AFTER the app had answered, so a raise while buffering an `/auth/register` response dispatched the whole registration a second time; recovery is now split at the dispatch line (hand the request to the app before it, carry on with the response already in hand after it), and the module's request path — untested anywhere until now, in all seven demo copies — gains a gate that was watched red on exactly that replay before the fix landed.
- 2026-08-11: a demo task can no longer ship undocumented (K-621) — four demos had grown tasks their README's own list never mentioned, and once the generated "Which of these run in CI" table landed directly beneath that list each README contradicted itself within one screen (`demo:rls | yes` in the table, no `demo:rls` at all in the prose above it, and nothing telling a reader which to believe); `bin/check-ci-tasks` now also fails when a defined task is never named in its README outside the generated block, asserting presence only and staying blind to which shape a demo documents tasks in, and the six gaps it found are filled — including the RLS enforcement proof and the pagination proof, both CI gates that no README mentioned.
- 2026-08-11: the live-activity telemetry module is no longer the one shipped component nothing can detect the breakage of (K-620) — it is copied byte-for-byte into all seven operator demos and swallows every error by design, so a broken write records nothing, raises nowhere and simply reads back as an empty landing tile; getgrocery's `demo:telemetry`, the only executable check of it, now runs on every push (watched go red first: rename a column in its INSERT and it still reports "wrote 40 rows" while the aggregate comes back all-zero). Because the task writes, it now refuses the SHARED hosted store unless an operator asks by name, so CI's safety no longer rests on an environment variable happening to be unset.
- 2026-08-11: which demo tasks are CI gates is now an enforced artifact instead of something a person had to re-derive by hand (K-617/K-618/K-619) — five separate reviews had walked every rake file to find assertion batteries no gate invoked, because nothing outside the workflow recorded the list and the workflow's own comments had already drifted; every `demo:` task must now be either in its CI task list or in a new per-demo `ungated:` map stating why it is not a gate, `bin/check-ci-tasks` fails the build on anything in neither (and cross-checks its own parse against Rake's task table), and each demo README carries a generated "Which of these run in CI" table so an operator reading a command can tell a gate from a local showcase. The two tasks that gap had left uncovered — hoteling's proof that browsing depth gets priced rather than banned, and atablefor's curl tour of the wire surface — now run on every push, each watched go red first.
- 2026-08-11: a reader opening a demo now sees a Rails app rather than a pile of loose drivers (K-503) — the ~45 `*_flow.rb` / `redteam_suite.rb` assertion drivers sat at each demo's top level beside `Gemfile` and `README.md`, so the first impression of a soon-to-be-public reference implementation was clutter; they move into `script/`, the directory Rails 8 already generates for exactly this kind of one-off developer script (three demos were shipping an empty `script/.keep`), and nothing about what they prove changes.
- 2026-08-11: the Stripe setup-reuse lookup loses its second silent route to the wrong answer (K-492 follow-up) — it asks for one page of the customer's open Checkout Sessions, so a principal holding more than that could have the reusable session truncated off it and get a fresh url per poll while every response still looked like the happy path; a full page with nothing reusable in it is now logged, exactly as a failed lookup already was, and the page size is a named constant that says why it cannot simply be raised.
- 2026-08-11: six more assertion batteries stop depending on someone remembering to type them — hoteling's pagination/cursor proof, stylish's real-Devise role gate and both registration-PoW gates, and getgrocery's claim-rebind pay path and its only RLS *enforcement* proof now run on every push, as does kiosk-pay-stripe's stripe-mock coverage layer, which had been silently skipping in CI because only the demos job installed the binary; each wired task was watched go RED against a deliberately broken invariant before being wired, and the task order records which ones reseed the database so a later one is never stranded.
- 2026-08-11: the three payment demos' poll instructions now follow the skill's own schedule instead of prescribing a second, conflicting one (K-477/K-595 follow-up) — the descriptors asked for a flat ~5 s cadence and promised a check count more than double what the skill's ~5 s-then-~15 s schedule actually yields in the same wall-clock budget, so an assistant honouring the skill would stop far short of what it had been told to expect; the counts are gone entirely, leaving only the give-up horizon, which cannot drift out of step with a cadence.
- 2026-08-11: the Stripe adapter stops treating "I could not find out" as "there is no outstanding setup session" (K-492 follow-up) — a failed lookup still degrades to minting a fresh Checkout Session so the readiness probe keeps answering, but it now says so in the operator's log, because that degrade is otherwise byte-for-byte the stable-url happy path it silently replaces; and the reuse the README, the CHANGELOG and getgrocery's descriptor all promise is now exercised through the real Stripe SDK over real HTTP rather than resting entirely on doubles.
- 2026-08-11: the pay-path regression net now actually runs — the tasks pinning the double-charge and order-swap races, the stuck-`paying` reconciliation, the malformed-uuid 400 and the delivery-slot past-filter existed but were reachable only by a human who remembered to type them, so the invariants they guard were protected by nothing on a push; getgrocery's CI job now runs all three (the two DB-free specs first, `demo:race` last because it reseeds), each one watched go red against a deliberately broken invariant before being wired, not merely watched go green.
- 2026-08-11: the pre-deploy eager-load gate an operator types now matches the one CI runs (K-510/K-541) — the checklist's command omitted `KIOSK_ISSUER` (and, for getgrocery, a Stripe key or mock url), so a human following it hit an initializer abort that says nothing about eager loading; the deploy docs also gain the issuer as an explicit env step, the uninstalled prune cron now loads each app's env before invoking a production task that would otherwise refuse to boot, and the rule that a new crash-if-absent config joins BOTH copies in the same commit is now recorded in both.
- 2026-08-11: a malformed uuid off the wire is now a typed 400 in EVERY demo, not just getgrocery (K-581/K-582) — the same `::uuid`-cast crash the K-579 guard fixed for one app was live in five more (skooti, hoteling, atablefor, tudu, philslist), where an agent-supplied reservation/booking/list/todo/account/listing id reached Postgres behind nothing but a presence check and came back as a raw 500 leaking the PG error text — worst of all on the two pay paths, where an assistant cannot tell a 500 from "the charge may have gone through"; each demo now carries the same shape guard ahead of any query or charge, and each redteam battery gains a `MalformedUuidArg` beat asserting 400 + `bad_request` + no SQL internals on the wire.
- 2026-08-11: hoteling's and skooti's `payment_setup` descriptors gain the poll cadence and give-up horizon getgrocery already published (K-595), so an assistant that ever meets a `setup_required` is told when to stop polling instead of inventing a loop; getgrocery's stable-setup_url promise is deliberately NOT copied, because both demos run a stub PSP that mints no session to be stable.
- 2026-08-11: getgrocery declares `order_id`'s uuid shape in the machine contract instead of only in prose (K-596) — the two descriptors that take one now carry a uuid `pattern`/`format`, so an assistant reading the schema learns the shape rather than inferring it, and the runtime guard stays the enforcement since nothing validates `input_schema` server-side yet; its toy bad-proof counter is also labelled as the demo prop it is (K-590), matching the atablefor wording, so nobody mistakes a global boot-truncated file for a reputation signal.
- 2026-08-11: the production-boot smoke can no longer destroy a deployment (K-594) — it used to `db:drop` the two REAL deploy database names with Rails' protected-environments guard explicitly switched off, guarded by nothing but the convention that only CI calls it; it now targets throwaway `kiosk_<app>_smoke` databases, refuses any name that is not one, refuses outright on a box carrying deploy markers, refuses unless the caller states the target is disposable, and no longer disables Rails' guard at all. Same assertions, same coverage.
- 2026-08-11: the deploy runbook stops contradicting itself and the checklist stops shipping an operator without an edge rate limit (K-592/K-593/K-597) — every path now agrees with the layout the systemd unit and scripts actually use, the prune cron is described as the deliberate skip it is rather than as an installed step, and the checklist an operator ticks through now carries the REQUIRED rate-limit module install and uncomment steps (and the right vhost count) instead of only the runbook doing so.
- 2026-08-11: the production eager-load CI gate declares `KIOSK_ISSUER` as well as `KIOSK_POW_SECRET` — K-510 made the issuer crash-if-absent outside dev/test, so all seven operator demos aborted in the initializer before Zeitwerk ran and the job failed for a reason that had nothing to do with eager loading. (It fails loudly, not silently: the task exits 1 and the step has no continue-on-error; both omissions went unnoticed only because the workflow had not run since 2026-08-09.) A rule is recorded in the workflow: a config that learns to raise outside dev/test joins this step in the same commit. Verified by reproducing CI's exact environment locally — all eight demos eager-load clean.
- 2026-08-10: a PoW challenge is now honoured only at the difficulty the server currently demands, and the deploy docs stop pretending the toll is a DoS shield (K-541/K-540) — the gate re-derives the expected alg/params from live config on every verify and refuses an off-spec challenge before any hashing (a valid HMAC no longer makes `{n:8,k:1}` acceptable, so a leaked secret degrades the toll instead of removing it, and a challenge cannot outlive a difficulty raise), while the Caddyfile/runbook now state plainly that register-time PoW is unauthenticated CPU cost and an edge rate-limit is a REQUIRED deploy step, with the exact module install it needs.
- 2026-08-10: the `KIOSK_<APP>_DB` / `KIOSK_<APP>_DB_USER` names that the shipped deploy templates set are now honoured end-to-end instead of by nobody — every demo's production `database.yml` reads them, and `postgres-init.sql` + `demo-reset.sh` provision and reset the names the apps will actually connect to, so renaming a database is a real knob rather than a documented one the code ignored; every default is the current name, so today's deploy is byte-identical.
- 2026-08-10: the demos stop answering errors with silence (K-532/K-533) — every app now ships static 404/422/500 pages, so a production error is an explanation instead of `Content-Length: 0`, and a JSON-shaped `DELETE /users/sign_out` on the four Devise demos gets the Kiosk error envelope with a pointer to the wire rather than a bodyless 401 that promised JSON; the aim is that an assistant which guessed a web-app path always learns where the machine surface is.
- 2026-08-10: the production-boot smoke and the deploy runbook now enforce what was only remembered (K-534/K-513/K-541) — the smoke drives assistant-shaped requests at the human surfaces so the agent-signpost class of bug cannot regress unseen, the deploy checklist carries the pre-deploy eager-load gate, and both production gates get the PoW secret they had silently been aborting without since it became mandatory.
- 2026-08-10: atablefor's bad-proof counter is labelled in code as the toy it is (K-498) — it is global, truncated at boot and read by nothing but the local driver, so the comment now says outright that a real per-identity, decaying signal has to be specified before anyone wires this into the reputation toll.
- 2026-08-10: the prove.my broker's production database name is now READ from `KIOSK_PROVE_DB`, the variable its shipped deploy template has always set (K-577) — `database.yml` hardcoded the name and silently ignored the variable, so an operator who changed it in the env file would have been connected to the old database anyway; the default is unchanged, so today's deploy is byte-identical.
- 2026-08-10: the card-setup readiness probe stops minting a new hosted Checkout session on every poll (K-492) — `payment_setup` is documented as the thing an assistant re-calls while its human is at Stripe's page, but each call created a fresh `mode:setup` session with a fresh url (five sessions were observed for ONE live card setup), so an assistant relaying the newest link bounced its human off the page they were filling in; the adapter now reuses the session already outstanding for that principal, and the getgrocery/`kyc_status` descriptors state a poll cadence and a terminal stop condition so an agent no longer has to invent one (K-477). Same wire shape, same responses.
- 2026-08-10: a getgrocery order stranded in `paying` can now be reconciled instead of sitting unpayable forever (K-578) — a lost paid-flip after a successful capture self-heals from the settlement row (at the next pay attempt and via a new `rake demo:reconcile`), while an order whose outcome only the processor knows is reported UNRESOLVED and deliberately keeps its claim, since blind-releasing it is exactly the retry that could double-charge.
- 2026-08-10: a malformed `order_id` arriving from the wire now gets a clean 400 from getgrocery instead of a 500 (K-579) — agent-supplied ids reach an `::uuid` cast in three places (the cashier's cart reference, `create_order`'s replace path, `reschedule_delivery`), so a malformed one surfaced as an unhandled Postgres error, and on the pay path that is the worst possible answer because an assistant cannot tell it from "the charge may have gone through"; one shared shape guard (`lib/uuid_check.rb`) now rejects it before a connection is even taken, naming the offending value and pointing at `create_order`'s pay_hint without leaking SQL internals, pinned DB-free by `demo:cashier_spec` and over the real database by `demo:race`.
- 2026-08-10: the getgrocery pay path is serialized per order to kill two double-charge races (K-544) — orders get a created->paying->paid lifecycle claimed by an atomic conditional UPDATE before the cashier check, and create_order excludes `paying` under FOR UPDATE, so a create_order can no longer swap an order's items out from under an in-flight /pay ("pay EUR1, get EUR500") and N racing /pay on one order capture at most once; proven by a new real-DB threaded regression (rake demo:race).
- 2026-08-10: a PSP charge failure on /pay now returns a typed `payment_failed` 402 instead of a raw 500 (K-545) — the Stripe adapter translates declines/timeouts into a PSP-agnostic PaymentFailed with a human-safe message (no Stripe internals) and a retryable flag; a definitive decline releases the order claim for retry while an unknown outcome is left claimed so a lost-response retry can't double-charge, and my_orders' paid flag doubles as the pre-retry settlement lookup.
- 2026-08-10: the prove.my broker binds an attestation's `aud` to the operator's REGISTRATION, not the request body (K-550) — an allow-listed operator could previously request another operator's audience and receive an A-audience ProveKey-signed claim on its own callback; `aud` now comes from the authenticated operator's record, a mismatched body audience is rejected (403), and a root `reference/.gitignore` is added before open-sourcing so a fresh public clone can't commit machine-local or secret-bearing files (K-552).
- 2026-08-10: engine security hardening (K-540/542/543/548/551) — a valid PoW proof is single-use even under concurrency (atomic spent-store claim before the verify, closing an N-way replay TOCTOU that also consumes a bad register proof's challenge so one free 402 can't fuel unlimited garbage verifies); mandate amounts must be positive integer cents (a negative total laundered the spending cap negative); the auth-challenge store is size-capped so an unauthenticated distinct-key flood can't grow it within the TTL; and malformed inputs (string exp/iat, non-object PoW params) return a clean 4xx not a 500, with a max mandate lifetime enforced and the cap tally scoped by currency. Redis/SQL spent-store overrides must implement the new atomic claim (SETNX / INSERT..ON CONFLICT).
- 2026-08-10: production secrets stop shipping with world-readable defaults (K-541/547/553) — KIOSK_POW_SECRET (>=32 bytes), the prove.my intake secret, and the Stripe return URL are now required from the environment outside development (crash-if-absent, matching the signing key and issuer), so a public repo no longer exposes the HMAC key that would make PoW forgeable; and atablefor's three overlapping PoW flags collapse into one KIOSK_POW_MODE selector whose hosted default is the real reputation policy (K-497), with the factors reset that had silently killed the DB lookup removed.
- 2026-08-10: corrected false doc claims across gem/demo READMEs, the e2e README and the repo constitution ahead of open-sourcing (K-558, K-562..K-571) — PoW proofs ride the Kiosk-PoW header not a body field, register PoW is always-on while browse tolls are opt-in, plus fixes to redteam counts/status codes, the prove.my env defaults, the engine-enforced KYC aud check, the fixtures list, and the demo-app count (eight, not seven).
- 2026-08-10: kiosk-reputation's Challenge now raises a typed ArgumentError for a nil/non-Hash params instead of a cryptic NoMethodError, so a malformed challenge fails loud and rescuable rather than 500-ing deep in the gem (K-574).
- 2026-08-10: the two legacy proof-of-work gems stop shipping their own agent-facing `SKILL.md` (K-526, K-529) — agent instructions live ONLY in the canonical `kiosk.tech/skill.md`, so a per-gem copy can no longer drift into teaching a wire the server stopped speaking; `kiosk-pow`'s copy had done exactly that, still documenting the proof as a request-body field and naming argon2id as the protocol challenge two days after ADR-0022 made it a `Kiosk-PoW` header and equihash the default, and `kiosk-pow-cuckoo`'s was a mis-named duplicate of its own README carrying no skill instructions at all. Same reasoning as K-376, which retired the per-demo copies; the shipped default gem never had one.
- 2026-08-10: shipped comments now match the hosted deploy (K-491, K-509) — PoW `high` is documented as a per-demo knob that the deploy sets on **atablefor alone**, not "skooti + atablefor" as all seven copies claimed; the dead `KIOSK_POW_REGISTER_DEMO` flag is gone from every demo driver comment, since register PoW has been unconditional since K-487 and no code has read the flag for two days; and the deploy runbook now sizes and counts the EIGHT apps the box actually runs (the prove.my broker was missing from the count in four places). Comments and prose only, no behavior change.
- 2026-08-10: the demo apps stop accepting a FORGED cleartext identity in production (K-539, K-555) — both the agent-IdP stub (`agent:u-…:a-…:r-…`) and the user-IdP stub (`user:u-…`, plus stylish's role-granting `X-Staff-Session` header) parsed an UNSIGNED bearer straight into an identity and were wired unconditionally, so a deployed box authenticated anyone who typed the string (any user, self-granted `owner`). Both stubs are now gated to development/test; a deployed demo authenticates only via the signed kiosk-pop JWT or a real Devise session. Proven by production-smoke assertions (forged bearer -> 401) and redteam beats so it cannot regress.
- 2026-08-10: the 7 demos and the kiosk-server default re-pin to skill-v0.3.10 (the first-party, content-addressed solver + the absolute no-foreign-aud rule ship to agents).
- 2026-08-10: auth and proof-of-work rejections stop being dead ends without becoming attack surface (K-512, K-511) — an invalid Equihash proof now answers with a hint pointing at the shipped reference solver at kiosk.tech/pow/solve.py, so an assistant that hand-rolled a solver (which cost a live agent its whole budget) is told the one thing that recovers it, while a leak guard keeps every construction detail off the wire; and an audience mismatch no longer names ANY origin back to the caller, because telling an assistant to sign an origin it never dialed is precisely the relay the aud-binding exists to stop — the misconfiguration diagnostic now goes to the operator's log instead. `c.issuer` also gains the documentation that explains why it cannot be derived from the request host and what breaks when it is wrong (K-507).
- 2026-08-10: every shipped "v0.1 alpha" version stamp is gone (K-506) — the string dated the code to a milestone the protocol (`API_VERSION` 0.3.0) passed long ago, and in `kiosk-server`'s action registry it made a false promise, describing the minimal name→callable surface as a stub whose full DSL "lands in a follow-up release" when in truth that DSL was never built and is superseded by the Rails-native redesign (K-495/T-053); the docstring now says so. The demo routes/initializer comments drop the stamp for a plain "for now"/"in this demo", and the three gem READMEs still promising stability only "across pre-v0.1 minor bumps" are aligned to the pre-v1.0 wording their four sibling gems already use. Comments and status prose only — no behavior change.
- 2026-08-10: the shipped deploy env templates now agree with the deploy checklist on Puma sizing (K-474) — all eight `deploy/env/*.env.example` drop to the lean single-worker `WEB_CONCURRENCY=1` the checklist has always recommended for a small box, so an operator who copies a template verbatim gets the sizing the runbook assumes (all apps fitting a 2–4 GB box) and, on atablefor, a per-worker in-process PoW BackoffStore that is actually authoritative; the Postgres connection-budget note is restated against the new default.
- 2026-08-08: post-redesign cosmetic cleanup on two demos (K-489) — atablefor's public `/reservations` board no longer seeds two synthetic "existing" reservations (nor their two board-only diners), so the board is honestly EMPTY at rest and fills only with real bookings made over the wire (the empty-state view already renders "No upcoming reservations yet"); and stylish drops the now-vestigial `appointments.stylist_id` column (unused since the K-446 roster removal — the `salon_calendar` gate reads `staff_role` alone), via a new reversible drop migration with structure.sql regenerated. No behavior, gating, spec, or skill change.
- 2026-08-08: register-PoW is now UNCONDITIONAL on every demo — the `KIOSK_POW_REGISTER_DEMO` env flag is dropped (a per-demo flag that had to be remembered was the latent toll-free-register bug), so all six flag-gated demos (atablefor, getgrocery, hoteling, philslist, stylish, tudu) now price fresh-identity minting at one Equihash proof always, matching skooti; the gate activates on code-deploy with no box env step and can't be forgotten. tudu's register-issuing drivers (collab/isolation/link/redteam) are routed through a copied `lib/equihash_register.rb` helper (added to the Zeitwerk ignore list) so every register solves the 402 transparently; philslist/stylish non-register flows were already register-free (StubIdp tokens / `/auth/claim`), so only their config + register_flow changed. Env templates and the two register rake tasks drop the now-ignored flag.
- 2026-08-08: register is now tolled like any other verb on the three demos that previously left it free — atablefor, getgrocery and hoteling gain an opt-in `KIOSK_POW_REGISTER_DEMO=1` gate that prices fresh-identity minting at one Equihash proof, closing the toll-free-register hole (POW-VERB-GATING, K-487); the deploy env for all three sets the flag (atablefor's register inherits its `KIOSK_POW_DIFFICULTY=high` n=168 k=7, the other two stay low n=96 k=5), every flow driver's register step now solves the register 402 transparently via a shared `lib/equihash_register.rb` helper, and the getgrocery/hoteling redteam batteries run `RegistrationWithoutPow` (a missing/bad register proof must be rejected) instead of self-skipping it. The kiosk-server engine default stays `registration_pow_count=0` (per-demo enablement only; no spec/skill change).
- 2026-08-08: stylish's evergreen model is simplified from the 7-stylist roster to an evergreen SERVICE MENU with overbooking (K-446) — the `stylist_slots` roster (table + model + migration) is dropped, and the salon's always-bookable STRUCTURE is now a small EUR service menu where every service has INFINITE capacity (a visitor can book any service any number of times, so the salon never fills up and nothing goes stale). A visitor books a chosen `service_id` (`book_appointment`), the owner's `salon_calendar` forecast is summed live from the ACTUAL bookings' captured prices (starts at €0, grows as visitors book — never a fixed number), and the role model collapses to owner (staff, sees the whole book + forecast) vs customer (books, sees own, no forecast). The landing two-entrance copy, roles/isolation/redteam drivers, and home stat cards follow the new model; contrast with atablefor's finite/scarce tables.
- 2026-08-08: stylish is redesigned off synthetic dated appointments onto EVERGREEN availability (K-446) so it never goes empty and needs no reseed cron — the salon's seeded STRUCTURE is now seven stylists, each offering ONE open bookable slot (a `stylist_slots` table; zero bookings at start, real bookings accumulate as visitors book), and the staff `salon_calendar` reveal shows a FORECASTED € revenue (the day's projected earnings if the open slots fill, folding in real bookings) computed from live slot/booking prices, never a fixed number. The landing page now states two honest entrances — a VISITOR books an open slot (a new `availability` query + `book_appointment` taking a chosen `stylist_id`), STAFF view the forecast — and, since multi-account is deferred, that a tester takes one OR the other, not both at once; roles/isolation/redteam drivers and the home stat cards follow the new model.
- 2026-08-08: finished the K-482 id-name sweep on two demos so no list/query row carries an id a verb won't consume under a different name — atablefor's `availability` row DROPS its dead `id` (book_table re-resolves by date/time/party, so nothing consumed it; K-484), and hoteling's remaining pairs are aligned to the canonical param the consumer takes: `properties`→`property_id`, `availability`→`room_type_id`, `my_bookings`→`booking_id`, plus `hotel_detail`'s room rows (SQL aliases + examples + prose + the demo/isolation/redteam drivers; K-485). Names only — no values, gating, or DB columns changed.
- 2026-08-07: stylish's landing page no longer hardcodes the owner "reveal" as a fixed "7 appointments, ~€370" — since the salon calendar is seeded relative to `Date.current` (rolling), a static count/total becomes a false claim the moment the deployed seed drifts from today (K-486); the copy now describes the shape ("today's whole book with its live € revenue total") and states the exact figures come from the live calendar. The seed already dates rows to today so `demo:setup` shows a non-empty day locally; keeping the hosted deploy's today non-empty is the reseed cron (K-446/T-032, deploy-side), not this change.
- 2026-08-07: every list/search query whose rows feed a follow-up detail-or-action verb now names its id field to MATCH that verb's id param, so a cold assistant carries the same key forward instead of guessing the mapping (K-482) — hoteling's `search_hotels`/`hotel_detail` rows expose `property_id` (was bare `id`), and the sweep aligned getgrocery (`delivery_slots`→`delivery_slot_id`, `my_orders`→`order_id`), atablefor (`my_bookings`→`booking_id`), stylish (`salons`→`salon_id`, `service_menu`→`service_id`), philslist (`browse_listings`/`my_listings`→`listing_id`) and skooti (`my_reservations`→`reservation_id`); tudu was already consistent. Only wire field/param NAMES, examples, prose, and demo drivers changed — no values, gating, or DB columns; the atablefor `availability` row exposes an id no verb consumes, left for a separate decision.
- 2026-08-07: getgrocery's `delivery_slots` now generates slot wall-clock times in the OPERATOR's locale (Europe/Dublin, a real IANA zone so IST/GMT DST is automatic) and FILTERS OUT already-started windows — previously it built naive UTC times and returned every slot for the date regardless of the clock, so at ~11:00 it still offered an un-bookable `08:00–10:00` today (K-480); `create_order`/`reschedule_delivery` re-validate the chosen slot isn't in the past (clean 400) and `slot_at` carries the true `+01:00`/`+00:00` offset, with the K-470 date-reconciliation intact; the shared time logic moved to `lib/delivery_slots.rb`, `demo:shop` asserts a past slot is both hidden and rejected, and a DB-free `demo:slots_spec` unit-checks the filter across DST.
- 2026-08-07: the proof-of-work proof now travels in a `Kiosk-PoW` request header as raw JSON instead of a `pow` body field (ADR-0022, K-483, completes T-022) — header-only with no back-compat; the wire verbs and `POST /auth/register` read `HTTP_KIOSK_POW`, dual-accepting a single proof, a JSON array, repeated header lines, or a proxy comma-combined value (all flattened), which finally lets the tolled `schema` GET carry its proof (a GET has no body). The body is now purely verb args so the challenge fingerprint matches untouched; a malformed header is a clean 400, opt-in request validation re-points to the parsed proofs, the 402 keeps its `WWW-Authenticate: Kiosk-PoW` header, and all demo/e2e/redteam drivers plus the skill (v0.3.9) send the header.
- 2026-08-07: kiosk-server gains an opt-in `c.validate_requests` flag (default off; ON in all seven demos) that validates a submitted `pow` field against the vendored normative PoW JSON Schema at the wire choke point, turning a malformed proof — the K-479 case where an agent sent `pow:{solutions:[…]}` instead of `pow:{proofs:[{challenge,nonce}]}` and got a silently re-issued 402 on every retry — into a clear `400 bad_request` naming the expected shape; the real cryptographic gate is unchanged and still required, json_schemer is an optional lazily-required dependency, and an absent or well-formed pow behaves exactly as before (UNIFORM-VALIDATION slice-1, closes K-479; fuller layer is T-045).
- 2026-08-07: getgrocery's admin orders view now renders each order's currency glyph from the currency it actually SETTLED in (the controller-provided `settled_currency`, mapped usd→$/gbp→£/eur→€), falling back to € for unpaid orders, instead of a hardcoded € — an honesty fix so a non-EUR settlement would show its own glyph, not a behavior change for today's EUR-only cashier (K-434); and `before-after.md` finishes the Neo-Tokyo→Dublin relocation, aligning its `create_order` example address with the canonical `42 Camden Street, Dublin 2` the flow driver uses (K-458).
- 2026-08-06: the K-473 fix moved to the skill (systemic, not per-operator): skill.md v0.3.8's `pay` flow now tells every assistant to relay any operator-returned URL for the human — a Stripe Checkout / card-setup link, a KYC `verification_url` — COMPLETE and VERBATIM, never dropping the REQUIRED `#…` fragment (dropping it is the proven cause of Stripe's "Something went wrong"). The getgrocery-local `payment_setup` description crutch was reverted to baseline, and the seven demos + kiosk-server default `skill_url` were re-pinned to skill-v0.3.8 (URL + sha256).
- 2026-08-06: corrected the deploy README/CHECKLIST note on getgrocery's `payment_setup` "Something went wrong" (K-473) to the PROVEN root cause — a relaying agent (LLM) truncates the required ~500-char `#fid…` fragment of the valid Stripe Checkout url, so the human lands on Stripe's error page; it is NOT the Stripe account/business-name (an earlier note wrongly claimed that) nor a two-account/deploy issue. Mitigation is skill guidance (relay the url verbatim, never drop the `#`-fragment); an operator-hosted short redirect is the ready escalation if truncation recurs.
- 2026-08-05: a kiosk-pay-stripe unit test pins the setup-mode Checkout session's explicit `payment_method_types: ["card"]` so a regression that dropped it (which would then require `currency`, yielding a session whose hosted page errors) is caught in-suite rather than only on Stripe's hosted page (K-473 regression guard; retained after the fix moved to skill.md v0.3.8).
- 2026-08-05: getgrocery's `reschedule_delivery` descriptor now states plainly that it REUSES an already-paid order's existing settlement — call it directly, do NOT pay again — and that its "requires a settlement" precondition means "the order must already be paid," not "settle now" (a live agent read it as pay-again, tried a second /pay, and hit `403 order already settled`); the `input_schema`/`example` and the not-paid-yet error message were aligned, no behavior changed (K-471). Separately, `demo:isolation` gains Assertion 6 confirming a re-pay of an already-settled order returns the JSON error envelope WITH a body (a real `error.code`), never an empty/bodiless response — the investigated K-472 concern was the agent's own body parsing, /pay always carries an envelope (K-472).
- 2026-08-05: getgrocery makes the delivery address a deliberate, EARLY input (ADDRESS-UPFRONT, K-468) — `delivery_slots` now REQUIRES a delivery address and validates it names a served Dublin postal district, so an assistant must obtain a real address before it can even see slots; an out-of-zone / district-less / non-Dublin address returns a clean `400 bad_request` naming what is needed (never a 500), and `create_order` re-validates the same rule for consistency. The code + README are honest that this checks format+zone only and cannot verify a plausible in-zone address is real — the human confirming it is the ceiling (the skill's job); `demo:shop` asserts out-of-zone→400 and the valid Dublin path→200.
- 2026-08-05: getgrocery reconciles the delivery DATE between `delivery_slots` and `create_order` (K-470) — both now derive a slot's wall-clock time from one shared `(date, slot_id)` helper, `delivery_slots` returns each row's `date`, and `create_order`/`reschedule_delivery` honor that date back instead of a fixed +1-day offset, so the day an assistant is shown is exactly the day the order books (a slot chosen for today no longer books tomorrow). `demo:shop` asserts the booked `slot_at` equals the chosen slot's.
- 2026-08-05: demo human-facing prices are now shown in EUR everywhere a human/operator can see them, never raw cents or a `¢` glyph — getgrocery's `db:seed`/`demo:setup` summary lists each in-stock product as `Free-Range Eggs — €5.99 (free-range-eggs)`, and the operator-facing flow stdout across getgrocery/skooti/hoteling prints order totals and per-unit rates in EUR (`€8.47`, `€0.15/min`, `€70.00/night`) instead of `847c`/`15¢/min`; the wire and DB stay canonical integer cents (plus the additive `price_eur`/`total_eur` fields). Separately, prove.my's `/verify` page renders a REAL per-claim `<input type="checkbox">` (client-side gate that keeps the "Yes" button disabled until every fact is ticked) in place of an inert `☐` glyph a human tried to click; the broker's `decision=approve` POST path is untouched, so the two-server KYC harness stays green. (K-466, K-467)
- 2026-08-05: skooti README + the rent_motorcycle gate now state the honest SCOPE of anonymized KYC — it is an ELIGIBILITY gate (a valid licence + 18+ exist behind the assistant), NOT an accountability mechanism (transferable claim, nameless hold); real vehicle rental needs identity + contract + insurance + deposit on top; anonymized KYC's clean home is the low-liability age-gated purchase (getgrocery). (KYC-DEMO-SCOPE a)
- 2026-08-05: getgrocery gains an 18+ anonymized-KYC age-gate on the PURCHASE (KYC-DEMO-SCOPE) — one age-restricted alcohol product (a coined wine) whose `create_order` is rejected `403 kyc_required` unless the agent carries an engine-verified `age_over_18` attestation, wired to the shared `prove.my` broker exactly like skooti (getgrocery becomes a second broker operator, asking only for `age_over_18`, not a licence); this is the low-liability eligibility showcase for anonymized KYC (where the transaction closes, contrasted with high-liability rental which anonymized KYC does NOT gate). A new two-server `demo:agecheck` gate proves alcohol→403→broker approve→200→pay, non-alcohol→200 with no KYC, and a forged attestation rejected.
- 2026-08-05: a `query`/`run` called with an unknown name now returns a recovery-oriented `not_found` hint that NAMES the available query/action names for that verb (capped at 20 + "…") and points at the schema verb, so an assistant that mistyped a name (`listings` for `browse_listings`) self-corrects without a schema round-trip; the wire code/status (404) is unchanged and the names are already public via `schema`.
- 2026-08-04: re-pinned the seven operator demos + the kiosk-server default `skill_url` to skill-v0.3.7 (ADR-0012) — the immutable cut that freezes the working skill's flat-params, token-only-login/un-enveloped-auth, auth-vs-PoW-challenge, KYC-initiation, pagination-cursor, and summarize-don't-dump hardenings; URL + sha256 pins updated together.
- 2026-08-04: the `prove.my` KYC broker is deploy-ready at its registered domain `kyc.demo.kiosk.tech` (T-043/PROVE-MY-BUILD-FORKS) — the broker's issuer `iss` is now env-configurable (`KIOSK_PROVE_ISSUER`, both broker and skooti defaulting to that origin so local single-server flows stay coherent and the two-server harness pins a matching value on both sides), the deploy layout gains the broker (Caddy `kyc.demo.kiosk.tech` vhost → :3008, `kyc-demo.env.example`, systemd `prove` instance, a `kiosk_prove` DB/role, skooti's env carrying the broker's public key + intake secret), the human explainer/verify pages spell out that this is a STUB that only self-asserts whereas the real broker checks government-account possession and account-to-person correspondence, and the broker carries no agent/kiosk-operator signal (no `/.well-known/kiosk.json`, no `rel=kiosk`, no agent header) — it is a human issuer shopfront, its DB holding request-state only (no identity, no pre-ticked re-KYC).
- 2026-08-04: KYC attestation operator-binding (`aud`) is now engine-enforced (T-043/PROVE-MY-BUILD-FORKS/ADR-0020): `KycVerifier` rejects at the `POST /kiosk/agents/kyc` wire any attestation whose `aud` does not equal this operator's new `kyc_audience` config (defaulting to its origin), so a claim the KYC provider minted for one operator can no longer be replayed to another — the `prove.my` broker mints `aud` from the operator's declared audience and skooti declares its stable broker handle, keeping its demo-layer check as defense-in-depth.
- 2026-08-04: the `demo-production-smoke` CI job now also boots the new `prove.my` broker (`kiosk-demo-prove`) in `RAILS_ENV=production` behind the simulated TLS proxy and curl-asserts its `/verify` human HTML surface — home 200 (eager-load of the broker's four `lib/` modules), the live yes/no form (200), a clean not-recognised page for a missing/unknown token (200, not 500), a browser-Origin form POST that must clear the assume_ssl/CSRF-Origin check (200, not 422), and the ProveKey PEM — closing the prod-only-bug coverage gap for the broker's distinct token-capability surface that the stylish-only smoke never exercised. (K-462)
- 2026-08-04: new `prove.my` anonymizing KYC broker demo (`kiosk-demo-prove`) generalizes skooti's per-operator KYC stub into a shared standalone issuer, and skooti is rewired to trust it: `request_kyc` now calls the broker's intake and relays a broker verification_url, and a new `POST /kyc/callback` receives the broker's signed anonymized claim (verified against the trusted ProveKey, with nonce + demo-layer operator-match + subject checks) — the engine attestation/wire stays unchanged, the agent-facing `request_kyc`/`kyc_status`/`/agents/kyc`/`rent_motorcycle` surface is stable, and skooti's self-hosted stub issuer retires; `demo:kyc` and `demo:redteam` become two-server gates (they boot the broker) and gain `CrossOperatorClaimReplay` + `ForgedCallbackNoSig` beats alongside the migrated `IssuedKycJwsTheft`.
- 2026-08-04: every demo home page now leads with a prominent agent-facing header strip ("Agents & AI assistants — learn here how to work with this site.", linking the operator's own `/.well-known/kiosk.json`) instead of a buried machine-readable footnote, so a local assistant that can't read the machine signals still gets an explicit human-legible invitation to transact over Kiosk.
- 2026-08-04: the other six demos align to the descriptor house style (T-042/K-452/ADR-0021) — each demo's PRIMARY read query and PRIMARY action (getgrocery catalog/create_order, skooti scooters_available/reserve, atablefor availability/book_table, stylish service_menu/book_appointment, philslist browse_listings/post_listing, tudu my_lists/create_list) now advertise an input JSON-Schema plus example params/row, and each `demo:schema` asserts those extensions are present, so every demo consistently demonstrates the machine-readable descriptor a cold assistant reads; additive and absent-by-default, so wire behavior and existing consumers are unchanged.
- 2026-08-04: hoteling becomes the data-plane API-shape exemplar (T-042/K-452/ADR-0021) — it now seeds ~100 coined Istanbul hotels and ships a paginated multi-parameter `search_hotels` (neighbourhood/price/stars/amenity filters + `limit`/`cursor`, returning summary rows with a `next` cursor while more match) plus a `hotel_detail` fetch-by-id, both carrying an input JSON-Schema + example params/row, so an assistant filters and pages instead of drowning in an unpaginated list; the booking, isolation, and redteam flows are unchanged, and a new `demo:search` proves a full page carries `next`, the echoed cursor returns a disjoint next page, and a complete filtered result omits `next`.
- 2026-08-04: kiosk-server data-plane API shape (T-042/K-452/ADR-0021) — a list `query` MAY now paginate via an opaque envelope `next` cursor (present = truncated, absent = complete; opt-in per handler via a new `Page`/`Cursor` seam that reads `limit`/`cursor`), and a `schema` descriptor MAY carry an optional input JSON-Schema plus example params/row, so a cold assistant learns shape without probing — all additive and absent on existing shapes, so consumers and e2e are unchanged.
- 2026-08-04: skooti ships a demo stub KYC-issuer so an EXTERNAL assistant holding only its own keypair can COMPLETE motorcycle KYC (K-440/K-443): a new `request_kyc` action returns a no-sign-in human-approve link (an unguessable request token), a KYC provider stub signs the anonymized {age_over_18, licence_a} booleans on approval, and the agent polls a `kyc_status` query then submits the issued attestation to the existing /agents/kyc — replacing the old dead-end where the only issuer was the demo driver's private key and the 403 hint now points agents at this path.
- 2026-08-04: skooti — licence-free scooters no longer require any KYC (K-442): start_rental drops its binary-KYC gate so a fresh agent rents a scooter on ownership + payment alone; only the combustion motorcycle stays KYC-gated (age_over_18 + licence_a). The redteam's MissingKyc beat is retired (the motorcycle's missing-KYC block stays covered by MotorcycleForgedKyc).
- 2026-08-04: getgrocery advertises a human-readable EUR price/total alongside the canonical integer cents, so an assistant renders euros instead of raw cents or a guessed glyph — closing a confusion seen in the live agent runs. (K-451)
- 2026-08-03: Spread the demos across cities rather than all-Turkey — hoteling is now Istanbul-only (the Cappadocia/Ankara/Izmir properties relocated to coined Istanbul hotels, so an "Istanbul" search is coherent), atablefor → Lisbon (Tasca do Tejo, Alfama), skooti → Amsterdam (Jordaan/Prinsengracht docks, Amstel Cruiser motorcycle), and getgrocery's sample delivery address → Dublin; all mechanics, ids, flows, and EUR prices unchanged. (T-041)
- 2026-08-03: tudu seeds a shared "Flat 3B" household (alice-owned, bob a housemate member) and adds a public read-only housemate view (home + `/shared`) so an assistant's created-and-shared list visibly lands in the collaborator's account — making the collaboration reveal observable without a second identity store. (T-041)
- 2026-08-03: philslist prices switched to EUR with named seeded listings, and a public read-only classifieds board (home + `/listings`) now shows open listings so a wire-posted ad visibly appears — making the household (two assistants, one account) and owner-scoped isolation concrete; `pay` stays absent. (T-041)
- 2026-08-03: stylish seeds a EUR service menu and a full day of priced appointments so the roles-from-IdP reveal is tangible — an owner-linked assistant now sees the whole book plus a real € revenue total (7 appointments, ~€370), each stylist only their own priced chairs, a customer nothing. (T-041)
- 2026-08-03: getgrocery's admin order view renders totals and line prices in EUR (€) unconditionally, matching the EUR catalog and cashier — dropping the stale USD/`$` display branch that the currency cashier check already makes unreachable. (T-041)
- 2026-08-03: skooti seeds a named, EUR-priced Istanbul fleet — electric scooters at Kadıköy Dock and Beşiktaş Pier (licence-free, €0.15/min) and the KYC-gated "Bosphorus Cruiser" motorcycle (€0.40/min) — and `scooters_available` now surfaces each vehicle's name and pickup dock, so a plain rental prompt has a concrete target; the KYC gate, offline unlock token, and EUR cashier check are unchanged. (T-041)
- 2026-08-03: atablefor redesigned as the flagship "viewable board" reference — its headline task runs entirely over the Kiosk protocol with no web sign-in (the home page now reads as a protocol endpoint an assistant drives, not a human booking form, fixing the framing that pushed a live agent into web-login mode), and an assistant's linked booking surfaces on a public read-only reservations board under the diner's name (seeded restaurant "Meydan Meze House", named tables + seatings, EUR, no payment). Adds a Devise sign-out and a prominent "Staff login" header. (T-041)
- 2026-08-03: Added a `demo-production-smoke` CI job that boots one demo (stylish) in `RAILS_ENV=production` behind a simulated TLS proxy + browser and curl-asserts four surfaces — home 200 (eager-load), well-known 200, unauth manage-page 302-to-sign-in, and a real Devise sign-in reaching a signed-in page — guarding the prod-only bug class (boot crashes, missing-migration 500s, assume_ssl/CSRF-Origin sign-in failures) that dev-mode CI and the demo rake gates never exercise. (K-422/K-436/K-439-class)
- 2026-08-03: All seven hosted demos now set `config.assume_ssl = true` in production so Rails trusts the Caddy TLS terminator's forwarded scheme — the human Devise sign-in was silently failing behind the proxy because Rails computed `request.base_url` as `http://` while the browser sent `Origin: https://…`, so the Rails 8 CSRF Origin check rejected the POST (422) and no session was ever established; with the proxy scheme assumed, `request.base_url`/secure cookies are correct and the sign-in round-trips (verified before/after in production mode on stylish + atablefor). (hosted Devise sign-in silent-fail)
- 2026-08-03: stylish roles-from-IdP now works through the REAL Devise web sign-in, not just the test stub — a salon owner/stylist who signs in at `/users/sign_in` mints link codes carrying their own role (owner/stylist), so their assistant inherits it and `salon_calendar` scopes correctly; previously every real sign-in fell back to the first configured role (`customer`) and saw an empty calendar. Fix is a one-line `User#kiosk_role` mapping `staff_role`; `demo:roles` now drives the real Devise path (owner/stylist/customer) end-to-end, which the stub-only coverage skipped. Surfaced by a live hosted Hermes run. (K-437)
- 2026-07-30: An unauthenticated browser visitor to the engine's «Manage assistants» page now lands on the operator's sign-in (flash prompt + return-to) instead of a bare 401, via a new IdP-neutral opt-in `config.sign_in_path` wired into the four linking demos; a non-HTML/API request (or no configured path) still gets the plain 401, keeping the wire contract and AR-less hosts unchanged. (MANAGE-PAGE-UNAUTH-UX, K-436-adjacent)
- 2026-07-29: Demo-page copy from a live review — stylish's role blurb no longer promises a "stylist" view a browser visitor can't reach (the web login is owner-only; generalized to "whatever staff role your IdP gives you"), philslist dropped a "pay absent" chip that duplicated its "Non-commerce (no pay verb)" one, and the getgrocery/hoteling notes now link the sibling demos they name.
- 2026-07-29: atablefor — added the per-assistant governance columns (`spending_cap_cents`, `human_label`) to `kiosk.agents` so the «Manage assistants» page renders instead of returning HTTP 500 in production; the page was wired in (2026-07-23) but its schema migration was never applied to atablefor, and `db:schema:load` loads `structure.sql` directly. Surfaced by a live hosted run. (K-436)
- 2026-07-29: atablefor dropped the inert AP2 mandate/settlement tables — the no-pay table-booking demo advertises no `pay` verb, so the payment-mandate schema (intent/cart/payment_mandates/settlements) was dead scaffolding carried over from the foodelivery reframe; `structure.sql` regenerated without it, matching the other no-pay demos that never shipped it. (K-420, T-033)
- 2026-07-29: Extended the payment-capture cashier check to the two remaining pay demos — hoteling (`ValidatingBookingProvider`) and skooti (`ValidatingRentalProvider`) — so each operator verifies the agent-signed cart's currency, single-resource reference, and total against its own quote (hoteling's nightly quote; skooti's per-minute rate) before charging, with wrong-currency / tampered-price / inflated-total redteam beats on each. The check is monetary-only — ownership stays a use-time gate — so the B-pays-for-A isolation proofs and skooti's KYC gate are untouched. (T-039, K-435)
- 2026-07-29: Fixed the `deploy/` provisioning so a first deploy actually runs and its payment claims are honest (K-423, K-424): `postgres-init.sql`/`telemetry-init.sql` created roles inside a `DO`/dollar-quoted block where psql can't substitute the `:password` variable (a real run errored "syntax error at :"), now rewritten as idempotent `\gexec` role creation that quote-escapes each raw password via `:'var'`; and the runbook/checklist/env template no longer claim atablefor takes Stripe payments (it configures no payment provider — `pay` is absent from its capabilities), leaving getgrocery the only Stripe demo.
- 2026-07-29: Public ROADMAP.md added at the repo root, consolidating the directions already promised on kiosk.tech (login adapters, turnkey KYC issuance, MPP adapter, durable revocation, key rotation, operator-push events, ports) plus the new client-preferred-currency direction (the assistant declares its currency and the kiosk serves prices in it — an operator cannot dictate the customer's currency; deliberately post-release).
- 2026-07-29: getgrocery order redesign — delivery is part of the order (create_order REQUIRES delivery_slot_id + delivery_address; a paid-but-undelivered order can no longer exist) and schedule_delivery becomes reschedule_delivery (move a PAID order's slot once); plus the cashier check: a ValidatingPaymentProvider decorator rejects, before any capture, carts in the wrong currency (operator prices in EUR, now advertised per catalog row), carts not mirroring the order's items at catalog prices, and totals that don't sum — with three new redteam beats (wrong-currency / tampered-price / inflated-total, 12 BLOCKED total). Driven by the first live external-assistant run, which paid in usd unchallenged and left a paid order with no delivery.
- 2026-07-28: getgrocery's settlement→order convention is now discoverable where an external assistant actually looks — `create_order`'s schema description + a per-order `pay_hint` in its result, `schedule_delivery`'s description, and an actionable Gate-2 403 message all state that the cart mandate's `line_items` must include `{"order_id": …}`; surfaced by the first live Hermes run, which paid successfully but whose order could never show paid or be scheduled (the convention existed only inside the gate SQL and the local flow driver).
- 2026-07-28: getgrocery's public `/admin/orders` back-office is now deliberately public — visitor-typed delivery addresses are masked server-side to a short head + tail, the page carries a "public by design; data resets" plaque, the home page links to it as the operator's view, and money renders in the settled currency (an external assistant may legitimately settle in a different currency than the local driver's eur).
- 2026-07-28: All seven demos re-pinned to skill-v0.3.6 (kiosk-server's default skill_url bumped alongside): the skill now pins its reference PoW solver by commit + content sha256 and teaches venv+numpy setup and operator-referenced `line_items` ids.
- 2026-07-23: getgrocery demo spelled consistently singular — the operator brand shown in the home/admin pages, the discovery-doc owner name/support email (`GetGrocery`/`help@getgrocery.com`, which also drives the live-activity telemetry label), and the before-after worked example now all match the gem/subdomain/runtime origin (`getgrocery.app`), and two dead `getgroceries.com` dev-host allowlist lines were dropped; the plural had been a stale leftover.
- 2026-07-23: atablefor now wires the «Manage assistants» HTML page (the engine's link-code mint UI, reused wholesale) so the human half of assistant-linking is actually walkable in the browser — matching stylish/tudu/philslist; all four linking demos' pages now link straight to it and spell out the mint→hand-to-assistant→redeem flow (the human mints, the assistant redeems over the wire — there is no code field for the human by design).
- 2026-07-23: All seven demo root pages restructured into four explicit sections — "Try it with your own assistant" (the one-line prompt, the Stripe test card on getgrocery, the production-PoW note on atablefor), "About this demo" (its one distinguishing capability, feature chips, and a link to that demo's own source on GitHub + kiosk.tech), a compact "Live activity", and the storefront visually separated from the Kiosk-facing sections; the assistant-discovery hint is now prose ("learn here how to") rather than a raw `/.well-known/kiosk.json` path. atablefor now surfaces its Devise sign-in and the seeded diner login so the account-linking beat is walkable by a visitor; tudu gained self-service sign-up (Devise `:registerable`, no migration) and a consistent header bar to match the other demos.
- 2026-07-22: skooti now boots under production eager-load — two `lib/` demo/KAT helpers (`equihash_register.rb`, top-level flow methods with no constant; `rental_token_issuer_kat.rb`, which defines the acronym `RentalTokenIssuerKAT` that Zeitwerk's inflector expects as `RentalTokenIssuerKat`) are excluded from `autoload_lib`; both are required explicitly by flow scripts, never at runtime, so eager-load otherwise raised `Zeitwerk::NameError`. Only surfaced on a real production deploy since dev/CI use lazy autoload.
- 2026-07-21: New reusable count-based PoW backoff strategy in `kiosk-reputation` (`Policies::Backoff` + in-process `BackoffStore`): one solved proof grants an identity N ungated follow-up requests, then it is re-challenged — a COUNT, not a time window (a window would let a bot flood inside it). Wired a duck-typed `on_proof_verified` hook into the `kiosk-server` PoW gate (hookless policies unaffected) and a demonstration `demo:backoff` flow in `atablefor` asserting the solve→N-free→re-challenge sequence end-to-end; operator-local config, transparent to the assistant (spec/skill unchanged).
- 2026-07-21: The flagship `atablefor` now features a real human diner account (Devise sign-in) and an account-linking beat — a diner mints a link code, their AI assistant redeems it (kiosk-pop possession proof), and the assistant's table bookings are thereafter tied to the diner's account; wired the real `kiosk-user-idp-devise` user-IdP in place of the stub and added a `demo:binding` flow (in CI) asserting the booking's `user_id` is the diner's.
- 2026-07-21: Every one of the seven demos now honors the `KIOSK_POW_DIFFICULTY` knob (tudu/philslist/stylish gained it; all default `low` so CI/e2e stay fast), and `atablefor` is designated the single production-grade showcase pinned `high` with a visible "runs production-grade proof-of-work on purpose" note on its root page; skooti dropped from `high` to knob-adjustable `low`, and the deploy runbook/checklist were corrected to say only atablefor ships high.
- 2026-07-21: IdP-seam honesty fixes across the demos — the four stub USER-IdPs (atablefor/getgrocery/hoteling/skooti) now subclass the correct `Kiosk::UserIdentityProviders::Base` (were wrongly on the agent-IdP base), and stylish's "swap the stub" walkthrough/README guidance no longer conflates the agent-IdP seam (`c.agent_idp`) with the user-IdP seam (`c.user_idp`); no runtime behavior changed.
- 2026-07-21: Corrected the `deploy/` hosting runbook so a first deploy actually boots — it named "Postgres 16" though the shipped `structure.sql` is PG17-format (PG16 rejects it at load), quoted a stale "~30 s" PoW solve time (measured ~9–10 s / ~1.3 GiB), and its env templates claimed the difficulty knob was "not yet wired" when it is; the runbook is now self-contained (internal design-doc citations removed).
